Despite the Odds

by longlivefatalefemme

Summary

Previously titled: “Can Leon and Ada be together?” Completed title: Despite the Odds

Being together has always been the easy part. Figuring out how to stay together is another story.

Leon had spent most of his adult life alone, moving from one assignment to the next, never really having the chance to build anything that lasted. And now, there was a woman whose absence could make the entire world feel wrong.

Ada had never intended to become that person for anyone. Yet here she was finding herself being drawn back to him in more ways than one. Somehow despite the odds, they had built something neither of them had ever believed they could keep. — Come with me to string the events of Resident Evil 6, Vendetta and Death island to answer the age old question: Can Leon and Ada be together? All while I fill in the gaps for Capcom

🤭 while trying to be as canonically accurate as possible (NOTE: CONTAINS SPOILERS FROM THE RESIDENT EVIL FRANCHISE.)

Chapter 1: "I was just resting my eyes"

Chapter Notes

this work begins in re 6! where leon sees ada injured because of simmons and jumps in to protect her, this is a rewrite of the scene in re 6 but less action packed so we can focus on leon's feelings during this moment. (i did do a little bit of remixing when it comes to the events of RE6 to fit the story better)

“She’s working for neo umbrella do you know what that means?”

“I do,”

“And you’re still going to protect this woman?”

“I am.”

Leon didn’t know what he meant when those words left his mouth. He wasn’t thinking straight because his words meant two things: one he would protect Ada against his better judgment and two he would trust Chris to handle the situation. He trusted Chris, but at the cost of…. What?

So when the radio crackled, Helena halted a few steps behind him as he reached his ear piece. “Leon!? Leon, are you all right?” Chris’s voice cracked through.

“Until now, yeah… But Chris, listen to me. I need you to rescue two hostages from an underwater oil field. Agent Sherry Birkin… and Jake Muller. He’s Albert Wesker’s son. He's got antibodies for the C-Virus.” He told Chris, bracing himself for the mention of ‘Wesker’ but nothing.

It was odd. Especially when Chris said “I got it. On my way.” guess he knew. Made Leon's job easier.

“Thanks.” Leon said anyway not giving himself the privilege to analyze vocal interpretations over an already poor radio.

Before he could shut it, Chris spoke up: “Leon, wait. There’s something I need to tell you.” His stomach dropped, did they catch her? What did they find that— “Ada Wong is dead.” chris’s words were followed by silence. The ground beneath him didn’t feel real, The fires across Lanshiang blurred into nothing. Screams of infected people growling as they ripped each other’s flesh, the gunfire—gone. For a second, the world narrowed down to just two words.

Ada. Dead.

“Leon?” Chris asked.

Leon’s hand opened, then closed again, slow and mechanical. Like he forgot how it worked, as if breathing was now voluntary. “Who did it?”

“Unidentified sniper— most likely from The Family, it was only one bullet.”

Leon stared out blankly over the skyline collapsing under smoke. “Copy” The word came out flat through his clenched jaw. In the spur of the moment he ripped the earpiece out and threw it off the building. As if the words Chris said can be unheard or rather, undone. It didn’t.

“Are you okay?” Helena asked quietly. He hadn’t looking up from the ground yet, but when he looked at the skyline of Lanshiang he was reminded once again that the choice was never his. He moved before she could respond. “Let’s just find the survivors and get the hell outta here.” There was no point in looking back.

- (significant time in game passes)

The elevator groaned its way up the shaft, metal cables straining overhead. Loud, but not loud enough to occupy the silence that followed the news of Ada’s death. Leon could feel Helena’s helpless gaze on him as he looked out the glass panes. The city still glittered, but everything seemed dulled now. “She was more than just a friend, wasn’t she?” she asked, and a moment of silence later, Helena answered herself: “You had feelings for her.”

“Helena we don’t have time for this—“ the elevator stopped abruptly making the entire frame jolt. Glass shattered outward, fragments spraying across the floor. Leon staggered, catching himself on the rail. “Guess this thing isn’t gonna hold up much longer…” he inspected the floor it was halfway up they couldn’t pry the door open if they tried.

Helena called from the edge. “Leon! It’s…”

When he looked over his shoulder, he saw Simmons crawling over the edge of the building below them, yelling something incoherent.

“Simmons,” Leon said grimly. “Fucking bastard.” his eyes catching a flash of red on the building. “Ada?”

“She’s alive!” Helena said as the elevator jerked beneath them. Leon didn’t take his eyes off Ada’s silhouette as she emptied her machine gun. What on earth?

“I need to get to her, Helena cover me!” He jumped off the edge of the elevator, using the rope to swing onto the building. “She really shouldn’t have ditched the chopper.” he muttered to himself as he landed.

“Leon?” Ada barely had time to turn when Simmons’ ruptured tail lashed out, slamming her into the ground.

“ADA!” Leon ran toward her crumpled form, blood staining her shirt a darker red. He fell to his knees holding her closer to him, his arm around her shoulder “Ada… Can you hear me!?”

Simmons growled from in-front of them, unaffected to Helena’s shots. “Get away from her, Leon! You’re not half the man she needs”

“That’s rich coming from you.” Leon said, watching as Simmons' flesh rapidly grew bigger.

Leon fired at him simultaneously has Simmons growled more half coherent bullshit: “What do you know about us, Leon? Come with me, Ada my love. We’ll be together, forever!”

He shot at Simmons in controlled bursts. Useless. Simmons’ body kept shifting, swelling, regenerating. “Not if I can help it.” Leon said. Simmons retreated to the back of the roof, going down a side of the building, Leon couldn’t care, not when Ada’s blood was warm against his gloves. His hands weren’t steady anymore. “Come on Ada, wake up!” Desperation bled into his shaky hands as he cradled her head close to his chest. “I know you can pull through this. We both can.”

“Leon…” she murmured weakly her eyes fluttering to his. “You need to leave…”

“You are crazy if you think I’m going to leave you.”

“Listen to me…before he mutates further, get out of here..” her hand weakly held his harness. Something slipped into his shirt pocket. Keys.

“No, I’m not listening to you!“ he says roughly. “I’m not leaving, not without you. I don’t want to hear any of it.”

“Leon..” she said weakly, He was already tearing open his kit, pressing gauze hard against her side, forcing the healing agent into place. Her body tensed under his hands. “This isn’t worth it,”

“Shut up, I’ve got you. I won’t let it end like this” Somewhere behind them, metal screamed as Simmons forced his way back through, his mass screeching against the building like it might tear the whole thing down with him. “You hurt anywhere else? Chris told me you got shot,”

She shook her head, “it’s a long story, that.. wasn’t me.” her grip tightening weakly against Leon’s vest as he looked at the situation around her. “But Leon… you need to listen to me…”

“No.” He pressed harder on the wound, jaw set. “I’ve had enough of you making a call for the both of us. You don’t get to do this again.”

Her brows pulled together faintly. “Do what?”

“Disappear.” The word came out rough. Another distant roar marked that Simmons was getting closer now. Leon barely reacted. “I’m not losing you like that, so don’t do this to me.”

Ada let out a shallow breath, something almost like a quiet laugh—but it hurt too much to follow through. “You don’t… lose me, Leon,” she murmured. “I’m not yours to lose.” That would’ve shut him up before.

Not now, when words were being exchanged like they were the last, not when Ada felt like a memory he was holding onto. “Yeah?” His voice dropped, tight but steady. “Then why does it feel like it every time?” A piece of debris crashed nearby making dust scatter across them. Leon shifted instinctively, shielding her. “I keep telling myself I know how this ends,” he continued, eyes locked on her now. “You show up, you help, you vanish. That’s just how it goes.”

Ada watched him—really watched him this time. “But I’m done pretending that’s enough,” Leon said. His hand tightened slightly over hers. “Because it’s not.” He said firmly. “You think I wouldn’t find out it was you who helped me in Spain? You made sure Mendez followed you so he’d stay away from me. You think I wouldn’t notice? You’re not exactly subtle Ada.” She looked like she was about to protest something but chose not to. “Why do you risk everything like this?”

“Someone has to,”

“Not you. I don’t want your damn life on the line because you’re trying to make mine easier.” He huffed tying the bandage securely around her.

The building groaned again louder. Time was running out. Ada’s breathing hitched, her strength slipping, but her gaze didn’t waver. “Leon…”

“If you walk away after this—fine,” he cut in. “If you disappear, if you lie to me again—fine. Leon didn’t need to look back in order to understand Simmons was back on his feet. “But don’t tell me to leave you behind like it doesn’t matter.” His voice lowered. “It matters to me, understand?”

Ada’s expression shifted subtly. “I think you’ve made yourself clear.” Something unguarded breaking through the usual control. For once, she didn’t have a quick answer. Instead, her hand tightened faintly in his as she studied his face. “…You know…. You were right, it’s not easy to get rid of you.” She said. “Back then I thought you were naive about it, but it turned out you’re just stubbornly bad at following orders,” she whispered.

Leon almost let out a breath that could’ve been a laugh. “Yeah. Guess I picked that up somewhere.” A heavy crash cut through the moment. Simmons, sounding closer than before. Leon finally looked over his shoulder, calculating distance, timing, options. None of them good. He looked back at her, Ada’s mouth twitched faintly as her eyes drifted shut again. Leon’s expression fell immediately. “No.” He leaned closer, thumb brushing against her cheek “Ada, look at me. Don’t close your eyes,” The helplessness of the situation was incomparable to the time in Raccoon City when his grasp wasn’t strong enough to not let her go. The same pit in his stomach sat differently this time. He pat her face almost desperately now. “Ada—”

A wet, distorted roar thundered across the rooftop. Leon looked up just as Simmons’ mutated form split open. “LEON TAKE COVER!” Helena's yell echoed from the other roof.

Leon knew exactly what was coming. Yet, he didn’t even try to move. Instead he pulled Ada tightly against his chest and wrapped himself around her, turning his body over hers protectively. Simmons’ mutated form convulsed violently, launching a barrage of jagged spikes straight at them. The splinters didn’t exactly miss him. Pain was sharp enough to make Leon choke on the impact. But he didn’t let go. His arm locked tighter around Ada automatically as shattered concrete and metal sprayed around them, spikes embedding deep into him.

The barrage finally stopped, neither of them moved. Leon’s breathing had turned ragged now, shallow against the pain burning through his back. He could feel stings beneath his shirt, barely registering the pain because he had decided none of it mattered. Slowly, he lifted himself just enough to look at her. “Ada…?” Nothing.

Her head rested against his arm, eyes closed, completely still. Blood stained her shirt, dark beneath the city lights, her head resting weakly against his arm. Leon felt his chest tighten hard enough to overpower the pain on his back. Fear hit him harder than the spikes did. “No…” His voice cracked quietly. “No, no, come on…” One shaking hand moved to her face. “Don’t do this to me,” he whispered, almost lost beneath the wind. His shoulders sagging as he held her closer, his forehead pressing into her collar. “Ada,” he squeezed her to chase her warmth, his eyes burning.

“Would you look at that Mr Kennedy? It seems that every time you hold my precious Ada, you. let. her. DIE!”

Leon ignored Simmons with a new found determination, he shook his head as he pulled back a bit. “Hey.” Holding her face carefully. “Open your eyes.” His breathing starting to fall apart a little. “Ada, please.” His voice cracking as he spoke. “Please don’t do this again.” he held her tighter, “stay with me, I mean it,” He leans closer, his hand firmly holding her head. “I can’t lose you again.” he said, quietly. “I don’t think I survive that this time.” he held her. “Please,” he shuddered into her shoulder, his throat hurting.

Leon froze when he felt A trembling pressure against his side, For half a second he thought it was another spasm from the pain tearing through his back—until he felt it again. Her hand clutching weakly at the side of his shirt. His breath caught. “Ada…?” Leon stared at her, disbelief washing across his face so suddenly it almost hurt. His vision blurred again and he blinked hard, swallowing it back immediately. “Ada,” he breathed, quieter this time. He held the side of her face gently as her brows pulled together faintly signaling her consciousness slowly returning. Leon could see it happening little by little—the tension in her face, the shallow inhale, her fingers tightening against him.

Relief hit him so hard his shoulders nearly gave out. He let out an uneven breath that sounded dangerously close to a laugh as his forehead dropped briefly against hers. “Are you kidding me?” He watched as Ada’s eyes cracked open slightly.

“…I was just resting my eyes.”

Leon shut his eyes for a second, a shaky exhale escaping him before he looked back at her with a disbelieving smile tugging at his face. “Shouldn’t sleep on the job.” Then, carefully, she pushed herself upright despite the pain in her side. Leon immediately moved to steady her. “Easy.”

A faint smirk ghosted across Ada’s lips. “I’m not dead yet.”

“Could’ve fooled me.” That earned the faintest smirk from her.

“You should know better by now,” she straightened to stand with his help, Ada’s hand had shifted to the back of his shoulder now—and the second her fingers brushed against the torn fabric there, Leon stiffened.

Ada’s gaze lingered on him for another second longer than usual before she spoke again. “When this is over…I’ll patch you up.”

Leon looked at her, raising his brows in surprise “Wow,” he said dryly, “you really must’ve hit your head.”

Ada rolled her eyes faintly, but he caught the small smile that followed. “Don’t push it,”

Behind them, Simmons snarled in frustration. “Enough of this sentimental nonsense!” The rooftop shook as his mutated frame slammed against the concrete, bone jutting violently from expanding flesh.

Leon ignored him for one more second. “Is there some back-story I should know here?”

Ada scoffed lightly. “Nothing worthwhile.” Reloading with one hand. “In case you haven’t noticed, Simmons doesn’t take rejection well.”

Chapter 2: "You’re not exactly easy to leave behind."

The city looked different from the air. Lanshiang burned beneath the helicopter in fractured streaks of orange and smoke, emergency sirens swallowed beneath the rotors overhead. Leon sat against the side of the cabin, head tipped back slightly as Helena piloted them away from the collapsing tower. Now that the adrenaline was fading, every injury hurt twice as much. The bio-spikes had torn through his jacket and grazed deep enough to leave blood soaking through the fabric beneath his shoulder.

Helena glanced back briefly. “You sure you’re okay back there?”

“I’ve had worse.”

“Yeah, and I’m guessing those times sucked too.”

Leon huffed quietly through the pain, when movement outside caught his attention. A distant explosion erupted near the Quad Tower skyline. Leon straightened immediately despite the protest from his entire body. “…Helena.”

She already knew from his tone alone. “No.”

“She’s there, Drop me on the roof.”

Helena stared at him like he’d finally lost it. “You can barely sit upright.”

Leon looked back out toward the burning tower. His jaw tightened. “I know but I don’t care.” That look he got whenever Ada was involved. Equal parts determination and absolute self- destruction.

Helena sighed heavily, guiding the helicopter toward the tower. “She just gave us a ticket out of here and now you want to ditch it?”

“I don’t like playing by her rules.”

“Or, you just make bad decisions when it comes to her.”

“Been hearing that a lot tonight.” As they neared the rooftop, Helena reached into her pocket and pulled out the small compact mirror Ada had hidden the microchip inside. The chip itself was secured away for Hunnigan. She held the compact out toward him.

Leon frowned slightly. “Huh?” Leon stared at her for a second before taking it, his thumb tracing the butterfly engraving on the casing.

Helena smirked faintly. “you should return it,” Helena gave him a look. “Unless you plan on keeping it,”

Leon shook his head with a tired smile despite himself as the helicopter settled close enough for him to jump. The second he stood, pain ripped through his back hard enough to make him pause. He moved toward the edge of the cabin anyway.

Helena watched him carefully for a second before speaking again, quieter this time. “…Try not to die before you see her.”

Leon glanced back toward her. Then toward the burning tower ahead. “That would be anticlimactic.” And with that, he jumped onto the rooftop. The helicopter lifted the second Leon’s boots hit the rooftop. Wind blasted around him as Helena pulled away into the smoke- filled skyline, the rotors disappearing into the burning dark above Lanshiang. Leon watched it go for a second before turning toward the Quad Tower. The upper floors were still burning. Glass rained intermittently from shattered windows while black smoke curled into the night sky. Parts of the structure looked ready to collapse completely. He knew she was still in there. Leon exhaled slowly, ignoring the pain pulling through his back as he started toward the edge of the roof. His hand brushed against something in his pocket. The compact mirror. He stared at it for a second before pulling out his phone instead; he re-read the message that Ada sent him earlier about the chopper.

From Ada: To Leon

Wish I could stay longer, but I’ve gotta run. I’ve left you a little parting gift on the roof. See you down the road, Leon.

Leon shook his head faintly to himself. His thumb hovered over the screen for a moment before typing back. Leon: you done wrapping things up? I’m by the quad tower. For a second, nothing happened. Just distant sirens. Fire. Wind tearing between the high-rises. Then movement caught his eye: Across from him, several rooftops over, a figure stepped out through the smoke near the edge of the Quad Tower. Ada. She paused the second she saw him. The city stretched between them chaos far below, but for a moment it felt strangely quiet. Ada stared at him from the opposite rooftop, the wind pulling at her dark hair and the red collar of her shirt. Even from this distance he could see the exhaustion in the way she stood.

A notification buzzed in his hand. Leon looked down. Ada: Thought you’d be smarter than coming back. A breath escaped him—half tired laugh, half disbelief. He glanced back up at her. Ada’s expression hadn’t changed much, but there was something softer in it now.

Leon typed back without looking away from her. Leon: Yeah? You’re not exactly easy to leave behind.

Across the rooftops, Ada’s phone lit briefly in her hand. And for the first time all night—she smiled. A second later, Ada clipped something onto the edge of the ruined rooftop beside her. Leon immediately recognized the grapple gun in her hand. The cable shot across the gap and locked onto the building above him with a sharp sound. Ada stepped clean off the edge of the Quad Tower. The wind caught her instantly as she swung across the burning skyline. Even injured, she made it look effortless. Leon moved forward automatically as she landed on his rooftop, boots skidding slightly against the concrete before she straightened. For a second neither of them spoke. Smoke curled between them. Helicopter lights swept across distant buildings. Then Ada holstered her weapon. “Since you’re here,” she said casually, despite the blood still staining her side, “I suppose I should keep my word.” Her heels clicking as she walked towards him.

Leon frowned faintly. “What word?”

Ada rolled her eyes. “Oh Leon,” she said with a faint smirk “you like to play dumb, don’t you?” She stepped closer, reaching for the torn fabric near his shoulder. “Let’s get you patched up.” Leon blinked at her for half a second. she meant it?

Ada noticed the look immediately. “What?” she asked dryly. Despite herself, the corner of her mouth twitched slightly. The rooftop shuddered faintly beneath them as another explosion echoed somewhere lower in the city. Ada glanced around briefly before motioning toward a partially collapsed maintenance structure near the edge of the roof.

“You’re serious?” He looked at the spot.

Ada motioned toward the concrete wall near the maintenance structure again. “Sit.” Leon lowered himself down with a tired exhale, carefully against the cold surface. The second his shoulder touched concrete, pain tore through his back hard enough to make his jaw tighten. “…Take it off.” She said Leon looked at her. “The shirt,” she said again. “Before I change my mind.”

“You always this bossy after almost dying?” He smirked at her, taking off his vest and unbuckling his harness. Despite himself, Leon let out a quiet breath that almost passed for a laugh. He undid his buttons carefully, wincing as he peeled the blood-soaked fabric away from his shoulder and back. The wound felt worse exposed. Jagged punctures and torn skin ran across his shoulder blade and side where Simmons’ spikes had hit him. Blood still tracked slowly down his back beneath the rooftop lights.

Ada’s expression shifted the second she saw it properly. “You’re lucky those didn’t go deeper.”

“Yeah. I’m lucky alright.” He said looking at her.

“Hm.” She pulled the first aid spray from the med kit. “Hold still.” She said shaking it.

The cold mist hit the wounds instantly. Leon inhaled sharply through his teeth, muscles tensing hard beneath the spray. “Jesus—a warning would have been nice!”

Ada didn’t even look up. “Complaining already?” The spray hissed steadily against the wounds while the city burned around them. Helicopter lights swept occasionally through the smoke overhead, but up here, for the first time all night, things were quiet. Or as quiet as Lanshiang could get. Ada set the spray aside once the bleeding slowed and started carefully wrapping bandages around his shoulder and ribs. Her hands were steady, gentler than Leon remembered.

“You know,” he said after a moment, “your parting gift was kind of insane.”

Ada glanced up briefly. “Hm?…thought you’d appreciate the firepower.” She shrugged.

“Oh, I appreciated the firepower.” Leon watched her secure another bandage around his ribs. “The kiss was a nice touch.”

Ada actually smiled faintly at that. Then she frowned at him “you were supposed to be on the helicopter with Helena.”

“I wanted to see what you were up to,” he said making her roll her eyes. He put his shirt back on, buttoning it lazily.

“And The microchip?” she asked quietly after a moment.

Leon nodded. “She’s taking it straight to Hunnigan.” He put his vest on, unbuckled. “You didn’t have to do that, you know.”

“Yes,” she said lightly, “I did.” Leon studied her face carefully. Even now she was trying to make it sound transactional. Like risking herself to clear his name had just been another assignment.

He reached into his jacket pocket carefully and pulled something out. The compact mirror. Leon held it toward her. “You should probably keep this.” The teasing expression softened slightly at that. Wind moved between the buildings around them, carrying distant sirens through the night.

Ada looked down at the compact quietly before slipping it back beside her holster. Then she stepped away from him. “Right, I should get going,” she said after a while. “another job to finish.”

Leon frowned faintly. “Seriously?”

Ada nodded as a tired breath escaped her. “…I think I’m done with this kind of work.”

Leon looked at her carefully then. “That sounds dangerously close to retirement talk.”

“Don’t get excited.” Her eyes shifted toward him again. “I said this kind of work.”

“Right.” Leon nodded slowly. “Wouldn’t want things getting too normal.”

Ada huffed the faintest laugh under her breath. Leon looked at her before he could stop himself. “What?”

He shook his head once. “Nothing.”

“Leon.” There it was again—that look she always gave him when she already knew the answer. He should’ve looked away. Leon’s gaze dropped briefly to her mouth before returning to her eyes. Ada noticed and gave him a faint nod.

So he kissed her. Leon forgot the pain in his back entirely when his hand lifted instinctively to her waist. Ada’s fingers slid lightly against the side of his neck as she kissed him back, like neither of them were trying to run yet. The kiss was nothing like the one fifteen years ago. They pulled away just enough to still share a breath, eyes still closed for a second. “…Fifteen years,” he murmured, still alive. And he was close enough that he could feel her breathing. “…feels like forever” Leon admitted quietly.

Ada’s hand lingered lightly against the side of his neck before she slowly pulled it away. Leon caught it before she fully could. Just enough to stop her from retreating completely. “Ada…” Leon’s thumb brushed faintly against her wrist. Then, before he could overthink it, he leaned forward and kissed her again. Softer this time. Like he’d spent years wanting to do it properly. Ada seemed briefly surprised by it before she kissed him back. Her fingers slid into the front of his jacket automatically, holding onto him despite herself. When they finally separated again, neither of them moved far. Leon rested his forehead lightly against hers, exhaling shakily through a tired smile. “…Are you going to leave now?”

Ada’s expression softened in that dangerous way it only ever did around him. “I have to,” she admitted quietly. “Eventually.” his breaking heart held by her steady hand on the side of his neck. Almost as guilt drowned her eyes. Hard to tell with Ada.

“Still answering questions like a spy?” Leon gave a faint huff of laughter at that. “You said you were done with this kind of work.”

“I said I was thinking about it.”

“Which means absolutely nothing.”

Ada’s lips twitched slightly. “There’s the Leon Kennedy I remember.” still not pulling away from him just yet.

“So what is it now? Good bye, Ada?” He studied her face carefully, the wind moving strands of her dark hair across her cheeks.

Ada looked at him for a long moment. “I’m not very good at goodbyes.”

“Yeah,” Leon murmured. “I noticed.” Something flickered behind her eyes then. “Guess coming here was a bad decision,” he admitted after a moment, stepping away from her.

Ada’s gaze dropped briefly toward the bandages wrapped around his shoulder. “No,” she said softly. Ada looked back up at him, then at his lips. “Not really.”

That caught him off guard enough to leave him quiet. The wind picked up around the rooftop, carrying ash through the air between them. Ada glanced toward the skyline, He watched her for another second before saying : “You could stay.” The words came out before he could stop them. He sat on the concrete slab as he took in ada’s expression.

She had gone still, like he had just voiced a thought she swore she had decided against. “You know I can’t,” she said quietly.

“Because of your job?”

“Because of yours. They will fire you.”

Leon looked away briefly, jaw tightening. “I sure hope they do” he muttered. That sounded a little too true. “You really believe they would fire me? I have a feeling they’re gonna keep me working till I’m seventy.”

Ada laughed softly, and stepped closer again until she was standing between his knees, close enough that he had to tilt his head slightly to look at her. “Leon,” she said softly, her hand placed on his chest lightly.

“Hm?”

The silence between them stretched quietly while the city burned below, neither of them looking away first. Ada exhaled softly through her nose, almost amused at herself. “You know,” she murmured, “you’re making this very difficult.”

Leon’s brow lifted slightly. “What?”

“Leaving.” The word hung there between them. That might’ve been the most honest thing she’d ever said to him. Leon let out a quiet breath through his nose, looking down briefly before his hand found hers again almost absentmindedly.

Leon looked at her carefully, like he wasn’t entirely sure he heard her right.

Ada gave a faint shake of her head, eyes dropping briefly toward the rooftop before returning to him. “This was supposed to be simple,” she admitted quietly. “Destroy Simmons and his work. Get you the evidence. Leave.”

“Sounds like a solid plan.”

“It was.” A faint, tired smile touched her lips “Then you decided to nearly die on me.”

Leon let out a breath that almost sounded like a laugh. “In my defense, you started it.”

Ada rolled her eyes softly, but her fingers tightened slightly around his. “You weren’t supposed to come save me,”

“That’s bullshit, Ada.” He scoffed. “You know I would,”

Ada held his gaze. This time, she didn’t deny it. “It still surprised me,”

“It’s surprises you that I care?” He said,

“I wish you didn’t. It would’ve made this much easier.” Ada’s composure cracked just enough for him to see it. “You should get some rest,” she murmured as she stepped back from him, he caught her hand.

“Is that an order?”

“A suggestion, its not like you listen to me anyway.” But this time, Ada was smiling when she said it.

“Fair.” he said, letting go of her hand. “Good bye, Ada.”

She nodded, “but how are you getting back?”

Leon instinctively reached for his earpiece “I’ll—” Only to remember he’d thrown it off a building hours ago. “…Right.”

“No backup?”

Leon sighed. “Lost the comms.” Didnt elaborate further.

“You came here injured with no back up? Seriously?” she said, shaking her head when he looked away from her.

He watched as she moved toward the rooftop access terminal panel, prying it open before it sparked once before stabilizing beneath Ada’s hands. Leon leaned against the concrete ledge nearby, watching her work while trying not to focus on the pain still burning through his shoulder and back. “You can hack a rooftop transmitter now too?” he asked tiredly.

Ada didn’t look up from the exposed wiring. “I like to diversify my skill set.” A few quick keystrokes later, static crackled through the emergency radio tower mounted near the ledge. Ada adjusted the frequency carefully before holding the receiver out toward him. “Here,” she said. “I tapped into the local air channels, don’t tell Chris I’m alive.”

Leon took the receiver from her. “Ada—” Leon frowned faintly. “You really think that’s better?”

“For now? Yes.” she said, He clearly didn’t agree. “You can argue with me later.”

Leon sighed through his nose before finally lifting the receiver. “Chris, you there?”

Static answered for a second. Then— “Leon?” Relief hit Chris’s voice instantly. Exhausted relief. “You’re alive.”

“Yeah.” Leon glanced briefly toward Ada beside him. “Still working on that.” A short breath crackled through the comms. “I need pickup,” he said. “There’s a nearby rooftop with a helipad east of Quad Tower.”

“I got you.” Chris answered immediately. “Sit tight. I’m on my way.”

“Thanks.”

Then Chris spoke again, quieter now. “Good to hear your voice, man.”

Leon swallowed once. “Yeah,” he answered softly. “You too.” The line clicked dead. For a while neither Leon nor Ada spoke. Wind moved between the rooftops while the city burned beneath them in fractured lights and smoke.

“That was faster than I expected.” Ada stepped beside him at the edge of the roof, arms folding loosely as she scanned the skyline. “We still have to get there.” Ada adjusted the strap of her grapple gun against her hip. “Looks like you’re finally taking my route for once.”

Leon glanced at her. “What route?” Ada gave him a look. Holding up the grapple hook. “You’ve got to be kidding me Ada, there’s no way that’s going to hold us”

She shrugged “you think I can’t carry you? Who do you think got you on Luis’ extraction table?” Of course that was her. The surrounding buildings were too far to jump, and the Quad Tower itself looked seconds away from collapsing inward. Ada stepped closer, calm as ever despite the collapsing skyscraper around them. “You trust me?”

Leon looked at her. After everything tonight—Simmons, rooftop, confession, The answer came easily now.“…Yeah,” he admitted quietly. “I do.”

For a moment, Ada’s expression softened again. Then she clipped the grapple line to the edge of the roof. “Good,” she said smoothly. “Because we’re about to do something incredibly stupid.”

Leon looked over the side of the skyscraper. “You have done this before… right?" A very long drop.

Ada actually laughed softly at that. “I don’t normally offer express transportation to DSO agents, but for you, first times free.” she smirked.

“…You know,” he muttered, “I’m starting to think this is your idea of flirting.”

A faint smile crossed her face when she firmly held him around his waist. “Hold onto to me.” she said. The grapple gun latched itself to the roof top of the building across from them before Leon could comment on how terrible this idea was, Ada stepped off the edge with him. The city dropped out beneath them instantly. Wind tore past as the grapple line pulled them across the skyline, smoke and neon lights blurring below. Leon’s arm tightened instinctively around Ada’s waist while she stayed impossibly steady against him, one hand firm against his chest to keep him balanced mid-swing. The rooftop rushed toward them fast. Ada adjusted their momentum smoothly at the last second before both of them hit the concrete harder than gracefully. Leon stumbled from the landing immediately, balance thrown off from both the impact and the injuries in his back. Ada caught him before he could fully lose it. One arm stayed securely around his waist while the other steadied against his chest, keeping him upright as he regained his footing. “Told you I had it,” she murmured.

Leon let out a breath, still mildly disoriented from the swing. “…Yeah, never doing this again.”

The smile on her face softened afterward, something quieter slipping into it as the reality of what came next settled between them again. Because both of them knew this rooftop— this moment— was temporary. Still holding onto him, she gave a light pat against the center of his chest. “You survived.”

Leon looked down briefly at her hand still resting there before meeting her eyes again. “Barely.”

“Mm.” Ada’s gaze lingered on him for a second too long. “Still counts.” Far in the distance, helicopter lights appeared through the smoke. “Looks like we made it in time” she said looking at the chopper. Leon didn't take his eyes off of her, he couldn't. They stood in a shadow of a wall, in something that felt like a pocket of time. “Take care of yourself Leon,” she said with a smile.

He instinctively held her hand over his chest. “Don’t say that.” Ada studied him when he said that. “It makes it sound like…”

“I’ll never see you again?” she asked.

He swallowed. “Raccoon city. The bridge.”

Her gaze softened at his words. She smiled softly at him with fondness. “You still think of that?”

That memory still lived between them after all these years. The rain. Her hand slipping from his grasp. Leon helplessly watched her disappear into darkness. He nodded with a clenched jaw. “More than I should.” He admitted, tucking a strand of hair behind her ear.

“Well? What can I say? It’s not a bad way to go,” she says softly.

“That's not funny…not when it was my fault—“ he said

“Leon.” Ada cut him off gently but firmly. The helicopter lights were getting closer now. Time running out again. “You need to stop carrying every death like it belongs to you,” she said softly.

The words hit him harder than anything else tonight. Leon stared at her silently for a moment. Then his hand moved carefully to the side of her face. “Ada…”

The approaching helicopter thundered louder overhead. But neither of them looked away yet. Ada’s gaze lingered on him for another second before she reached into her pocket. The compact mirror. Without a word, she slipped it carefully into the front pocket of his shirt, fingers brushing lightly against the fabric over his chest. “For the next time I see you,” she said softly.

Leon looked down at his pocket, then back at her. For once, he didn’t have a response ready. he hadn’t expected that. Because that wasn’t a goodbye. Before he could process that thought properly, Ada leaned in again and kissed him on the cheek. Leon barely had enough time to react before she started to pull away again, his arms instinctively caught her lightly at her waist, trying to stop the moment from ending so fast.

Ada noticed the look on his face at once, faint amusement touching her expression. “What?” she murmured.

Leon stared at her for a second longer than he meant to. “…You’re planning on seeing me again.” The words sounded more surprised than accusing.

Ada had always been someone who disappeared after moments like this. Now she was standing in front of him talking about next time like it was obvious there would be one. “You sound surprised.”

“I am.” Leon shook his head faintly, almost disbelieving. The helicopter blades thundered louder now, Chris closing in fast somewhere above the smoke-filled skyline.

“Well, I figured id tell you the long story once we both have the time.” she said softly, and something in Leon’s chest tightened painfully at that. After years of almosts and unfinished goodbyes, hearing her say that felt dangerously close to hope. The way he looked at her like he wanted to believe her but didn’t quite know how yet. So she stepped closer one last time, her hand settling lightly against the side of his jaw.

Leon didn’t move away from her touch. He just looked at her for a moment like he was trying to decide if hope was something he was allowed to have. “…A long story,” he repeated quietly.

Ada’s thumb brushed lightly against his jaw. “Mhm.”

Leon exhaled through his nose, something almost like a tired laugh. “Like what? Over coffee?”

She smiled raising her brow “Maybe? let’s see how this plays out,” she said “I’ll find you when the time is right,” Ada said softly kissing his cheek briefly. She started backing away toward the far side of the rooftop, toward the shadows where Chris wouldn’t immediately spot her. "Don't worry yourself waiting.”

Still, he didn’t step back yet. Instead, his voice lowered just slightly. “You’ll just… show up when it’s ‘the right time’?”

Ada’s lips curved faintly. “If you prefer,” she said, “I can stop showing up entirely.”

Leon immediately shook his head once. “Don't joke about that.”

“Don’t overthink it,” she said.

Leon let out a quiet breath through his nose before finally admitting:“…Yeah. See you then.” Ada smiled softly at that. Then the helicopter spotlight swept across the rooftop. Time was up. Ada looked toward the approaching aircraft briefly before her eyes returned to him one last time. Leon already hated the look on her face. It was not a goodbye. But it was close enough to scare him anyway.

"later,” Ada said softly as she started backing away toward the far side of the rooftop, toward the shadows where Chris wouldn’t immediately spot her.

Leon watched her go, the words settling somewhere deep in his chest. Then his hand drifted absently toward the compact mirror in his shirt pocket. He pulled it out slowly, turning it once beneath the rooftop lights. The metal was etched with butterflies, but he could still see the wear near the edge, finding his thumb brushing over the wings unconsciously. Above him, rotor blades thundered louder through the smoke-filled skyline. Leon looked up just as Chris’s helicopter emerged between the surrounding towers, spotlight sweeping across the rooftop. Chris spotted him almost immediately. The helicopter shifted position carefully, descending toward the helipad nearby. Leon glanced back toward Ada. She had already stepped farther into the darkness near the ruined edge of the rooftop, hidden just enough that Chris wouldn’t notice her from the cockpit.

Ada tilted her head faintly toward the lowering helicopter. Go. she mouthed, I’ll be fine. He nodded once. But he didn’t look away from her. Leon’s eyes stayed fixed on Ada standing half-hidden in the smoke and shadows, the wind moving through her dark hair while the city burned behind her.

“Leon!” Chris called out the second the helicopter fully touched down. “You look like hell.”

Leon huffed tiredly. “Good to see you too.”

Chris shook his head once, relief finally breaking through the exhaustion written all over his face. “C’mon,” he said. “Let’s get out of here.”

Leon nodded automatically, but before stepping toward the helicopter he looked back one last time. Ada was still there. Half-hidden near the ruined edge of the rooftop, shadows curling around her while the wind swept ash through the night air. Even from this distance, he caught the faintest smile pulling at her lips, which made his hand tighten unconsciously around the compact mirror still in his hand.

Chris noticed the direction of his stare but didn’t comment on it. The helicopter lifted moments later, pulling away from the rooftop and into the smoke-filled skyline above Lanshiang. Leon sat heavily against the side of the cabin, finally letting exhaustion hit him properly now that the adrenaline was wearing off.

Chris glanced at the bandages around Leon’s shoulder immediately. “…The hell happened to you?”

“Long story.”

Chris gave him a look. “Try me.”

Leon leaned his head back briefly against the cabin wall. “Simmons happened.”

“Yeah, figured that much.” Chris adjusted his headpiece slightly, expression easing just enough to become more familiar. “How’d you get separated from Helena?”

Leon rubbed tiredly at the side of his face, glancing out the helicopter window toward the distant rooftops disappearing behind them.“…Had something else to take care of.”

Chris followed his gaze for half a second and sighed. “Right.”

Leon looked back at him. “You’re not gonna ask?”

Chris shook his head faintly, somewhere between amused and exhausted. “Just glad you made it out.”

Leon looked over at him. Understanding that look better than he wanted to. “…You too,” he answered softly.

Chris nodded once. The helicopter cut through the smoke above Lanshiang, city lights flickering beneath them in fractured streaks through the cabin windows. He kept his focus mostly on the skyline ahead, though his eyes flicked toward Leon every so often like he was still making sure he hadn’t passed out yet. “You bleeding?”

Leon leaned back carefully against the seat, trying not to aggravate the bandages beneath his jacket. “Nope,” he answered. “Got everything patched up.”

Chris looked over briefly. “Then what’s that on your face?”

Leon frowned faintly. “What?”

Chris motioned vaguely toward his own cheek while keeping the helicopter steady. “Left side.”

Leon reached up automatically. His fingers brushed against something faintly smeared near his cheekbone. He froze internally when his fingers came away with a faint red smudge…Oh, you have got to be kidding me.

Chris noticed the exact moment realization hit him. He raised with eyebrows giving him a side glance. “Oh Kennedy.” Shaking his head with amusement.

Leon immediately wiped at his face with the heel of his hand. “It’s not what it looks like.”

Chris snorted. “Really.”

Leon immediately wiped the mark away against his sleeve. “Yeah, uh…” He cleared his throat “Probably blood or something.”

Chris looked unconvinced. “Bright red blood. On your cheek.”

Leon stared out the helicopter window. “Lot of weird stuff happened tonight, Chris.”

Then Chris snorted quietly to himself and shook his head. “You disappear from Helena for twenty minutes looking like you got mauled by a B.O.W…” He glanced sideways. “…and somehow come back with lipstick on your face.”

Leon stayed quiet because there was absolutely no believable explanation he could legally give. “Let’s just say, I saved a lady and she thanked me with a kiss,” Chris stared at him for another second before laughing quietly under his breath. “You know what?” he said. “I'm not even going to question it.”

“That makes two of us.”

Chris smirked faintly and looked back toward the skyline ahead. But Leon caught the amusement still lingering in his voice when he added: “…Glad somebody had a better night than me.”

Chapter End Notes

How long will it be until they eachother ‘down the road’?

Chapter 3: “So, about that long story.”

Chapter Notes

one thing i see people forget often is that ada and leon can contact eachother, so why not make the most use of it?

(Less than a month later after the end of RE6)

Rain tapped steadily against the windows of the bar, blurring the city lights outside into streaks of gold and white. The place was quiet for a Friday night. A few people scattered near the counter. Low music humming through old speakers. Nothing loud enough to demand attention. Leon preferred places like that now. Less noise to think through. He sat alone in the corner booth furthest from the entrance out of habit, one arm resting near a half-empty glass while his phone glowed dimly in his hand.

Ada: did I wake you?

Leon: no, I’m out right now,

Ada: What are you doing?

Leon stared at the message for a second before replying. Leon: Depends. Is this work or are you checking on me?

The typing bubble appeared almost immediately. Disappeared. Appeared again. Ada: Does it matter?

Leon leaned back slightly against the booth. A faint smirk pulled at his mouth before he typed back. Leon: A little.

This time her response took longer. Ada: Not work. Why would I contact you for work?

Leon: Maybe you're in trouble?

Ada: and you think I would call you for help?

Leon: maybe. Leon glanced briefly at the untouched plate the bartender had dropped off five minutes ago. Leon: it’s been a long week.

Ada: thought you were taking time off

Leon: I am. got bored in six hours.

A few seconds passed. Ada: That might be the saddest thing you’ve ever said.

Leon actually laughed softly under his breath at that, right then— the seat across from him pulled out. Leon looked up automatically, blinking as Ada slid into the booth like she’d been expected all along, dark coat still damp from the rain outside. One ungloved hand rested briefly against the table as she settled in across from him. “…That’s one way to answer a text.”

Ada set her phone down calmly beside his drink. “I was nearby.” She took off her coat, letting the dim yellow lights make the deep red of her top more vibrant.

“That’s never reassuring when you say it.”

A faint flicker of amusement crossed her face. “You looked inconvenienced for a second.”

Leon leaned back slightly, still processing the fact she was physically here, sitting across from him like this was normal. “That depends,” he said carefully. “Are things going to get more complicated from here?”

Ada glanced around the quiet pub once. “Nothing I planned.” she smirked faintly, taking a fry off his plate. “You know you can text while eating right?”

Leon watched her with narrowed eyes as she calmly bit it. “You know,” he muttered, “most people just say hello.”

“You’re assuming I’m most people?” She said with a pleased look on her face.

A waitress passed by a moment later and slowed slightly beside the table. The waitress glanced between them politely. “Can I get you something?”

“Whiskey,” she said simply. “Neat.”

“Is that all?

Ada looked up politely “For now yes,”

The waitress nodded before turning toward Leon. “You ordering anything else?”

Leon looked down at the untouched food in front of him and shook his head. The waitress left. Ada’s eyes drifted toward his plate of chicken tenders and fries. “You haven’t touched that.”

Leon followed her gaze briefly. “Was getting there.”

“You’ve been staring at your phone for ten minutes.” Leon picked at the edge of a fry before finally giving up and eating one properly. “There,” she said quietly, folding her arms. “Progress.” the waitress dropped off Ada's whiskey on the table.

Leon pointed at her with the fry. “You know, you’re surprisingly judgmental for someone who disappeared for months only to show up to eat my fries.”

That earned the faintest smile from her. “It’s not even been a month.” she said. “Miss me that much?” She smirked.

Leon shook his head picking up his glass. “Yeah, well.” Leon took another sip from his glass. “I’m dramatic.” Ada huffed softly at that into her glass. The rain continued outside while Leon studied her quietly across the table. She looked less sharp around the edges without the tension of a mission hanging over her. Leon took another sip from his glass before looking back at her properly. “So,” he said carefully, “about that long story.”

Ada’s fingers stilled slightly around her whiskey glass. For the first time since sitting down, some of the lighter ease between them faded. “You don’t have to tell me everything,” he added after a second. “I know how this works.”

Ada looked at him over the rim of her glass. “Do you?”

“Ada,” he said.

A faint pause settled before she finally set the whiskey down. “Simmons,” she said calmly, though Leon caught the subtle tension beneath it. “Was involved in ordering the strike on Raccoon City.” Leon’s expression hardened instantly. Even now, after all these years, hearing those words still hit like a punch to the ribs. Ada watched the reaction flicker across his face before continuing. “I didn’t know at first,” she admitted. “The Family kept most of their operations compartmentalized. Information was currency.” Her gaze lowered briefly toward the table. “But once I found out… I cut ties.”

Leon leaned back slightly, eyes fixed on her. “So...”

Ada exhaled softly through her nose before continuing. “So? Simmons didn’t take it well.” That was putting it lightly. Leon remembered Simmons on that rooftop. The obsession in his voice. The way he looked at Ada, as if she belonged to him. “Simmons was…” She searched briefly for the right word before settling on: “Fixated.”

“That’s one way to describe it.” A muscle tightened in Leon’s jaw.

A faint humorless smile crossed her face. “He thought loyalty was something he could buy.” Her eyes shifted back toward Leon’s. “And when that stopped working, he convinced himself the problem was me.”

Leon’s grip tightened slightly around his glass. “So he made Carla.”

Ada nodded once. “The C-virus gave him a way to recreate whatever version of me he wanted.” Her voice stayed controlled, but Leon could hear the disgust beneath it now. “Carla Radames volunteered to help develop the virus. Simmons used that against her.”

Leon frowned faintly. “Volunteered?”

“She admired him, you could say.” Ada’s expression hardened slightly. “That turned out to be a mistake.”

“Because what could go wrong being head over heels for a mad scientist, right?” Leon scoffed, remembering Carla’s hatred toward Ada, and the destruction she left behind trying to become someone else entirely. All because Simmons couldn’t accept rejection. “Can’t take a hint, can he?,” Leon muttered.

“It’s power.” Ada corrected quietly. “Men like Simmons stop hearing the word no after a certain point.”

Leon looked at her for a long moment after that. “You should’ve told me.”

Ada’s brow lifted faintly. “About Simmons?”

“That he was targeting you like that.”

“I didn’t know until it happened,” Ada gave a small shake of her head. “It wouldn’t have changed anything.”

“It would’ve changed how I handled him.” There was something colder underneath Leon’s voice now.

Something that made Ada study him more carefully. “You’re angry?” she observed quietly.

Leon let out a short breath through his nose. “Guy turns himself into a nightmare bio-weapon to terrorize an entire city because he thinks he owns you like some desperate ex?” His jaw tightened slightly. “Yeah. I’m angry.”

Ada looked at him for a second longer than necessary after that. “Jealous?” She smiled faintly.

Leon blinked once. “…What?”

“Relax, Leon.” She lifted her glass again. “He’s dead.” Ada hid the amusement behind another sip of whiskey.

“…You can’t just say things like that.”

“Why not?” she murmured calmly over the rim of her glass, “I just did.” Ada finished the last sip of whiskey slowly before setting the glass down against the table.

He watched her check the time on her watch and felt something in his chest sink a little despite himself. There it is. The leaving part. Something he’s afraid he’ll never get used to. Leon looked away toward his drink, as his jaw tightened slightly. “Got another flight to catch?” he asked, trying for casualness and not quite getting there.

Ada didn’t answer right away. Instead, she walked around the table. Leon frowned faintly, following her movement with his eyes—then went still when she slid into the booth beside him instead. She leaned her head lightly against her palm to look at him properly beneath the dim amber lights of the pub. The softer lighting caught the edges of her features differently up close. A faint amusement flickered in her eyes. “What?” she asked softly. “Thought I was leaving already?” Leon opened his mouth for nothing useful to come out. Ada’s smile widened just slightly at that rare victory. “Don’t tell me you’re nervous now that im sitting beside you.”

That finally got a reaction out of him. “I fought more bioweapons than I have met people,” Leon muttered. “I think I can handle sitting next to you.”

Ada only looked calmly pleased with herself. “You haven’t changed one bit, have you Leon?”

“…You can’t possibly know that.”

“I’m very observant.” She replied.

Leon let out a quiet breath through his nose, somewhere between exhausted and completely doomed. “Yeah,” he murmured, unable to stop the small smile tugging at his mouth now. “I know.” He picked fry as an aversion. Leon glanced down when Ada reached over and casually took another fry from his plate, pulling a quiet laugh out of him. “Ohh so there it is,” he muttered. “This had to be about something.”

Ada hummed lightly, unbothered. “The truth comes out eventually.”

"didn't know you'd stoop so low" Leon shifted slightly in the booth so he could look at her better beside him. “sitting next to me just to get closer to the fries, huh?”

Ada leaned her cheek more comfortably against her hand, the dim amber lights catching softly against her features while the slow music drifted through the pub around them. “You caught me,” she said quietly.

Leon shook his head faintly. “Fifteen years,” he murmured. “And this is the con that finally works.”

Ada’s shoulders moved slightly with a quiet laugh under her breath. It was subtle, but Leon quickly realized that he liked the sound of that probably more than he should.

Leon nudged the plate further between them. “Try the chicken,” he said. “It’s probably the only decent thing on the menu.”

Ada gave him a mildly skeptical look but tore off a piece anyway. To Leon’s satisfaction, she paused halfway through chewing. “…Not terrible, for kids meal,” she admitted.

“Kids meal? Seriously?” He raised a brow

“It’s… comforting I suppose”

Leon leaned back slightly. “High praise.”

Ada ignored that, taking another smaller bite before a tiny crumb caught briefly against the edge of her lip gloss. Without really thinking about it, he reached into his jacket pocket for the napkin he’d stuffed there earlier. But as he pulled it out, something else slipped free with it. His keys dropped onto the booth seat between them with a soft metallic clink. Ada’s eyes lowered to the booth seat and there it was: The small teddy bear keychain hanging from the ring. Ada looked at it for a second before slowly lifting her eyes back to his face. “…hm?” she said softly. There was a smile in her voice already. "you kept that old thing?"

Leon grabbed the napkin a little too fast and handed it to her while trying very hard to pretend this was not suddenly the most embarrassing moment of his life. “Don’t.”

A faint laugh escaped her as she dabbed carefully at the corner of her mouth. Then she picked up the little bear keychain gently between two fingers, studying it beneath the dim pub lights.

Leon let out a long suffering sigh and looked away toward his drink. “…It’s been a while since Spain.”

“So I see.” There was something warmer underneath her teasing now. Ada turned the tiny bear slightly, thumb brushing over the faded blue ribbon. “I bought it in Paris,” she said casually. “Thought it reminded me of rookie Leon Kennedy.”

Leon looked back at the keychain in her hand. Ada caught the completely unprepared look on his face. The fact that somehow this stupid little keychain had emotionally taken him out harder than several bio-weapons. “Didn’t think you’d be so sentimental.”

“Didn’t think you would actually keep it,” A soft smile pulled at her lips. “I guess we surprise ourselves sometimes.”

Leon huffed quietly, rubbing a hand across the back of his neck. “…you could say that.”

Ada’s gaze lingered on him before she carefully hooked the keychain back onto his fingers instead of setting it down. the tiny blue bowtie still unmistakably matching the color of his old RPD uniform. “Twins,” she murmured.

“Okay that’s ridiculous.” Leon huffed. “And concerning on multiple levels.” He said going over the worn fabric of the bear. “I aged better.”

“Seriously?” She raised her brow. “I thought you were going to say ‘I’m unbearable.’ ”

Leon smirked, leaning closer to her into a conspiratorial whisper. "Is that a pun, Ms Wong?”

She rolls her eyes with a faint smile. “Unintended.” Leon surprised her when he kissed her cheek. "aren't you getting bold?" she whispers turning to him.

Leon shrugged, failing to look innocent. "You stole my fries."

"That's your defense?"

"It felt justified."

Ada shook her head with a quiet laugh. "Mr. Kennedy, that may be the weakest excuse I've ever heard."

"Worked, didn't it?"

"It absolutely did not." The smile lingered on Ada's lips for a second before she reached for her whiskey again.

Leon grinned into his drink as Ada was watched the room lazily, turning her glass in slow circles against the table. She was looking at another table with a drunk guy. “What’s caught you attention?” he asked.

She lifted a fry and subtly pointed at a terrible first date happening across the bar. "He's going to spill that drink."

Leon glances over. "No chance." Thirty seconds later, the guy at the other table knocked his drink clean over trying to gesture mid-sentence. Leon exhaled through his nose. “…I hate that you’re right.” Beer everywhere, mostly on his date’s dress, she looked horrified. “What an idiot, why is he laughing at her?”

“Chivalry must be dead” Ada whispered to him.

Leon chuckled, “Chivalry’s been dead. That guy’s just making sure it stays that way.” he said. “What a guy.”

Ada took another sip of whiskey with a chuckle. “The audacity.”

A waitress swung by their table, "You two doing okay over here?" Leon nodded. The waitress looked at Ada. "Anything else for you?"

Ada glanced down at the menu sitting forgotten near Leon's elbow. To his complete surprise: "Dessert." Ada looked at the menu for all of three seconds. "Chocolate fudge cake."

The waitress smiled. “Alright, anything else?" Ada shook her head and the waitress scribbled it down and disappeared again.

Leon stared at Ada, she had a sweet tooth all this time?

Ada calmly set her glass down. "What?"

"I don't know." Leon gestured vaguely. "You always seemed more like... black coffee and classified documents."

"I'm glad to know what image you've built of me."

Leon leaned back slightly, seeing her in a new light. "So this happens often? You voluntarily enjoying yourself."

Ada studied him over the rim of her glass. “I enjoy myself plenty.”

The waitress returned a few minutes later carrying a generous slice of chocolate cake. She set it between them with two forks, they noticed the cake having a heart fondant on it. The waitress merely smiled knowingly. "Enjoy, you two. "

Leon didn't know what to say at first. "Apparently we've been assigned a team objective.” "Try not to fail it." she said, as he watched her take the first bite. “Mm It’s good.” Ada stared at him because he was looking at her as if he had seen completely otherworldly. “What?” She took another deliberate bite, just to annoy him.

Unfortunately for her, Leon looked entirely pleased by this development. “I'm realising I know pretty much nothing mundane about you”

“That’s because this is our longest conversation outside of surviving bioterrorism,” she nudged the plate further in his direction, “stop staring at me and try it”

Leon didn't even bother denying it this time. “I'm just trying to wrap my head around all this” he gestured around them, because the thought of sharing a chocolate cake with Ada at a pub was something that was too much of a fantasy for him to think it through. “The cake’s good” he said after trying it. “But did you really think of me when you were in paris or are you just pulling my leg?”

The subject change was so abrupt that Ada laughed. “Seriously?”

"I'm serious."

“So am I,”

“You think of me when you're on missions,” he said.

Ada took another bite, thinking. “More like dreading meeting you, because you always make things complicated.”

“Right.” He glanced at her sideways before he could stop himself. “So, what else is new?” he said finally, trying to sound casual and failing slightly less than usual. “Back in Lanshiang… you said you were done with the bioterror stuff.”

“I said I was thinking of it,” she corrected. Her eyes flicked to his, calm as ever. “Most of my work has been… different lately.”

Leon raised a brow slightly, leaning back in the booth. “Different how?”

Ada took her time before answering. “Normal intelligence work,” she said. “Surveillance. Extraction. Data recovery.”

Leon gave a short nod. “Normal spy stuff.”

Ada glanced at him. “‘Normal’ is generous.”

A faint smirk tugged at Leon’s mouth. “And?” he prompted.

Ada’s gaze shifted slightly, like she was weighing whether to say it. “I’ve been enjoying training more with long-range weapons.”

Leon blinked. “…Sniping?”

“Yes. Nothing knew.” That landed in his brain with the same delayed processing time as most things she said that rewrote his understanding of her. Something unhelpful and immediate flickered through his thoughts before he could stop it: Ada perched on a roof top handling a firearm almost half her size.

He cleared his throat quickly and looked down at his empty plate like it had suddenly become very interesting. “Right.” He reached for his drink just to have something to do with his hands, the cake was finished.

Ada watched him carefully now, just to see the reaction settle in. “You look like you have an opinion.”

“…No opinion.”

Ada’s eyes narrowed slightly. “Not convincing.”

Leon rubbed the back of his neck, glancing at her for a second too long. “…It suits you.”

Before Ada could reply, the waitress returned. “Alright, I’ve got your bill—” She placed it down between them.

“I’ve got it,” he said before Ada even moved, she turned her head toward him slowly, expecting him to look at her but Leon ignored and reached for his wallet anyway. Somewhere along the lines, his brain had decided this was important. It just felt wrong for her to pay. Which was ridiculous, considering who she was and how many governments probably owed her money. He slid the receipt back toward the waitress. “Thanks.”

Ada finally spoke the second the waitress walked away. “You didn’t have to do that...”

“I know.” He said watching Ada smile, amused in a way that felt a little too knowing. Leon felt his ears go faintly warm and immediately decided to ignore that fact. “What?” he asked.

Ada tilted her head slightly. “You’re very traditional.”

“…It’s not a big deal,” he muttered, because arguing this felt like a losing battle he didn’t sign up for. “You just ordered a drink and cake.”

“I’ll remember that,” she said quietly.

Leon narrowed his eyes. “That sounds like a threat.”

“It isn’t.”

He didn’t believe her. “Were not keeping score,” he insisted,

“Right, this isn't a date.”

“Absolutely.” He nodded solemnly.

After that, the moment Leon dreaded came, this pocket of normality was coming to an end when Ada stood, the booth suddenly felt much emptier, losing its warmth. Leon followed a second later as she slipped her coat on properly and reached for her phone. “This was fun,”

“Yeah it was..”

Ada adjusted her glove, stepping closer just enough that he had to look at her properly. “You’ll be fine,” she said.

Leon blinked once before chuckling. “…That’s your goodbye?”

“What? Were you expecting a kiss again?” she teased, “I told you not to get used to it”

Leon huffed. “Wouldn’t dream of it.”

Ada studied him for a second, like she was checking whether he actually meant that. “… Good,” she said simply, returning the space between them. “I should go, I’ll leave you to your time off.” a quiet exhale of amusement from her, he already knew he’d replay later.

He nodded once. “Good night,” he said, as he watched her walk out the doors. Leon stayed standing, watching the door even after she was gone.

His phone buzzed. It was Ada of course..

Ada: Don’t overthink it.

Leon stared at the message before letting out a short breath through his nose. “…Too late,” he muttered.
