Chapter 4: "I think you should take it back."

Chapter Summary

yikes where do we go from here??? leon is already regretting this as i write chapter 5 ;)

Chapter Notes

i hope you guys arent too mad at me for this (i was listening to fallen star by the NBHD on loop)

(a few months later)

Leon stared at the compact mirror sitting beside his apartment keys. The silver casing caught the warm light of New York’s sunset. He hadn't touched it all morning, which was a conscious effort he made that day despite how he caught himself reaching for it twice without thinking—once while making checking files, once while staring out the apartment window as he drank his whiskey. Both times he'd stopped himself. He had to put an end to it. The after work calls that had become routine. The late night texts that no longer needed excuses. The fact that somewhere along the way, seeing Ada had stopped feeling unexpected and started feeling normal. He'd spent years telling himself not to get attached, years watching what happened when he failed at that. Yet somehow Ada had slipped past every defense he knew how to build. His gaze lingered on the silver casing as he downed the rest of the whiskey.

Trust had never come easily to him after Raccoon City, but against all logic, against every reason he should've known better, Ada had become the person he trusted most. The realization should've been comforting because it seemed like they had finally stopped running away from each other, Instead it terrified him.

Leon exhaled slowly and rubbed a hand across his face. Maybe he was overthinking it, trying to solve a problem that didn't exist. But lately every time his phone lit up with her name, there was a second where he wondered what would happen if one day it all stopped because Ada had left, once again. That thought had been following him for weeks, and he was getting tired of carrying it.

He picked up his phone before he could change his mind, telling himself it was also practical, something easier to hold onto. Leon leaned back slightly in his chair, eyes still fixed on the mirror, but his thoughts drifted further out.

Normal was never really on the table for him—not after Raccoon City. Whatever “normal” was supposed to look like, his life didn’t allow clean lines like that. Everything he touched eventually turned into an incident report. And now, after Simmons, things were worse. The DSO are hunting leaks, traitor links, any hint of unauthorized contact with bio-terror networks or outside operatives. Every connection was being scrutinized, every pattern analyzed. Leon knew exactly how that would look if anyone ever connected the dots between him and Ada Wong. It wouldn’t matter what she was to him, or what he told himself it wasn’t. To them, it would be a liability the moment her name was attached to his in the wrong context.

So maybe this was the better choice; because it made everything… simpler because it wasn’t one.

Stalling anymore wasn’t an option because he knew Ada would be leaving for Shanghai, they had spoken while he was still in D.C and now that he was in New York, he wanted this to get over with before she left. His phone rang in his hand until Ada picked up. “Ada... You still in New York?”

“Yeah, fight's at 9 pm” she replied over the sound of rustling in the background. “Just packing up, why?”

“I’m in the city... could you meet me at the East 72nd Street entrance to Central Park at 6pm?”

There was a brief silence on the call, this a soft chuckle “Am I being arrested?”

Normally, Leon would have joked, but today nothing felt light when his heart sinking with every word that left his mouth. “Not this time, I just need to talk.” He said trying to maintain a decent dry tone as he gave in and took the mirror into his palm.

“Need to talk? Now I'm curious, I’ll be there,” she replied. “Is everything okay?”

He clenched his hand around the metal casing. “yeah, fine.”

“See you there.” She said, and Leon hung up before he replied.

Leon let out a quiet breath, though it didn’t really ease anything. He told himself he was doing the responsible thing but not letting this get messier.

-

Ada spotted him before he saw her. Leon Kennedy was many things, subtle was not one of them. He was standing beside the wrought-iron Vanderbilt Gate, one shoulder against the stone wall, hands shoved into his jacket pockets. Half in the fading orange light of approaching evening. Shoulders slightly hunched in that way he never admitted to when he was thinking too hard and somehow that made him look more exposed than she was used to seeing him. She slowed her pace without meaning to when she neared him, something about the message had already told her this wasn’t going to be routine. Leon didn’t send “need to talk” unless he’d already talked himself into something he didn’t like. When he met her gaze she immediately recognised there it was—that fraction of hesitation he always tried to hide, like seeing her still came with a delay he hadn’t learned how to process. “…You picked a scenic location,” she said first.

Leon gave a short exhale that almost passed for a laugh. “It was closest.”

She folded her arms. “That sounds like you didn’t want to think about it.”

“I didn’t.” he said, gesturing to the path, “let’s go”

Together they drifted into the park, falling into step naturally. Leon kept his eyes ahead, the first sign he was thinking too much.

"well," Ada said after a minute. "You said you needed to talk."

"Right." he said but nothing else followed.

Leon usually filled silences with bad jokes, questions, anything. Today he seemed determined to wage war against conversation entirely. They followed one of the winding paths through the garden with flower beds lined either side. Quiet enough because the evening crowd was sparse in the Conservatory Garden, Nobody paid them any attention.

Leon glanced toward a gardener trimming hedges. "Think that guy enjoys his job?"

Ada looked at him. "Leon. You didn't ask me to come here to discuss landscaping."

His mouth twitched. "Fair." A fountain splashed softly somewhere ahead Ada only noticed it because Leon was being unusually quiet. "How was Prague?"

Ada laughed, a short disbelieving sound. "That's what we're doing?"

"It was a legitimate question."

"You don't care about Prague."

"I care a little." he said, when she glanced at him he wasn’t looking at her anymore. “You didn’t tell me much about it,” he added, with something else hiding underneath a very poor disguise. “Last time.”

Her gaze sharpened just a fraction, but her voice stayed even. “What exactly are you asking?”

Leon gave a small shrug, nothing casual about it. “You know. Contacts. Friends.” he trailed off. “Some… European boyfriend situation I wasn’t briefed on?” Ada stopped walking entirely, and it took Leon two steps before he turned back toward her. “What?”

She looked at him for a long second, studying him, with a tilt of her head. "Is that what this is about?"

Leon blinked once. "What?"

"You dragged me to Central Park," she said calmly, "to ask if I’m dating someone in Europe?"

His jaw tightened. "No." Leon exhaled through his nose "I’m not—" he started again, then stopped because he knew there was no version of this sentence that didn’t sound stupid. "I just don’t know what your life looks like when I’m not in it."

Leon’s question still hung there between them, and Ada started walking a step ahead of him, hands in her coat pockets, almost like it wasn’t worth slowing down for. “I don’t have time to entertain men.”

“Right,”

Ada glanced at him to confirm whether he understood her point. “I barely have time for sleep,” she added. “Let alone whatever you’re imagining.”

“…I’m not imagining anything,” he muttered automatically.

Ada hummed slightly, unconvinced as they continued walking and with every minute that passed she became more certain of one thing: Whatever Leon wanted to say— he had absolutely no desire to be the person who said it. A few fallen petals drifted across the stone walkway between them. "Spit it out, Leon." His shoulders stiffened immediately recognising that she knew prague wasn't the topic of interest, that the conversation he'd been trying to outrun since she arrived wasn't something she was going let continue the way it was. "You know I don't have all day."

Leon looked away first, anywhere except her. "That's not really fair."

Ada folded her arms. "Neither is making me walk laps around Central Park in heels while you rehearse in your head."

That earned a reluctant exhale from him. "You always wear heels in places you shouldn't. Props to you." She smiled faintly at his words but still had her arms folded as she waited for him to finally say it. "The DSO's been reviewing everyone." Ada's expression didn't change. "They've tightened protocols. Investigating possible information leaks."

"Leon, stop talking like you're reading from a briefing document." His jaw tightened, and for a moment she thought he might actually get annoyed. Instead he looked tired like the fight left him before it ever started. Ada watched him more carefully now taking in the dark circles beneath his eyes, the slight hunch of his shoulders, stubble growing around his jaw… the way he seemed distracted even standing still. "What's really going on?"

Leon didn't answer immediately, he just looked down at the path as if it would help in trying to choose the right words. Eventually his hand disappeared into his jacket pocket, it emerged again holding the compact mirror she gave him months ago. The silver surface reflected the evening sunlight between them. He stared at the casing like he was getting a good look at it before he sighed and held it out her without looking at her or the mirror. "I think you should take it back."

Ada felt something tighten in her chest because he had planned this. It wasn't something he'd decided five minutes ago. He'd been carrying it around all day thinking about it. She looked from the compact to his face. "Why?"

Leon swallowed as his grip tightened around the silver casing as he looked down at it, genuinely lost as if what he was about to say sounded reasonable right up until she actually showed up and asked him. His thumb brushed absently across the butterfly eching of the compact. "You know what DSO would think if they found out."

Ada's expression remained unreadable. "That suddenly matters to you months later? since when have you cared what people think?"

Leon looked away knowing he wasn't entirely sure anymore but still admitted the part he'd been avoiding. "I'm tired." he started, "I'm tired of wondering if the next phone call is going to be last." His grip tightened slightly around the compact. "I'm tired of losing people."

Ada's gaze softened almost imperceptibly. Leon laughed once, humourless for the situation. “I know were not together or anything, but were not exactly apart, are we?” He shook his head. "Just...we keep doing this."

Ada waited. "We meet." he sighed "It goes nowhere." His eyes finally met hers. "And every time I find myself wondering if that's what its going to be for the rest of time." Leon looked away again. "I think..." The words caught briefly. "...maybe we should stop."

The silence that followed only existed between two people who already understood each other.

Ada finally stepped forward intending to take the mirror. "Is that what you want?" Giving him one last chance to take the words back.

Leon looked at her as if he wished she would make this easier by telling him he was being an idiot. Instead she just looked at him with patience because she already knew the answer before he did, yet she didn't know if he was sticking with his words.

"I don't know what I want anymore," Leon admitted quietly.

"You should've started with that." For a moment she looked past the stubbornness and excuses and saw what he'd been trying to hide, but even seeing it, she couldn't force him to show that part of him to her and take it as truth. "You know," she said eventually, "for someone who spent years refusing to let me disappear...you're making a surprisingly strong effort let me go."

The compact mirror still rested in his hand, she knew he carried it through a hundred small memories attached to something that should've been meaningless. Ada took it from his hand, she’d already decided this was hers again even though it was warm from his grasp. “Do you have anything else to say?”

Leon shook his head and didn’t stop the distance as it snapped into place again between them like a drawn line.

So she turned around started down the path they took get here. She Leon watched her go, she could feel his gaze on her back as if he was expecting her to look back. She wasn't in spain anymore helping Leon, even then she knew he would refuse to take the chopper with her, which is why she went out of her way to give him a path he would be comfortable taking. This time, she didn’t fix anything, it wasn't really in her hands, so she didn't look back. Not even when she heard him softly call out her name, not even when she felt his warm hand softly hold her wrist.

His fingers tightened slightly as if he only now realised what leaving meant, to push her away before fate could do it for him. She didn’t turn yet. “…Leon,” she said.

He exhaled through his nose like he was annoyed at himself more than her. “I—” he stopped because he didn’t actually know what came after that. Ada finally turned her head slightly, just enough to see him over her shoulder. Leon’s grip loosened like he had decided he didn't deserve to hold on. “…Will you pick up if I called?” he said, the words coming out small.

Ada stared at him. “…Seriously?” she said very aware of his thumb shifting slightly against her wrist before he let go fully. “You don’t get to ask that now,” she said.

Leon nodded once, like he deserved that. “... yeah, I know.” The city moved around them like they weren’t part of it.

Ada looked at him for one last moment and turned away again.

But before she could take more than a few steps, he spoke again. “…Have a safe flight.”

Ada only turned her head slightly to the left without actually glancing back over her shoulder. “Yeah, right.”

-

Leon didn’t call her again, he just stood there with his hand still slightly raised like it remembered holding something it wasn’t allowed to keep. His hand dropped slowly back to his side as he watched her go until the path bent and swallowed her into the distance. And only when she was gone did he realize his hand was still slightly curled waiting for something that wasn’t coming back. The woman he had just spent an hour trying to convince himself he could live without seeing.

Leon didn’t move for a while. The park kept doing what it always did—people passing, leaves shifting, distant traffic bleeding in from the city beyond the trees, but none of it reached him properly. Everything felt slightly delayed, like the world had decided to buffer.

Eventually, he exhaled and started walking without really choosing a direction. Away from the gate. Deeper into the Conservatory paths. His phone buzzed in his pocket, he didn’t check it because he already knew it wouldn’t be her. There wasn’t anything official to lose with Ada, but it still felt like a part of him was ripped clean when thinking of the meetings that kept happening when they shouldn’t have, the messages that came after and stayed in his head longer than they had any right to.

Leon stopped walking near a small stretch of stone railing overlooking the garden below, his thumb brushed the edge of his jacket pocket out of habit even when he knew nothing was there anymore. He let out a slow breath through his nose, shaking his head slightly at himself. “Goddamnit,” he said quietly, standing in the aftermath of something that never officially happened, trying to figure out why it still hurt anyway. He leaned forward slightly, forearms resting on the stone railing instead of the absence int his pocket. For a second, he considered turning back, undoing it by saying something less final, even when nothing about it felt finished.

Chapter 5: "would you pick up if I called?"

Chapter Notes

enjoy hehe ;)

December

It’s been a month since he gave Ada the mirror back. Since then everything around him felt emptier than before, she had a way of having a presence in places she wasn’t physically there and it terrified him how comfortable he’d gotten over the course of barely four months of talking to her every week since Lanshiang. If he didn't know better and this was a honey trap, he would've simply been doomed.

Now as he sat in his echo of an apartment in D.C, case files over every surface imaginable, it was frustrating to say the least since he had no distractions. During the Christmas party, and he had nothing much to say other than: "The DSO spent our entire tactical budget on ornaments." both Hunnigan and Sherry refused even though he pointed out the shit load of tinsel they shoved on the tree, making it look more like a pom-pom than a Christmas tree. He returned home with free cookies and a mug sherry got him with a bear dressed up as Santa on a mug because she thought he liked bears because of Ada’s keychain that hung off his bike keys. He made a mental note to take it off later but never really quite got to it. When he told Chris about it, he said the bear looked like him so it was fitting. Seriously?

He had to go out of his way to find a new place instead of his usual, because going to there ended up in him being on edge thinking of how Ada she showed up mid conversation. Maybe even hoping that she would. The waitress even asked him where she was, and wondered if things hadn't worked out, and that was okay. He told her "you could say that" because there was nothing else he could possibly say. I mean seriously? how do you even respond to that? Usually Ada left his life first, something that he didn’t have to think much about it because he knew somewhere in his heart that she would be back, but this time the distance felt different even when it shouldn’t have been.

January

Just twenty-four hours ago, his team was alive.

It was three in the morning, January in D.C., the radiator in the corner of his apartment hissed aggressively against the freezing night outside. Leon sat on the carpet, his back pressed against the base of his couch, staring blindly at the uniform jacket tossed over the kitchen chair. The fabric was torn and dusted with grey ash from the explosion. He closed his eyes, and the silence of the apartment vanished. It was replaced by the deafening crack of the booby-trapped DSO transport vehicles. He saw the flash of the firestorm again, felt the shockwave that threw him into the asphalt. He remembered screaming into his comms, begging for a response, getting nothing but static and the crackle of burning tires.

All he remembers was dragging himself through the smoke, pulling his weapon to fight off the local A-virus outbreak that immediately mutated the survivors. He had been forced to look into the glazed, undead eyes of people he knew, people who trusted him, and watch the infection warp them into the very monsters he had spent his entire adult life trying to eradicate.

A leak, they called it. A political play. Senator Eyre’s greed and Patricio’s cowardice had bought and paid for those body bags.

Leon took a slow, burning swallow of the whiskey knowing it did nothing to numb the ache in his chest. When the DSO had called him into the military morgue to identify the remains, he had to stand in that cold room and watch the clean-up crew handle them—it all felt like a sick joke. He had spent over fifteen years giving his mind, body, and soul to the U.S. government, believing he was the shield protecting the innocent. But the corruption went all the way up, in every crevice of every sector, and they very people he swore to protect, paid the price in blood.

His phone on the floor lit up, buzzing against the wood. It was a supervisor from the DSO. Probably calling to remind him of the upcoming debrief. He remembered what Ada had told him back in Lanshiang, we’re up against the people who really run this country. At first he thought she was talking about Simmons, but now her words had an entirely knew face. Corruption. He always knew Ada was wiser than him, she probably knew he would be wronged by the very government he had stupidly spent his life defending. Isn't that why he chose to give her back her mirror? To be loyal to what he swore? To not be associated with a mercenary? Thinking of it now, it was almost comically foolish.

February

Leon dreaded this month the second he clinked glasses with Chris on New Year’s Eve. It wasn’t the couples crowding restaurants or the stupid heart-shaped decorations taped onto storefront windows. It was the color red. How many times he had unconsciously glanced back thinking it was Ada, Every time, some stupid part of his brain reacted before logic caught up. His head would turn instinctively, pulse hitching for half a second before reality settled back into place.

In the cold mountains of Colorado, he didn't have to worry about accidentally passing by Ada when he barely left his room. Snow buried the world outside in thick silence leaving only the fireplace crackling low behind him while untouched room service sat cold on the counter.

His thumb moved over old messages he already knew by memory.

Ada: I’m flying to Shanghai tomorrow night. /// 7 days

/// was their code for no contact. Leon wondered if being stuck in a very long version of no contact would have been better than whatever this was. There was some sort of comfort that would be worth the wait: the fact that it would end one day. But now, just months of silence stretching endlessly because of one conversation in Central Park that replayed in his head more often than he wanted to admit. There was no end in sight, even as Leon typed: you would have hated me in this state His thumb hovered over send for barely a second before he deleted every word. Leon: I hope you're safe. He sighed undoing his message, tipping back his head against the headboard behind him as frustration settled heavy in his chest. “This is ridiculous,” he muttered quietly into the empty room.

The irony wasn't lost on him. He was thirty-seven years old, sitting alone in Colorado at two in the morning, staring at old messages from a woman he had practically pushed out of his life himself. The rational explanations he kept feeding himself for months didn't stop him from thinking about her every single day anyway.

March

His room in Colorado was a evidence of his bad habits, bottles in various places, his clothes on the couch, empty takeout containers that still sit on the tv unit because he didn't have the heart to make housekeeping clean this mess for him. It was almost like a visual punishment, for him to see how bad it had gotten, mirroring how out of his hand grief was. He started to go on runs just to keep his body occupied and tired enough so he could force himself to sleep it was starting to become some sort of twisted routine. The bottle isn’t always in his hand, but it’s always within reach. He tells himself it’s just to take the edge off. Just something to make sleep possible. Just something to stop the replay of things he doesn’t want to analyze too deeply.

It doesn’t work the way he wants it to, he ends up thinking of her anyway, thinking maybe this would have been different if she was here. He remembered the faint amusement in her eyes when she knew he was lying to himself. The way she never asked for anything from him directly, and somehow still ended up being the only person he instinctively wanted to reach for. Because even when exhaustion finally dragged him down, even when the alcohol softened everything into something hazy and distant, his mind still found its way back to her.

Leon found himself holding his finger above the call button knowing he wouldn't press it, it was a new kind of torture, maybe he thought it was better not to call than to call and for it to unanswered.

Would you pick up if I called? He remembers the way he asked it then, as if something slipped out of him that he didn’t have the emotional distance to fully control. And then her response, You don’t get to ask me that. At the time, it had sounded like a boundary; now, months later, it feels different when it resurfaces, more like a consequence he cant go back on.

April

Its been four months here in Colorado, he didn't even realise how they went by. He still ran, still forced himself into motion most days just to keep his body from settling too long in one place. The DSO check-ins were sparse enough that he could almost forget he was technically still on their radar. Almost. Most of the time, it just felt like he’d been set aside and expected to reassemble himself quietly. Drinking became more of a habit now, while silence grew predictable, and predictability gave his thoughts too much space to repeat themselves. Always circling back to the same place.

Ada.

One night, he sits on the floor with his back against the couch, bottle resting loosely in his hand, and realizes he’s started building entire conversations in his head with someone who isn’t here. What she would think if she walked in right now. What she would say if he called her and asked her to come over. Whether she’d answer at all. Whether she’d even be annoyed, or just distant in that way that meant she already knew more than he was saying.

Regret was becoming harder to ignore the longer he stayed alone with it. He’d stopped trying to frame it as anything complicated. It was just absence. Maybe I should call her anyway?

The thought repeats itself more than he’s comfortable admitting. It makes him chuckle with self depreciation. Leon exhales through his nose, almost amused at himself. “Seems like old habits die hard.” he mutters quietly. “Or maybe,” He stares at her name on his for a long time. “I miss her.” he said it. Simple enough when nobody could hear it, not even his brain argued those words, neither did it argue when pressed call.

Dialing. Leon stares at it like it might undo itself if he looks hard enough. It keeps ringing Each ring makes the room feel more real than it should. He swallows, suddenly aware of his breathing in a way he hasn’t been for months.

.

.

.

.

.

“Hello?” Her voice cuts through everything. “Leon?”

He freezes up because everything he rehearsed, fades out of his brain, making him settle for just her name: “…Ada,”

“Leon,” she said his name again in conformation. “Do you know what time it is?”

“Late?” he asked sheepishly feeling more emotion in the first 12 seconds of this phone call than the past four months.

He heard her sigh, “You could say that,” her words making him almost smile at the annoyed tone of her voice.

“I know it’s late, I’m sorry..” He leans his head back against the couch, eyes closing for a second. “I didn’t know if you’d pick up, I didn’t—” he starts. “I didn’t know what else to do.”

“Are you safe?” her voice is slightly lower.

“Yeah, I’m safe.” he says “Just not great company for myself.”

a faint exhale from her side “What happened?”

He looked up at the tv playing softly, his hand pushing away a bottle into the others. “I don’t know anymore.”

“You called me,” she said after a moment. “You realize that?”

“Mhm” he nodded even though she couldn't see. He lets out a quiet breath that almost turns into a laugh. “Yeah,” he says. “Guess I did.” Leon sits in the silence for a moment realising he’s past the point of pretending this call was accidental. “…Can you stay on the line for a bit?” he asked quietly. “I think,” he murmured, voice rough, “I just didn’t want to be alone tonight.”

“Where are you?”

“…Colorado,” he says. “…I’ve been here for months,” he swallows. “thought it would get quieter, but it didn’t.” he looked down at his cold hands. “I just… keep thinking.”

“Leon,” she said softly, “when was the last time you slept properly?

“I don’t know…Ada…I’m just exhausted.” His tone cracked slightly on the last word.

Ada was quiet for a few seconds, listening carefully to everything he wasn't saying. "How much have you had to drink?" her voice was calm.

Leon glanced toward the collection of bottles occupying various surfaces around the room. "...Enough that I'm answering your questions honestly."

A faint sound came through the phone. "That wasn't the question."

"I know, but that's all you're going to get"

Ada actually laughed. “So much for honesty,”

Then the line settled comfortably quiet again. Leon looked at the dark hotel room that carried the evidence of months spent trying not to think. Somehow hearing her breathe on the other end made it all feel slightly less suffocating. His throat tightened unexpectedly. "...I missed you." The words came out so quietly he almost thought she hadn't heard them. "Because apparently my life gets worse when the most questionable part of it isn't around anymore."

"You've had too much whiskey." he knew it was supposed to sound like she was scolding but all he felt was fondness.

Then Leon laughed quietly. "Yeah. Probably." His fingers tightened slightly around the phone, reminded of the thought had been sitting in his head ever since she'd answered, knowing it was unreasonable. "Ada…?"

"Hm?"

He hesitated. "Never mind. I said that"

“Which part? The part where you miss me or the part where you question everything?”

Leon rubbed at his eyes. God. This was ridiculous. "Forget it..."

"You can’t take it back now," The warning in her voice made him sigh.

“It’s just…” He stared at the ceiling and finally gave up. "...Would it be insane if I asked you to come here?" The words hung there in the silence of night, all he could hear was the blood rushing in his ears.

"To Colorado."

“When you say it like that..” Leon let out a breath through his nose. "yeah I know it's ridiculous.

"That wasn't a no." she added faintly.

Leon's head turned immediately toward the phone, thinking he misheard her. "Ada."

"What?"

His pulse kicked unexpectedly. "Ada. Did you just say ‘that wasn't a no’ ?"

She laughed softly. "You've had enough whiskey to become optimistic, how poetic."

He narrowed his eyes. "You're pulling my leg,”

“Am I?” she teased. “Or did you just hear what you wanted to?"

His heart stopped for a very stupid second. Leon stared at the floor with a disbelieving laugh. “It wouldn't be the first time,”

She understood what he meant before he finished saying it. “Mhm?”

“Mhm.” he nodded.

“Well you better not wait up for me Leon," she said he could faintly hear the clicks of her keyboard, “I might not come, Colorado is four hours away”

“Shit—yeah, you must be in New York...” His stomach sank almost immediately after saying it. What the hell was he thinking? She wasn't twenty minutes away, she'd have to get on a plane. A whole flight for someone who was determined to push her a way 6 months ago. He shook his head despite her inability to see it. "That was a stupid thing to ask."

"Leon—"

"No, seriously, forget I said that,"

Ada hummed softly. "You seem very committed to changing your mind."

"I am changing my mind." Leon said that but the thought refused to leave him. Ada Wong boarding a flight because he got drunk and couldn't handle being alone with his thoughts, it made the more sober part of his brain wake up just enough to be horrified. "You shouldn't have to fly across the country because I'm having a rough night."

"A rough night? That’s seems like an understatement."

Leon snorted. "Okay. Several rough months."

He could almost picture her smile when she said "There we go." He could help but roll his eyes because somehow she always managed to sound like she was winning an argument without actually arguing.

A few seconds passed of the faint clicking of keys on her end. Working probably, something productive. Doing whatever it was Ada did at ridiculous hours of the night. "Besides," he said eventually, "you probably have somewhere to be."

"...yeah, sort of."

Leon looked down at the phone in his hand, the room didn't feel quite as empty anymore. "Thanks, anyway.." The words slipped out before he could stop them. "I shouldn't keep you long.."

Ada was quiet for a moment. "Thanks for what?"

He thought about it. "Picking up."

"Goodnight, Leon." her voice was soft enough that it settled in his chest warmly. “Get some sleep.”

"Night, Ada. I’ll try my best." he replied, just like he ended those late calls with her, back when things were normal. The call disconnected a few seconds later. Leon sat there staring at the dark screen. Colorado was still Colorado, and Ada wasn't coming. Or at least, he was completely convinced of that.

He frowned. “The hell am I supposed to do there? Collect sea shells and balance them on my head? Don’t do this me Sherry.”

“It’s a new directive,” she said. “I can’t override it.”

Leon stared at the window like it might explain itself. “…A new directive,” he repeated slowly.

“It’s not punishment.” She said gently hoping to convince him as if he had a choice in this.

Leon let out a quiet, humourless breath. “It sounds suspiciously like they're just prepping me like I’m some turkey before thanksgiving.”

she snorted softly. “…That’s a terrible metaphor.”

“It’s accurate.” He actually smiled a little at that, leaning his elbow on the table, lowering his voice slightly. “So let me get this straight,” he said. “I get dragged off field duty, forced into a retreat, and I’m not allowed to argue?”

“You weren’t exactly on field duty,” Sherry said, making him sigh. “Just… try it.”

“…Yeah,” he said. “Yeah, whatever. This is like glorified rehab, how comforting” he relented. “If I come back worse, I’m blaming you.”

“Deal.” Sherry said. When the call ended, Leon sat there for a moment longer, phone still in his hand.

“…She really did sign me up for yoga, didn’t she?” he muttered to himself eyeing the orange juice with suspicion. Though Ada was right, the view from this window was nice. Movement appeared across the table which made him look up automatically. Ada slid into the seat opposite him with effortless calm, as if they’ve been having breakfast together for years. For a second Leon just stared. The mountains behind her blurred into complete irrelevance immediately. “…I thought you left.”

Ada reached for the coffee pot sitting between the tables without looking at him. “You thought I flew all the way to Colorado to meet you in your room for thirty minutes before going back?” She poured herself coffee smoothly. “That wouldn’t exactly justify the two thousand dollar plane ticket.”

Leon blinked hard. “Two thousand dollars?”

Now she looked at him over the rim of her cup. “You’re focusing on the wrong part of that sentence.”

“No, hold on—” Leon leaned forward slightly, genuinely horrified now. “You spent two grand because I called you drunk?”

Ada tilted her head like she was pondering that thought. “When you say it like that you make yourself sound very important.”

“Ada.”

“You’re welcome, by the way. Tipped housekeeping too for their extra efforts.”

“Ada…” he was at a loss for words. “How long are you staying?”

“For another few hours.” She took a sip of coffee. “Don’t look so shocked.”

“I’m a little shocked.”

“You recover quickly. Yesterday you were a little out of it.”

Leon leaned back in his chair like he’d just been physically wounded. “Ada.”

She smiled into her coffee. There was something dangerously warm about seeing her like this in daylight. Relaxed enough to tease him. Real enough that he could actually watch her reactions instead of replaying memories of them weeks later like scraps.

His gaze drifted over her face for a second too long before he caught himself.

“Feeling better?” she asked quietly.

Leon exhaled through his nose. “…A little.” That answer seemed to satisfy her more than she let show. He realized suddenly he hadn’t had this sort of easy silence with anyone in months. His eyes drifted toward the mountains again before returning to her. “I’ll drive you to the airport later.”

Ada rolled her eyes immediately. “Yeah, yeah. Whatever.”

“I’m serious.”

“I know you are.”

Leon frowned slightly. “What’s that supposed to mean?”

“It means,” Ada said calmly, “you’re currently in the phase where you’re trying to make up for pushing me away.”

Leon opened his mouth, with no argument because unfortunately she was right. Ada smirked faintly at the realization crossing his face. “I’m just offering to drive you.”

“Mhm. Next you’ll carry my luggage and hold doors open for me.”

Leon pointed vaguely at her with his fork. “I already hold doors open for you.”

“That’s because you were raised correctly.”

He laughed quietly again, shaking his head. “…You should’ve just stayed in my room.” The words left his mouth casually enough that it took his own brain a full second to catch up with what he’d actually said.

Ada’s eyebrow lifted immediately. She leaned back slightly, the corner of her mouth curving upward. “You’re getting ahead of yourself.”

Leon stared back at her as realization hit him all at once. “Oh.” His ears actually burned a little. “Okay, that’s not— I didn’t mean it like—”

“Mhm?”

“I did not.”

“I’ll take your word for it.”

Leon groaned quietly into his coffee because Ada looked unbearably pleased with herself now, to his own horror, the sight of it made something warm settle low in his chest instead of panic. At least she was back.

Chapter End Notes
