(ps, i had a split screen of vendetta leon's photo so i can look over and think 'would he actually say this or do i just want him to say this' and i also watched the movie again, which i highly recommend to do after reading this chapter because the Leon events with Chris and Rebecca take place right after the end of this chapter!) also, to think aboutt the next chapter, what do you think is going through leons mind when theres an A virus outbreak in NYC where ada lives?

Chapter 7: “You’ll see me soon enough,”

The airport signs started appearing ahead, more frequent than the entire ride, which meant the world was preparing for separation without asking either of them for permission. Leon slowed slightly at a light, hand flexing once on the wheel because soon enough he’d have to stop driving. The airport came into view too early. He naturally wasn’t a fan of airports, especially not now. The entire environment around them was build around anticipation, whether it’s waiting for someone or watching someone go, it always felt like they were built for leaving people or something behind.

Eventually, he had to slow his car as he pulled into the drop-off lane, hands tightening slightly on the wheel as be briefly glanced over at Ada the car came to a stop. She was looking outside her window ahead at the glass doors under the 'DEPARTURE' sign. Leon stayed seated for a second before muttering: "…I hate this place,”

Ada glanced at him. “The airport?”

“The concept.” He finally turned the ignition off making the silence that followed feel absolute. Leon unbuckled his seatbelt slowly, and got out of the car. Cold air hit them both at the same time, sharper than the garden wind had been. Ada adjusted her coat automatically. Leon walked around the front of the car and stopped near her without thinking about where exactly he was supposed to stand for this part.

There wasn’t really a script for it.

Ada looked up at the terminal building for a second, then back at him. "so," The gnawing feeling in his stomach growing by the second. “…When will I see you again?” he asked before he could stop himself.

She studied him for a moment before shrugging slightly. “I haven’t decided yet…I have things to take care of,” Leon’s expression flickered—just a little. “…but you know where to find me.”

Leon nodded accepting it on paper even if his body didn’t agree. “Yeah,” he said. “I do.”

Don’t let her walk away without this.

He exhaled once through his nose, annoyed at himself for being predictable. “…Ada,” he said. She was already looking at him, he didn’t need a reason to say her name, but he said it anyway. “…I’m glad you came.” His hand lifted slightly, asking without words if he could, and Ada didn’t stop him, she smiled faintly instead, hugging him. For a second, the airport noise faded into something irrelevant. Her scent, expensive perfume mixed with the crisp air of the Colorado morning—cut through the swarm of thoughts that were in his mind. When he pressed his face against her scarf, a shiver when up his spine, anonymous if it was the wind, or just the feeling of holding her.

“I’m glad I came too...” she said, muffled against his shoulder, he felt her hands holding onto the back of his jacket. “I wish you the best of luck for the wellness retreat program.”

Leon groaned against her shoulder, pulling back slightly to look at her face, she looked like a cat who just dropped something and looked proud of it. “Did you really have to bring it up now?”

She shrugged with one shoulder, that stupid smile still on her face. "I'm working on my goodbyes,"

"but this isn't a goodbye, this is a see you later." he said.

When Ada smiled, Leon didn't fail to notice the how Colorado had rosed her cheeks. "confident, aren't we?"

Leon narrowed his eyes slightly. “You definitely know more than you're telling me.”

Ada raised an eyebrow. “That’s a vague accusation.”

"its not vague, its in your smile I can see it."

She laughed softly. “You have no evidence.”

“I don't need evidence.” Leon tightened his arms around her just slightly. “I've known you too long.”

Ada tilted her head. “And what exactly are you accusing me of, Mr. Kennedy?”

“I think,” Leon said slowly, “you had something to do with this.”

“With what?”

“The retreat.” Her expression remained perfectly innocent. Leon immediately looked more suspicious. “Remind me not to play poker with you."

“I don't know what you're talking about.” she shrugged again.

“Yes, you do.” He let out a disbelieving laugh before pulling her a little closer again. “I'm not letting go until you admit it.”

Ada laughed properly this time. “That's a terrible interrogation tactic.”

“It's working.”

“It really isn't, this is just an excuse not to let go of me.”

Leon couldn't deny it, he just huffed a quiet laugh. “I prefer ‘strategically delaying departure.’"

Ada let out a soft breath through her nose, shaking her head slightly like she’d accepted this particular brand of nonsense a long time ago. “in other words, you’re stalling,”

When he pulled back, it was only slightly. "fine," Leon exhaled slowly, holding something in his chest for months and only just realized it had weight. “…Okay,” he murmured, almost to himself. It was time for her to go.

Ada watched him for a moment, faintly amused probably because he was acting like he would never see her again. “You look like you’re bracing for impact.”

Leon let out a quiet, breathy laugh. “I am. In my own way.” That earned a look from her that said she understood exactly what he meant without him having to explain it. She adjusted her coat once more and stepped back, which made him realize he was holding her hand. “Text me when you land.”

Ada nodded. “Okay.” Leon stayed close for a second longer than he meant to, like his body hadn’t received the memo that the world was still moving around them. His hands were still holding hers, forgetting to let go. “Leon,” she said quietly. He didn’t want to let go, it was evident. Ada stepped closer and patted his chest with her other hand, making sure he was still there and not somewhere too far inside his own head. Leon nodded and let go of her hand which felt like it cost him something. His hands settled at her waist again, he sighed, knowing his body was being unreasonable by deciding it wasn’t finished with this moment even if the clock was.

Leon pulled her into him again which earned a small sound of surprise before it softened into a laugh—caught against his chest as she stepped fully into the hug. The vibration of her laugh went right through him; and for some stupid, unfair reason, his mind decided that was something he didn’t want to lose to time or distance or whatever came after this. So he held her a little tighter, just enough to memorize it properly. Ada exhaled through a soft chuckle, patting his back lightly now, as if she’d already accepted this was the part where he didn’t know how to let go. “You’ll see me soon enough,” she said, still amused, voice muffled slightly against his shoulder. The hug lingered long enough that Ada eased back just enough to look at him properly. “You planning to let go anytime soon?” she teased.

Leon blinked, looked down like he’d just discovered his own hands. “…Yeah,” he admitted, and finally loosened his grip, though reluctantly, he received an almost fond look from her. This was going to get him full of himself. Too full, maybe. He cleared his throat slightly, needing to reset the atmosphere before it got too heavy. “I feel like I’m getting more sentimental the older I get,” he said dryly.

Ada’s eyebrow lifted as she gave a quiet hum, considering whether to judge him or simply accept that as a long-term anomaly. “Bad sign for you,” she said.

Leon let out a short breath that almost became a laugh. “Yeah,” he said. “Tell me about it.” Ada glanced toward the terminal doors like she was timing her departure before looking back at him. “…Can I go now Mr. Kennedy?”

Leon nodded. “Yeah. You should… before you miss your flight.” and before I do something stupid.

“Try not to miss me too much, handsome.” Ada smiled, stepping back.

“Funny.” he quipped as she turned to go. Trying not to reach out for her again was a conscious effort until he watched her look back over her shoulder with a faint smile, right before the glass doors closed behind her where he knew he couldn't follow.

-

It was entirely too quiet now that Ada was gone. Aside from the low, rhythmic clinking of a lone bar lady wiping down glasses at the counter, Leon was now completely alone.

He sat on the same place as he did before Ada left. He leaned back, his thumb idly tracing the glass rim of the almost over bottle. She had been dead serious when she handed it over—one last bottle, a strict allowance before he’d be forced to stay sober for the proper retreat. He’d actually listened to her, which was surprising considering how many of those bottles he had drunk in the past 4 months.

He took a slow sip, letting the burn settle in his bones in hopes of replicating the vibration of Ada’s laugh against his chest. Obviously, it was no match for the feeling he was trying to hold onto. Now, the heavy silence of the room felt like peace rather than emptiness.

Only gracing him with one moment before the heavy doors swung open, the sound then followed by a calculated tactical roll of combat boots and the rustling of a vest equipped with many things. His eyes remained fixed on his glass and didn't lift his head. “Careful you don't scare the locals. Your stealth's for shit.” The vacation he was promised was over before it even had a chance to start.

“It's a little early to be that deep in the bottle, Leon.” Of course it had to be Chris.

Leon sighed bracing himself before tuning back to look at them with the fakest smile he could manage. “Well, look who it is! The BSAA's golden boy and Dr. High Hopes!” he glared at Chris. “The hell do you want?”

Chris already looked sick of him. “I got a job. We need your help.”

That made him chuckle. Because what else was he useful for? “I'm on vacation.” he said casually, turning back to his glass.

Behind him, Rebecca walked and placed something down. “Let's talk about Los Iluminados. Remember the type of B.O.W.s they were using?”

“That's so long ago... I don't remember.” he shook his head slightly, no will in him to look at them.

Chris standing on his left cast a shadow on the amber liquid preventing it to glimmer like it did. “So, what? You just gonna sit around here another week and do nothing?”

Leon sighed at his words. “I never make plans that far ahead.” He looked up and turning toward the bar lady walking towards the cabinet. “Hey! Another bottle here.”

“Cancel that.” Chris cut in.

“Hey, who the hell do y—”

Chris slammed the table, making his glass shudder onto the wood and click into the bottle. “Enough! All right?!”

Leon turned to him sharply. “What. Do you want, Redfield?”

Chris’s face was absolute when he looked at him with his finger pointing at him. “What do you want, Leon?” Leon huffed, pulling out the flask form his back pocket maintaining a glare as he unscrewed the cap and brought it to his lips, only to be stopped by Chris’s firm grip on his wrist. “Cut the shit.”

“Guys, come on.” Rebecca spoke, her hands out as she looked in between the two of them with disappointment. “Leon,” She sat down on the wooden chair beside him. “I'm sorry to interrupt your vacation” glancing at the bottle on the table before turning back to him. “but we wouldn't be here if it wasn't serious. We think you might have some intel, and we need it. Now.” Great, just when I thought things were getting better.

He blinked at Rebecca then turned to Chris with a sigh, letting his hand fall on his thigh. “What intel?” he was too tired to argue about this anyway.

Chris circled the table behind them going to the table nearby, he followed Rebecca’s gaze, looking over to Chris placing a laptop on the table before taking a seat. “Leon you don't get it,” he said, while typing something. “This shit's spreading fast and thick.”

Leon held his head with his fingers, resting on his elbow in hopes of bracing himself what whatever bullshit they were about to say. “All of the researchers and staff at my university were infected.” Rebecca said.

bingo.

He sighed, letting his hand fall on the table in defeat. “This again, huh?” He watched Rebecca look at Chris. “It's like I'm stuck in a goddamn loop.”

“What?”

Rebecca leaned toward him properly. “What are you talking about?” This again.

“You know that bomb that went off in D.C.? Well, it was my mission to stop the terrorists.”

“We got to our rendezvous point... and some asshole ratted us out.” he sighed slipping into the memory of the explosion. “The bomb went off and everyone on Metro SWAT... my whole unit—gone.” his eyes fluttered close for a moment before he continued. “I keep fighting... and fighting, and fighting.” The memory of his units being reduced to numbers on a body bag coming back to him. “Instead of seeing an end to this shit, it just keeps getting worse —is this what my life's supposed to be?” He asked Chris, looking him dead in the in hopes he’d understand even a sliver of pain that was hung over him ever since. “Fighting the living dead and the bastards that make them?” he shook his head. “What's the point of it all?”

In response, all he got was being face to face with the laptop Chris turned to him. “Look. This is Glenn Arias, an arms dealer.” It was a picture of man in an expensive suit with white hair as he looked over his shoulder. “His deals on the black market are so shady they landed him on a certain government's hit list. A smart bomb was dropped on his wedding— he lost his family and his wife. And now he's got a grudge.” Leon sighed leaning back on his chair as Chris spoke. “After the attack, he went underground... but now he's back with B.O.W.s and a score to settle.”

Of course he does.

“Here's the kicker,” Chris continued, “he said his “products” have targeting capabilities. From what I've seen, I believe him.”

Leon looked down briefly. “So you got an arms dealer on one side... and on the other... a government dropping bombs on weddings.” He shook his head at the absurdity of the situation with a frown. “Who's the bad guy here?”

“Arias!” Chris raised his voice, pointing at him. “And it's our mission to bring his ass down!”

“Your mission! not mine!” He replied, point back toward him because this was ridiculous.

Chris slammed the table and stood up. “Goddamnit, Leon!

“Stop!” Rebecca cut in. “Leon. With everything that's going on, you just sit here and feel sorry for yourself.” she said then turned to Chris and pointed at him “And you? You're letting a grudge get the better of you.” She shook her head looking at the two of them. “God. I cannot believe how alike you two are.”

Leon glanced at Chris before turning back to her. “What are you talking about?”

Rebecca stood up, gesturing to them. “You're acting like a couple of selfish brats right now.” She walked to the bag she kept earlier, the zipper occupying the silence for a moment before she pulled out some Sci-Fi looking thing, Leon was about to ask what the hell that was but her glare said that she had enough of them. Rebecca rolled up her sleeve of her left arm and pressed the white gun looking thing, which took out a vial of blood with insane speed. Leon looked at Chris then back at Rebecca as she spoke. “The virus is right under our noses,” she turned to Chris. “Always has been.”

Chris shook his head slightly “I don't follow.”

“We don't need to look for some new virus that Arias is infecting people with.” She said. “The virus is lying dormant inside of everyone— even you.” she pointed right at him. “What we need to find is the trigger; something activates the latent virus and only Arias knows what it is. But until we find that out... everyone, living and dead, are his potential weapons.” She pointed to the glass window behind him. “And If we don't figure this out now... there won't be little towns like this for you to drink your sorrows away in... ever again.”

“I created a vaccine. And I tried it on myself and it works. But until we find out how Arias triggers the virus... who knows if it'll work the same for everyone else?” She pulled the vial out with a neat click. “My blood holds the answers— If I die, get this to a lab you trust.” she put the vial on the table with more force than Leon expected. “I can't imagine what you two have been through. But I know that neither one of you are the type of person... who would sit here and argue while the world dies.” she pointed to the outside. “That's not who you are.” Rebecca was looking right at him. “Or is it?”

The silence settled heavily on the room until the door shut behind Rebecca after she walked out. “Guess even Dr. high hopes has a limit to our bullshit”

Chris sighed, "Rebecca's right.” For a moment Chris and Leon just sat there, recovering from the brief lecture they had received.

So when the door opened again Leon wasn't expecting to the face of the man which led them there. “ Leon!”

He looked up to his right, taking a moment before he took in the man who was running in. “Patricio!” Leon lunged, holding him by the collar as he roughly pushed him against the wooden post.

“Wait, wait!” His grip on Patricio’s collar tightened as he spoke. “Let me explain!”

Leon turned to Chris. “You know that guy I was telling you about?” nudged his head toward Patricio. “The one that sold out me and my unit?”

“You don't understand—”

Patricio’s protest was cut off roughly by Leon’s forearm pushing right into his throat. “This is the guy.” His voice was raspy with anger.

Patricio struggled under the chokehold. “Please..stop—”

“Hey!” the bar lady called out somewhere behind him. “What do you think you’re—”

Chris cut her off. “Official business. It's okay.” Leon punched the traitor right in the abdomen. The audacity to be a mole and show up with your full chest expecting to be treated nicely? Yeah right. “Leon, come on!”

“What do you want?” Leon said with belligerence as he watched Patricio sink to the floor.

“Please.” Raising his hand in need of mercy as he struggled to speak. “You're the only one I know from the DSO.” he winced, holding his stomach. “It's my family—you've got to save them.”

“What?!” Leon feigned a lunge with a sharp snarl, startling a gasp out of Patricio as he cowered.

“My wife and my daughter are in Spain—an arms dealer is getting paid... by the surviving members of Los Iluminados…to launch a large-scale bioterror attack!” The cult's name rang with recognition the second time today. “I know too much.” Patricio shook his hand frantically. “They're going to kill me and my family!”

Just as Patricio finished his sentence, Chris grabbed him by the collar and lifts him to push him against the post. “Did you hear what he said? It's our lucky day.”

Leon’s gaze snapped to him. "Lucky"? You crazy?!”

“He's gonna lead us to our guy.” he nodded faintly.

Patricio tremored as he spoke: “the hell are you talking about?”

“The name Glenn Arias ring a bell?” Chris prompted.

Horror washed over Patricio’s face. “How do you know—”

“I'm psychic.”

Patricio turned to Leon with a pleading look. “I need your help! My family!”

Leon pushed Chris away by his arm and took his place in front of Patricio. “Let me get this straight: You try to get me killed... Now I'm supposed to save your family?!” His voice was rough with frustration as he held his collar. “Well, how about this! You tell us what you know first, and THEN we'll decide if we help,”

Patricio cowered looking to the right briefly as the loud rumbling of a vehicle approaching catches all of their attention. The truck rolls in from the right, halting just as three SWAT guys jump out with guns. “The hell?” Leon muttered as a giant stepped out. He didn’t even know what it was, all he knew was that that “guy” was holding a cannon of a gun pointing right at them.

Glass shatters as the bullets fly into the room, Leon hears the bar lady shriek as he crouched and shielded himself with a wooden table, he looked over his shoulder to where the woman was standing and saw nothing but a severed hand. Leon turned back toward the directions of the bullet, trying to get a look at who this maniac was.

Its a good thing Ada left before he dragged her into this mess.

“Son of a b*tch!” Chris cursed.

“He was followed—“ Leon added, looking at Patricio with fury, he was clutching his stomach as he sat in a pool of blood.

“My family…. Please—save them…” his hand goes limp.

Leon looked at the ground where Patricio's cracked phone laid, “Damn it.”

Chris stood up abruptly in the chaos looking at the truck. “Rebecca!” Leon followed Chris’ gaze just in time to see Rebecca being held over the shoulder by some woman in a wet suit.

The truck's engine roared into the distance. Chris was already moving, running through shattered furniture and broken glass toward the street, but they were gone.

He followed to get closer to the windows, carefully stepping to avoid the glass and debris, the shattered windows now letting cool air in as Chris worked his earpiece. “D.C.,” Chris said sharply. “They hit us at the hotel and took Rebecca. Track them. I know the big guy. He's with Arias.”

Leon stared at the empty road beyond the wreckage. This wasn't adding up. “What's Arias want with Rebecca?”

Chris shook his head. “I don't know.” His voice rough with frustration. “I just want to get her back.”

The phone in Leon’s hand began to ring, he gad almost forgot he was holding it. The screen was cracked from the fight. Patricio's phone: Dead man's final plea. When picked up, a woman's voice immediately came through the speaker.

“Hi, honey, it's me. Were you able to get the man you said would help? I'm so scared. Selena misses her daddy. Patricio?”

Leon closed his eyes briefly, knot forming in his throat as he couldn’t even get himself to look at Patricio's body.

The little girl's voice echoed through the speaker. “Can I talk to Papa?” She begged her mother.

Leon’s thumb hovered over the end call button.

The woman again. “Patricio? Who is this?”—“Can I talk to Papa? Please.”

There was nothing he could say.

“Where's Patricio? Put my husband on the phone right—”

The line disconnected as soon as he pressed the button. Silence that Leon forced himself to sit with. This wasn’t fair.

Chris looked at the phone. “There's a reason he gave that to you before he died.” Leon's grip tightened around it slightly as Chris spoke. “It might have the answers we need in it.” He nodded toward the hotel. “We have Rebecca's laptop. We have leads.” He sighed. “Look, man. I need you.”

A day ago, those words might have bounced right off him. Back to the comfortable numbness he'd nearly buried himself in before Ada flew to see him, the stubborn way she'd refused to let him disappear into himself. He thought of Rebecca, of the little girl's voice on the phone. He thought of everyone who was counting on them without even knowing it.

Leon exhaled slowly, slipping Patricio's phone into his jacket. “Alright,” he said. "Let's go get Rebecca."

Chapter End Notes

Hope you liked it! because the writing process for this was interesting. I had to watch the movie (in pieces) multiple times side by side to the script to see if there's any inconsistencies, also needed to take a mini math session to calculate the times of everything taking place so that was interesting. During my 'break' i actually worked on the rest of the chapters about how and where i wanted this to go, and i've planned something fun which is going to be interesting to write!

Chapter 8: "Go save New York, Leon."

Chapter Summary

💋 THANK YOU SO MUCH FOR 100 kudos ♥

Chapter Notes

hello!! hope you enjoy this chapter... it took longer than i expected oops... there should be one more part regarding vendetta and then we should be done!

During Leon's motorcycle scene, people love to say that he did that because 'Ada was watching' so i thought it would be fun if we actually had her react to it so this chapter is for that!! the next chapter has some interesting conversations that take place... so stay tuned!!

The carrier hummed beneath Leon's feet as he stared at the screen. "Bring up the map again."

The display showcased reports and data logs with a map of the United States. Red markers appeared across it specifically around the east. Chris stepped closer beside him, gesturing toward the screen. “She must have thought these outbreaks were related." He pointed. "See how they're all concentrated around the Great Lakes?"

Leon narrowed his eyes. The pattern became obvious once someone pointed it out. "If what she said is true..." His gaze remained fixed on the map. "...and this thing can lay dormant inside people..."

God knew how many people were infected already. Completely unaware they were carrying a weapon inside their own bodies.

"If the trigger's airborne," Leon continued quietly, "it's harder to control where it goes or who gets it."

Chris folded his arms. "So then Arias has to be using some type of vaccine."

Leon nodded. "Uh-huh." His mind was already moving ahead. "That's how he controls the spread of an outbreak. He vaccinates anyone he doesn't want infected first." He pointed toward the screen. "Which makes for a more accurate weapon…See?"

Chris looked at the screen pondering what They were both seeing.

Leon felt a familiar knot tightening in his chest. "Arias is planning something big." He reached into his pocket and pulled out the small data chip from Patricio's phone. The one that had cost a man his life. His thumb brushed across its edge. "Patricio knew way too much about it." The chip was small in between his fingers, too small to be significant enough to kill a man. "And that's why he was killed."

When Leon inserted the chip, the screen changed. Files appeared. Documents inside of it filled with reports. His eyes locked onto one specific section. Which gave him a moment where he forgot to breathe.

The dormant virus: Bottled. Distributed and Integrated into public water systems. Anyone who drank water. Everyone. Chris was already reading over his shoulder. "He's gonna use the trigger virus on New York?"

Leon stared at the screen blankly as Chris’s words processed in his mind. The answer was sitting right in front of him yet he still blinked, giving it a chance to go away. The truth didn’t budge: Arias had already infected the city. The trigger was just the final step. "That's what it looks like." Leon said carefully trying not to make sudden moves to reach for his phone.

Chris shook his head. "He's out of his mind."

Leon’s eyes moved across the text. "If the outbreak is deemed uncontrollable..." He read aloud. "...tanks labeled with a green A contain the vaccine and are ready to deploy. Location classified."

Chris looked up immediately. "All we have to do is find that stuff and we can change everyone back."

Leon nodded. "Like flipping a safety switch."

For the first time in several minutes there was something that resembled hope. "But if you were Arias..." He looked at Chris. "Where would you keep it?"

Chris didn't hesitate. "Someplace he can get to fast…. Someplace secure."

Leon felt his stomach drop as the answer arrived before either of them said it, His eyes flickered back to the map. "His hideout in New York."

New York. Back to Ada.

She had left Colorado this morning; The image of her walking towards the airport gates, scarf catching the wind. The way she'd looked at him before disappearing through those doors. You’ll see me soon enough. She was there now, amongst millions of infected people, unknowingly infected herself most likely.

"I bet we'll find Rebecca there too." He barely heard Chris.

“Excuse me,” Leon said suddenly, walking away from the laptop, reaching for his phone before he consciously decided to.

Four hours ago he'd been standing in an airport trying not to act like he hated watching her leave. Now every scenario his mind produced ended with the same thing.

Too late.

The moment the call started ringing, it immediately felt endless, with every second stretching longer than the last. "Leon?" Chris called after him. "What is it?"

Officially, there was no reason for Leon Kennedy to be making a personal phone call in the middle of a bioterror crisis. So instead he shook his head. "Nothing." He sighed. “Just give me 10—I’ll be back” He walked towards one of the quieter corners of the carrier. Chris gave him a look that clearly said he didn't believe that.

The line disconnected. Leon immediately pulled up their messages instead. A text from Ada. Thirty-seven minutes ago. Landed. "Fuck." Thirty-seven minutes ago meant she'd be in rush hour traffic, the thought itself made his hand tighten around the phone. The image of the outbreak map flashed through his mind again as he pressed the call button again. “Come on, Ada.”

Leon stared at the screen trying to ignore the feeling of his pulse as every second felt longer than it should. He immediately called again when it disconnected. His heart beating hard enough now that it was becoming difficult to think. Four hours ago he had been standing outside an airport trying not to look pathetic because she was leaving. Now he'd give anything just to hear her annoyed voice telling him he was overreacting.

The ringing continued.

Leon closed his eyes briefly, letting the mage of Colorado flash through his mind. Ada’s laugh that he caught against his chest, a warmth that filled his chest in a way that it hadn’t for the months before. He’d spent six months wondering if he'd ruined things between them, wondering whether she'd answer if he called. she'd flown across the country and sat with him through the worst night he'd had in years. Right now He wasn't supposed to be worrying about whether Ada Wong would survive the next six hours. And yet it was the only thing he could think about.

"Pick up," he muttered.

.

.

Click.

.

.

Leon straightened so fast his shoulder protested. "Ada?"

—

Ada was halfway through another phone call when Leon's name appeared on her second phone.

She glanced at it briefly. Leon. A faint smile pulled at the corner of her mouth before she looked away. The phone continued vibrating beside her. Ada returned her attention to the call she was already on. "No, move the funds through Zurich instead," she said calmly. "If they trace the Hong Kong account they'll start asking questions."

The taxi crawled through Manhattan traffic outside, into the familiar rhythm of New York.

Her burner phone stopped vibrating briefly before starting again almost immediately. Ada frowned slightly. Leon again. Interesting. She had to let it ring as the conversation she was having took another minute.

By the time she picked up there were 4 missed calls from Leon and another one incoming the second she cut the other line she was on and picked up his call. “Leon..." she said, lifting the phone to her ear. "You're persistent."

"Ada, where the hell are you?" She blinked at the rough tone of his voice. "Why weren't you picking up?"

Ada raised her eyebrows slightly. For a moment she actually pulled the phone away from her ear just to make sure she hadn't imagined it. She settled back against the taxi seat. "I was on another call. I have a life outside of you, you know." Normally that would've earned at least some kind of sarcastic response. Maybe a ‘it didn’t seem like it when you came over last night’ Instead she was met with silence, which was a bad sign. “Leon?”

“Where are you?” He said.

“In a taxi, heading to my apartment,” she said looking outside the window.

"How long?"

Now she sat up slightly. "Until I get home?"

"Yeah."

Ada checked outside where the traffic wasn't moving particularly fast. "I don't know. Twenty minutes maybe? There's traffic—"

"Can you walk?"

She frowned. "What?"

"Listen to me. How far?”

"It’s..." She glanced ahead toward the next intersection. "Walkable, yeah."

"Ada," he repeated, sounding like a man trying not to panic. “get the hell out of the taxi and go home immediately."

Oh. she pulled the phone from her ear and just looked at it for a second before deciding to hear him out. The order was so immediate, so unlike the careful way Leon usually spoke, that it completely derailed whatever sarcastic response she'd been preparing. "Leon, what is going on?"

"There's going to be a virus outbreak in New York."

Leon’s words caught her off guard."What?” she looked at the street with people crossing unsuspecting of what would happen. “Seriously?"

"The trigger is Airborne." His voice came out sharper than intended. "Ada, Do exactly as I say: go home immediately and close every window, lock every door.” she could hear him pace, heavy boots against the floor. “Find a room with no windows and stay there.” he said, “Do you have a mask?”

Her eyes furrowed “A mask—“

"Because if I'm right, you're already infected."

The taxi driver asking someone to move, all she could hear was the sound of her own heartbeat. "...What?"

"The virus can stay dormant," Leon said. "For weeks. Months. Maybe longer." His words came quickly now. "Anyone who drank contaminated water is potentially carrying it already."

Ada's hand tightened around her wallet. “It’s in the water?”

“Yeah. and if you breathe in the trigger you’ll turn. Do you understand?”

“Yes, I do.” she leaned forward slightly holding her phone away to speak to the driver, "Just drop me off here, please."

The driver looked at her through the rearview mirror like she'd lost her mind. "Here?" He gestured vaguely toward the street. "In the middle of traffic?" Ada was already reaching for her wallet. She pulled out a hundred-dollar bill and handed it forward. The driver's objections disappeared immediately. "...You know what? Fair enough."

The taxi barely had time to stop before she was out the door. “I'm out,”

“Good, I’ll be in New York soon enough too”

Ada slowed slightly. "You're coming here?"

"Well who's gonna to stop the psychopath trying to gas Manhattan?"

"I don't know," Ada said lightly. "Chris?" Silence after made her chuckle.

"...Chris." he said, deadpan. "Redfield?"

She smiled. "Yes, there's only one Chris I know."

"That's your pick?"

“I thought you’d be pleased,” she teased. "He's very capable."

He sighed on the line, she could picture him closing his eyes in disappointment. “I’m glad you find this funny,” Leon added.

“I do,” she admitted.

“…Chris.” he muttered, “that’s not even a fair comparison.”

“Mhm?” Ada pressed her lips together to keep from laughing. "Well, you'll call me when it's over."

A faint laugh escaped him. "Bossy."

Ada raised an eyebrow as she stepped around a cluster of pedestrians. "You're the one talking." She recalled the tone he'd used only moments ago. Do exactly as I say. Ada shook her head slightly. "...You're lucky I like you."

"Yeah?" His tone more optimistic than she intended her words to be received. Despite everything happening, despite the urgency in his voice and whatever disaster he was currently running toward, she could hear the faint smile.

Ada smiled too. "Don't let it go to your head, Leon."

"Too late." he said. “I’ll deal with you once this is over.”

Ada almost stopped walking. “Oh?” her eyebrows lifted slightly. “Is that a threat?”

On the other end, Leon seemed to realize what he’d just said. Because there was a brief silence—followed by a very controlled continuation, as if he was trying to patch over it with professionalism. “…I mean,” he added, “assuming the city doesn’t explode…—no. It’s not a threat.”

Ada smiled faintly as she slowed near her building entrance. “Sure sounds like one.”

He chuckled. “Trust me I know better,”

“Mhm. I agree, you're smarter than that.” the cool AC of the lobby greeted her as she walked towards the elevators. “Try not to die before you ‘deal’ with me.”

“Thanks. I’m working on it.” he replied. Ada pressed the button glancing up at the floor it was on. “Are you home yet?”

“I'm waiting for the elevator.”

“Okay good… I’ll call you once this is over. Just stay inside meanwhile.”

"Go save New York, Leon." the elevator doors opened, she stepped inside. "I'll be fine.”

“Okay…” he said softly. “Just be safe.”

“You too.” She said before the call ended. Ada stared at her phone for exactly two seconds before slipping it back into her coat pocket and sighed.

When she entered her apartment, everything was exactly where she'd left it before she left for Colorado. She set her bag down and immediately started checking windows. The curtains were already drawn, windows still locked. Realistically, she could help, find out where Leon was deployed, find her way into whatever operation the government was scrambling to assemble. She knew she could help. Probably better than most of the people currently being briefed. But if Leon is right, she’d turn the minute she’d take a breath.

By the time she finished, the apartment felt strangely sealed off from the rest of the world. Ada switched on the television and went through news channels, local broadcasts, national networks, pretty much anything that would cover the situation when it happens, but nothing. There were just weather reports and traffic updates. One anchor discussing restaurant openings in Brooklyn.

Absolutely nothing, which made the disconnect felt surreal.

As she waited for the city to catch up, she rummaged through her gear closet to find the P100 mask she knew she had. Ada returned to her couch with her bulky mask on. She folded her arms across her stomach and looked toward the TV. Her gaze drifted briefly toward her phone sitting on the coffee table, part of her wondering where Leon was now. A small smile threatened at the corner of her mouth when she remembered the way he'd looked at her at the airport. when will I see you again? At the time, when she had responded with ‘you'll see me soon enough,’ she hadn't intended it to be this soon, certainly not under these circumstances.

Outside, somewhere beyond the city skyline she could only imagine, she heard a rumbling of motor, what she would imagine is a helicopter or a aircraft carrier moving through the evening sky. Ada leaned back against the couch and kept her eyes on the TV. So it begins. A moment later, the burner phone she had set to contact Leon rang, vibrating on the coffee table. Except, it wasn't Leon. It could only be one person that has this number other than him. “Sherry?” Ada said, recognizing the number. “To what do I owe the pleasure?”

“Ada, yeah it’s me. Don’t worry this call is not being traced.” Sherry said. “I just wanted to let you know that I got Leon’s wellness retreat approved..”

“Mhm,” she said. “I’m aware.” Ada leaned back more comfortably against the sofa. She’d already already changed into something more comfortable.

“... He told you?” Sherry asked.

Ada smiled. “Yes, he did.”

“Oh.” Sherry hesitated. “Well… but—”

“Hm?”

Sherry took a pause that sounded like she was choosing her words very carefully now. “But… The BSAA needed Leon’s intel urgently for an outbreak in New York, so thats been forwarded to a couple days.”

Ada let out a soft laugh. “Oh, Sherry.”

“I—look,” Sherry rushed slightly. “I know you were… very specific about the retreat but—”

“Sherry,” Ada cut in gently.

The silence on Ada’s end immediately filled “I mean, it’s not like cancelled-cancelled, just postponed, it’s still technically in effect, it’s just—”

Ada cut in again. “Try not to sound so nervous when you say that, I’m not going to eat you alive.”

Sherry clearly did not know what to do with that information. “…So you’re okay with it?” she asked.

“Of course I am.” Ada’s smile lingered. “You sound nervous.”

“I am a little nervous.”

“Why?”

“Because last time we talked, you were very scary.”

Her words made Ada chuckle again. “You should have heard Leon twenty minutes ago.”

“Oh no.” Sherry immediately perked up. “Was he upset about the retreat being postponed?”

“No, no, he was just ordering me into my apartment.” Recalling it again made her roll her eyes.

“Honestly, it was ridiculous. ‘where the hell are you’ and ‘Get the hell out of the taxi’ ”

“Oh my God.” Sherry laughed. “Wait… you're in New York.”

“...Yes.” Ada sighed.

“No wonder he wasn't complaining.” her voice trailed off. Before Ada could ask, another voice broke through on Sherry's end. “The BSAA is requesting immediate backup for Redfield's unit. Do we have anything available on such short notice?” papers rustled faintly, “What happened?” Sherry asked. “They're requesting support in Manhattan.” Another voice answered: “Nearest unit won't arrive for at least two hours.”

Ada rolled her eyes. Two hours. Which in Leon's world basically translated to good luck.

Sherry sighed. “There has to be something we can do until backup arrives… maybe we can clear the roads and get vehicles into the area?” another muffled group of sounds “Do it.”

Ada listened to the exchange for another moment before speaking. “Send Leon a bike, it’ll get him there faster.”

Another voice on Sherry’s end cut in briefly—something about available units and route clearance. “A motorcycle.” she said carefully. “Are you sure about this?”

“The bike might not come back in one piece… just warning you.” Ada smiled faintly.

“…Okay, I guess we don’t have a choice” Sherry said. “Motorcycle it is.”

“I’ll leave you to it,” She replied, “Oh and Sherry? Thank you for getting it approved.”

“It’s not a problem, Leon deserves it. He's been through so much.”

“So have you.”

“...Yeah,” Sherry admitted after a moment. Ada could hear the small smile in her voice. “I guess we both have.”

“Maybe you should assign yourself a retreat too,” she teased making Sherry chuckle on the line.

“It does sound kind of nice.”

“Just, next time you sign someone up for mandatory recovery,” Ada said, tone almost amused, “try not to make it look like a punishment.”

Sherry made a small sound of relief. “Noted.”

—

For once, Ada was doing exactly what he'd asked. She sat on her couch, mint popped in her mouth as she was busying herself with work. The peace waiting came with lasted approximately two minutes before Leon's warning became reality. Ada looked up from her laptop when she felt the vibration, her apartment windows shifted in their frames accompanying a distant thud followed by muffled sounds of sirens and lots of shouting. "...Well."

Outside, the city’s sounds transformed almost instantly. She could hear NYPD officers below yelling something incoherently before shots were fired. With a sigh she turned on the news, that had finally caught up. Every channel was covering it in different locations. Breaking news banners over top drone footage. Ada tucked one leg beneath herself to get more comfortable because the mask was so annoying to wear she almost considered falling asleep to ignore it.

Another particularly loud blast finally made her look up again. "...What now?" Curiosity got the better of her so she crossed the apartment and carefully peeked through the curtains. Smoke rose several blocks away, far in the distance she could see movement of some animal, probably a dog. Ada narrowed her eyes when she saw a motorcycle’s rumble really loudly."...Leon?" She sighed when he turned the corner. "To think I escaped this mess." She closed the curtain again and returned to the couch.

The television was now broadcasting live drone footage following BSAA operations through Manhattan. Which unfortunately meant she had a front-row seat to Leon Kennedy being Leon Kennedy. Tearing through traffic on a freeway while Cerberus dogs chased him on the DSO issued Ducati. “Shouldn’t have gone this fancy…” Ada said, turning the volume up of the footage. The drone was struggling to keep up with the ordeal, especially when cars swerved and crashed, confused whether or not to report the crash.

Ada stared as Leon scratched another car in the middle of the lead away. “Today is a bad day to be on the road!” The news anchor sounded horrified. She watched the destruction unfold with the expression of someone observing a weather forecast because Leon only took down one out of three dogs yet.

On screen, a car flipped in the chaos. Leon somehow managed to destroy more vehicles than the dogs, at some point it looked like he had his foot on the handle.. “…You should have called your sister instead, Chris." On screen, another vehicle exploded. Ada nodded to herself. "Claire would've gotten the job done with significantly less property damage."

The drone camera struggled to keep up as Leon accelerated through another intersection.

Something exploded, the metal framing falling on top of one of the dogs. Ada wasn't even sure why that one exploded, because at this point it felt more like a personality trait. Which was a sharp contrast to the anchor who continued describing the situation with increasing concern.

As sunset washed over New York, nothing but time had changed, the city still had a biohazard outbreak, Leon was probably trying to find ways to use the motorcycle in tasks that didn’t need them. Ada sat on her couch well rested after giving into a nap earlier, the mask wasn’t any less irritating though. She’d done most of her work on the arm chair in her closet because that was the one place she could sit and have the privilege of breathing normally.

Now she regretted coming into the living room. Ada had been in enough cities during disasters to recognize the warning signs. New York, including her, had spent the last hour doing an impressive job pretending everything was normal.

That was until a deep, thunderous impact rolled through the city. Her windows rattled when the building suddenly lurched. The hanging light above her kitchen island swayed. "What the hell was that?" Ada abandoned the file she’d been putting off and crossed the apartment in seconds, trying to understand what she’d just felt beside the faint sense of unease settled in her stomach.

Two minutes later her phone erupted with the same tone from other apartments throughout the building.

EMERGENCY ALERT

SEEK SHELTER IMMEDIATELY.

AVOID WINDOWS.

FALLING DEBRIS REPORTED.

FOLLOW LOCAL EMERGENCY INSTRUCTIONS.

Ada stared at the message before slowly looking back toward the window. "...so not an earthquake.” This was considerably worse than she'd expected. Her thoughts immediately jumped to Leon. Whatever happened out there had his fingerprints all over it. “Back up should have arrived by now…” she said looking at the sky to see if she could spot any DSO issued choppers.

She grabbed her binoculars, and examined the skyline. Far in the distance, beyond the maze of buildings, she suddenly saw movement. A building that was standing up straight lost its light all at once and folding into another building. Her breath caught. "Leon... what on earth are you doing?" Even from this distance it looked impossible. The scale alone was difficult to comprehend.

Five minutes later, Ada was no longer pretending she wasn't worried. The helicopters were impossible to ignore as they thundered over the building one after another. News anchors were talking over one another, trying to piece together information as fast as it arrived.

The broadcast abruptly shifted not long after getting replaced with a live feed instead. Emergency vehicles with the BSAA logos alongside DSO convoys creating a background for the podium where Chris stood. “Sir Redfield, Is the outbreak confirmed to be contained?” the reporter asked.

Chris cleared his throat before speaking. “Yes, it’s contained.”

“Or we wouldn't be standing here,” Leon added. “That seems counterproductive.”

A few clicks of cameras. “Was there any indication of how many civilians were affected?”

Chris looked down briefly. “Too many.”

Another question followed immediately. “How bad was this outbreak compared to other ones we’ve seen worldwide in recent years?”

“Bad enough that I wouldn’t recommend sightseeing.” Leon coughed. “Excuse me.” She watched him step away from the podium and pull his phone out of his jacket pocket. A few seconds later her burner phone rang. Her eyes stayed on the broadcast where he had just exited frame.

"Honestly, Leon." She said, picking up. "You leave Colorado for eight hours and immediately start committing traffic violations on a city-wide scale."

“So, you’ve been watching me on news after all.”

“…Chris dragged you out there?” she asked at last, tone flatter than it needed to be.

On the other end, Leon made a sound of half agreement, like a shrug she could practically hear. “Mm-hm,” he said. “Did me a favor,” he added, cutting in gently, like he knew exactly what he was doing, “I got to show my motorcycle skills to you.” Ada rolled her eyes at that. Leon, on the other hand, sounded entirely too calm for a man who had just survived a city- wide disaster. “So,” he said, “you calling to yell at me, or—”

“Yes, actually.” Ada interrupted immediately. “I’m pretty sure you weren't entirely sober during the… I don't even know what you would call it.”

“People sober up pretty fast when you're stuck in an aircraft carrier with Chris Redfield for three hours.” She could hear the smugness in his voice when he said that.

Ada stood up, opening the curtains and the windows. “I lost count of how many crashes you caused to kill three dogs.”

Leon actually laughed, tiredness weighing it down to a huff. “Yeah well, good luck to them to explain that to insurance.” Despite everything, he sounded fine. And he was back to making jokes that don't particularly read the room. “Ada?” he asked. She hummed in response. “You’re fine yeah?” His voice softer than before. “We released the antidote over the city it should be fine to breath it in now.”

“Mh-hm. I did exactly what you said.” she mocked him. “Are you going to come ‘deal with me’ now or are you heading straight to D.C?”

“You’re not letting that go, are you?” he chuckled. “But to answer your question… I don’t have to head straight to D.C.”

“That sounds like you're making it sound optional.” Ada smiled faintly.

“It is optional.” She could hear voices in the background. Emergency crews and reporters distant discussions. Leon cleared his throat. “So.”

Ada immediately recognized that tone. The one he used when he was pretending not to ask for something. “So?” she repeated.

“Nothing…I should probably head to D.C. eventually.”

Ada rolled her eyes because she knew where he was getting at. “You sound like you're waiting for someone to stop you.”

“Maybe.” The answer came so quickly she almost laughed.

“Subtle.”

“Never claimed to be.” He said, making a small smile tug at her mouth. On the other end of the line, Leon seemed content just hearing her breathe for a second. Which told Ada more than anything he was actually saying.

“Come over.” She sighed glancing toward her apartment. “Before you disappear into D.C. paperwork.”

His grin was practically audible. “Paperwork is very important.” Trying very hard not to sound pleased. “I'm on my way.”

Ada shook her head. “You sound entirely too happy about that.”

“Well,” Leon said lightly, “I almost died today, it’s a good way to end off my day.” Then he added: “the no paperwork part I mean…”

”sure,” Ada responded accepting his terrible lie.

“See you soon,” his words landed strangely, the kind of thing people said when they expected to see each other.

Ada found herself smiling despite herself. “Drive carefully.”

“That's cold.” Despite trying to sound wounded all that came out was him being pleased with the situation.

“Goodbye, Leon.”

“See you soon, Ada.” he repeated. She shook her head and cut the call.

Chapter End Notes
