Chapter 9: "Long overdue."

Chapter Summary

“We stop pretending this is… nothing.”

one hour after the phone call.

.

.

.

Leon stood outside the door for longer than he cared to admit, which was saying something. He just refused to ring a doorbell. He shifted the pizza box in his hands, aware of how ridiculous it looked; peace offering if you will. His jacket felt too creased, it was torn in someplace. His face Yeah… He already knew what his face looked like. “Get it together,” he muttered under his breath, adjusting his collar.

Leon hesitated pressing the doorbell again, his hand hovering over the button instant regret when the bell actually rang. Too late now. His grip on the pizza tightened slightly as he shifted his weight, suddenly hyper aware of everything—his pulse, his breathing, the faint ache still lingering from earlier, the fact that he was about to see her again after everything that had just happened in Colorado. The door clicked. Leon straightened instinctively as it swung open; Ada stood in the door way, his brain didn’t process what he was seeing.

She was in a nightgown that caught the apartment light like silk, hair wet and messy in a way that made absolutely no sense in his head. “…You’re not injured, are you?” Ada said immediately, eyes scanning him once. Her gaze dropped. “…What’s that?”

Leon forgot the pizza in his hand. “…Hello to you too,” he managed finally, holding up the pizza box slightly like it might explain his entire existence. “Pizza.”

She leaned slightly on the doorframe, unimpressed. Her robe dropping slightly from her shoulder. “I can see that.”

Leon swallowed, suddenly very aware of everything again as she looked at the damages. Focus. “New York pizza,” he added, like that clarified anything.

Ada didn’t move. “Why? ” she asked, prompting him to speak.

Leon shifted his weight, he hadn’t, in fact, prepared a single coherent sentence for this moment. “Because,” he said, walking past her before his brain could sabotage him further. “you can’t come to New York and not have the pizza.” He glanced back over his shoulder. “It’s practically illegal.”

He heard the door close behind him followed by Ada’s voice, dry as ever: “…Is it?”

“Mm-hm.” Leon stopped a few steps in realising where he was standing. Ada’s apartment. It was quiet in a way that felt intentional, warm lighting that allowed you see the Manhattan skyline outside catching the movement of cars, people alike. A record playing somewhere low enough that it almost blended into the air itself, he only noticed it when he placed the pizza on the kitchen island carefully, aware of the fresh white roses that sat in a vase nearby, their scent faint beneath the aroma of the pizza he had brought. Leon stood there, unsure what to do with his hands, he shoved them in his pockets, “Besides,” he added, lighter now, “NYC pizza is to die for, no?”

Ada was still observing him. “You always look worse in person than on the news.”

That got him caught off guard. “Gee, thanks.” He watched her for a second before forcing himself to look elsewhere. Nothing about this place was temporary which was a realization that landed strangely to perceive, nothing about Ada seemed permanent ever, but being in her space was a threshold he didn’t remember being allowed to cross. It was exactly what he'd expected and somehow nothing like he'd imagined. Floor-to-ceiling windows with the type of view you see in movies.

He leaned lightly against the counter shifting awkwardly, eyes flicking over to the house again. Stairs curved upward to a second level beside the floor-to-ceiling windows. Of course it was a penthouse. "Nice place." he said. "It's got walls." Smooth, Kennedy. Real smooth.

Ada walked deeper into the place, glancing at him over her shoulder. "Grateful you didn’t blow them down during your detour,” She said, giving him a look. "Sit," The couch dipped slightly beneath his weight, he looked at Ada who was standing in front of him with her arms crossed. “Did you get checked up?”

He nodded. “…I just came from medical, I’m fine.”

Ada turned around, walking towards the kitchen again. She didn't look over her shoulder. “Do you want anything to drink?” reaching up to grab another plate from a shelf.

The silk robe she was wearing shifted slightly as she moved; causing him to immediately look out the window. “Im good, thanks” he said. After she returned with the plates, she settled beside him with one leg tucked beneath herself. Leon immediately became aware of how close she was as he opened the pizza box. The smell of it immediately filling the room. "Okay," Leon said, reaching for a slice. "Now this is therapy." Ada accepted a slice of her own, he felt her gaze on him as his cheek was full of a bite. “what?”

She smiled faintly. "It’s not worth causing a city-wide incident over,”

Leon furrowed his eyes. “You don’t like pizza?”

“Well, I wouldn’t say it's to die for.” she repeated his words, and when she looked at him there was an ache in his chest that started growing with warmth he couldn’t stop.

Leon forgot to answer because her smile was completely reorganizing his focus. It was just her, stripped with all armor, sitting beside him with her hair loose around her shoulders, a slice of pizza in hand, looking entirely too comfortable in a silk robe while jazz played softly in the background. Somewhere beneath the exhaustion and bruises and adrenaline that hadn't completely left his system yet, the reality of the sight being something he’d register as beautiful stuck with him. That wasn't even the right word. Beautiful was easy. He'd known that since he was twenty-one and stupid enough to develop a crush in the middle of an apocalypse because some bossy lady saved him from getting hit by the infected dogs.

This— was unfair. He'd spent years wondering what Ada did when she wasn't saving him or shooting at something. If she ever had nights where she just sat around doing absolutely nothing just like he had spent his nights in Colorado. Now as he was looking at her, in her apartment, eating pizza. His heart was losing its damn mind."Leon?"

He blinked. "Hm?"

"You've been staring at me."

"no?"

Ada raised an eyebrow, the corner of her mouth lifted into the more familiar smirk he was used to. "What?"

Leon shook his head and laughed softly to himself. "Nothing." She folded her arms and looked at him. "No, seriously." He rubbed his forehead with his free hand. "I'm just..." He looked at her again, and immediately regretted looking because somehow that smile was still there. "I'm trying to process this."

"This?"

"You."

Ada's expression softened just a fraction. "Me."

"Yeah." He gestured vaguely with his pizza. "You."

She tilted her head slightly. "You've known me for years."

"Not like this." Leon looked down at his slice before continuing quietly. "I know about the mission Ada. Mysterious Ada. Disappearing-for-six-years and maybe-even-more Ada. Holding-me-at gunpoint-and-then-saving-me Ada." He smiled faintly. "But this..." He glanced around her apartment. "It's new."

"And?"

"And…" He laughed under his breath. "I don't know. I think I expected you to live in a cave or something."

Ada's eyes widened and she laughed, "I was hoping you'd picture something more glamorous."

"I mean, I knew it'd be nice, but..." He looked around again before his eyes drifted back to her. "...I don't know." His voice lowered. "I guess I just never let myself think about…” Leon met her gaze. "This." Outside, Manhattan glittered beyond the windows. “Sitting in Ada Wong's apartment doing nothing in particular,” below, sirens echoed faintly.

Ada's eyes lowered briefly to the pizza box. "To be honest…" she said, picking at the crust in her hand. "...I don't think I expected anyone else to ever enter my house. Let alone sit with me on the couch,” She smiled faintly, not looking at him. "But somehow..." She glanced over at him, warmth hidden behind amusement. "The first guest I ever have is a government agent who brings New York pizza for dinner.”

Wait a minute… Leon stared at her. "The first?"

Ada took another bite of pizza, "Hm."

"The first first?" he asked.

She gave him a look. "How many definitions of first are there?"

"No, I mean—" He blinked. "Nobody?" She shook her head taking another bite. Leon looked around the apartment again. This was her home, that realization followed with somehow, being here feeling heavier. Suddenly having more weight and meaning to it. "Ada. You mean to tell me," he said carefully, "that after all these years, I'm the first guy you've brought home?"

She nearly choked on her pizza. "Oh, don't flatter yourself Leon,”

He grinned despite himself. "You said it, not me." His grin softened after a moment. "Seriously?"

She held his gaze. "Seriously." She'd trusted him with her space and with herself in it. Because she'd never looked like this before. Only here. The once place where the world couldn’t catch Ada Wong. Who currently was spending her free evening smiling at him over pizza. How was he supposed to just walk away from this? He'd pick doing nothing with Ada Wong over a beach vacation, every single time. Which was unfortunate, because tomorrow morning he'd be on a train back to D.C., spending three hours pretending he was excited to write a report no one would read properly, and then after that— One whole month; Sherry had said.

The word seriously kept echoing in his head like it didn’t quite belong in this room. Leon set his slice down. “I wasn’t fair to you,” he said suddenly. “I was an asshole in Central park” He let out a breath that sounded almost like a laugh but wasn’t. “Yet you still—” He stopped again, jaw flexing. “Flew to Colorado for me.” He sighed. “I didn’t even know why I was calling,” he continued. “I just… I couldn’t breathe. And then you picked up.” His fingers curled slightly against his knee. “You flew out there,” he said, looking at her now. “After everything I said to you when I basically pushed you out of my life.” Something flickered in Ada’s expression at his words then. “And you still came.” Leon shook his head slightly. “I didn’t deserve that.”

Ada set her pizza down carefully on the plate. “Leon,”

“No— I acted like I was doing the right thing. Like you were—“

“No,” she interrupted, still soft but firmer now. “Listen to me.” She held her gaze with steadiness she always had when everything around them was chaos. “You called me, because you needed me.” She said. "You don't owe me an apology for that."

“I shouldn’t have pushed you away in the first place,”

Ada looked at him for a long second. “I shouldn’t have let you believe I was dead for six years either.”

Leon frowned at the comparison. “That’s not remotely the same…”

“Well? It makes us even.” she shrugged.

“And what if I want things to go back to how they were before Central Park?”

Ada chuckled at him. “Yeah, okay.” She said, “that’s fine with me,”

“I’m serious,” he needed her to understand that this wasn’t him reaching for nostalgia or comfort—this was him trying to name something he didn’t have words for yet.

She let the silence sit for a moment. “We don’t break that easily Leon,” she stated it like it was a fact she had already tested over and over again to be true.

“…I still shouldn’t have said it,” he admitted.

“You say a lot of things when you’re trying to convince yourself you’re right,” she said.

That hit something quieter in him. He looked down briefly, “…I didn’t mean it,” he said.

“I know,” Ada replied immediately.

He let out a rough laugh, almost disbelieving as the knot in his chest relaxed. “…I’m still sorry,”

“And I’m done with you feeling guilty over something I’ve already forgiven you for.” Ada picked up her slice again. “Now eat your pizza.”

Leon chuckled. “Yeah okay,” .

.

.

For a while they simply ate as Manhattan continued recovering from disaster. The last slice disappeared eventually. Leon had done his best to prevent that. Unfortunately pizza, much like time, continued moving whether he wanted it to or not. He finished the final bite with the sort of reluctance usually reserved for paperwork.

Across from him, Ada was watching him the whole time, finally deciding to comment. "You've been eating that slice for fifteen minutes."

Leon swallowed. "It was the last slice, it had emotional significance."

Ada rolled her eyes. The joke earned a small smile from her. Leon, however, didn't feel like he could because for the first time all evening Leon became aware of the clock. Much later than he'd realized. Leon found himself looking around the apartment again well aware that the rest of his life was patiently waiting outside this apartment.

Eventually he cleared his throat. "...I should probably go." The words tasted wrong immediately. “I’ve got a report to write,” he muttered. A report no one will read properly but him.

Ada turned toward him. "Okay." Leon nodded but made no move to go. Her mouth twitched. "Leon. You haven't actually stood up."

"I know." Leon considered several answers. The honest one was somehow the worst. Because he didn't want to walk out the door and return to whatever undefined thing existed between them when they weren't sharing a couch. "Just … making sure we got our money's worth." He said looking at the empty pizza box on the coffee table.

Ada stared at him for a moment before she laughed at him. “By staring at the box?” The sound settled somewhere directly behind his ribs. A different version of the one he’d caught at the airport. Leon suddenly realized that leaving was going to be a lot harder than he'd expected.

“I’m not stalling.” Ada’s eyebrow lifted slightly. “I’m not,” he insisted, then immediately ruined it by staying seated. “I just…” he started, then stopped. “I hate this part,” he admitted.

Ada didn’t move. “Leaving?”

“Yeah.”

"How long until you leave for New Jersey?"

The smile faded slightly and he replaced it with a glare remembering Ada had something to do with the wellness retreat. "The day after tomorrow.."

Ada nodded. "Good."

Leon frowned. "Good?"

"You need a break." she said, closing the pizza box before picking it up.

“But,” he said, picking up the plates they’d used and following her into the kitchen, he stopped, his brain briefly caught up with what his body was doing. "I'm going to be stuck in New Jersey for a month."

Ada smiled faintly. "A tragic loss for the D.C."

He huffed a laugh at that. “Do you know how long that is?”

“Thirty days?”

He laughed looking at her. "Thank you, Ada."

She rolled her eyes. "It's just one month, Leon. It'll be good for you,”

He looked at her. “Yeah… you’re right.” Instead he just sighed. After the dishes were put away and the table was cleared, Leon technically didn’t have a reason to stay. He shoved his hands in his pockets and stood there in the kitchen while Ada rinsed her teacup. "...Well," he said.

"Mm." Ada dried her hands and nodded toward a small breakfast nook nestled against the floor-to-ceiling windows. A curved bench wrapped around the corner with cushions that somehow looked too comfortable for him to trust. "I'm making tea," she said. "You leaving?"

Leon followed her gaze to the bench before looking back at her with an accusatory look. "...That was a trap."

"It wasn't." The corner of her mouth lifted. “If you’re not, sit down,”

He was going to say something but he decided against it and sat down on the bench. By the time she joined him with two cups, he'd claimed the corner closest to the window, one arm draped over the backrest while Manhattan glittered below them. Somewhere in the distance, emergency lights still flashed. The city was bruised, recovering.

Ada settled beside him after turning off the lights, tucking one leg beneath herself, and handed him the cup. “It’s chamomile,” now the view of Manhattan was clearer than the sky.

Leon accepted the cup. “Smells nice,” The apartment had settled into that late-night quiet he'd only ever experienced in hotel rooms and safe houses. But here it felt more peaceful than lonely. After taking a sip, he huffed. “This is probably the type of stuff they’re planning on making me drink at the retreat.”

“Mh hm,”

Sitting here with Ada was one of those things that felt more normal than they were. He noticed her looking at him with an all too knowing smile. “What?”

“You’re thinking about something,”

Leon let out a slow breath. “Yeah well, of course I am.”

Ada smiled faintly over the rim of her cup, “go on”

Tomorrow he'd be on a train back to D.C. Then paperwork. Then New Jersey. Then— He frowned slightly. Then what? Because for the first time in years, he realized he'd been thinking beyond the next mission. “…I’m wondering what happens after I get back” Leon watched her face carefully, his fingers shifting around the warm cup. “I mean… after the retreat.”

“What do you mean?” Ada’s eyes lifted to his. “I assume you’ll be less insufferable,” she said lightly.

He chuckled. “…I’ll try.” His smile faded a bit when the thought of normality came again. Was it too selfish to want this forever? “I just…” He shook his head. “I'm asking because I don't know.”

“Don’t know what?”

His laugh came softly, almost embarrassed. "God, this is going to sound stupid."

Ada's expression remained calm as she sipped her tea. "When has that ever stopped you?"

He smiled. “Low blow” he said, gesturing between them. “I just can’t help but wonder how much longer I can take this, whatever this is between us. Before…”

Ada studied him for a second longer. “…Leon,” she said quietly shifting towards him while his hand settled on her back when she did. “I don’t think this works the way you want it to.”

He sighed, with a nod. “I know,” he said after another nod. “Trust me I know, that’s why it’s been bothering me.”

Ada’s gaze stayed steady. “It’s complicated,” she continued. “Isn’t it?”

He looked down at his tea. Then back at her. "Is that selfish?" He asked, earnestly. "Wanting you around?"

"No."

The answer came so easily. "No?" he repeated.

“No. but sitting here worrying about forever when you haven't even gotten through tomorrow's train ride isn't a smart choice."

He laughed softly. "Yeah… I know. It’s just that for years this was enough. Seeing you when I saw you. A phone call every now and then, wondering if you were alive." Leon hesitated. “But it doesn’t have to stay… like this.” Ada listened to Leon carefully as he spoke. “I can do the distance,” he said quietly. “…But I can’t keep doing this version of it without wanting more.” A faint, almost tired smile tugged at his mouth.

Ada shifted closer, and he felt her lean into the arm around her back like she belonged there. Her cheek pressed to his shoulder. “…After your retreat,” she said quietly, looking back up at him, "Come back to New York."

Leon didn’t move. “…Yeah?”

“We decide what this is.” His breath caught just slightly. Ada continued, carefully: “No… in- between. And no pretending it doesn’t matter when it does.”

Leon stared at her for a second. “…You’re serious.” He needed to make sure he wasn’t imagining it.

Ada gave a small, almost resigned smile. “I wont repeat myself,” Her version of yes.

Leon smiled despite the heaviness that loosened in his chest. “…Okay,” he said, tightening his hold around her waist slightly again; anchoring the moment to memory. “…So after New Jersey,” he said softly, “We stop pretending this is… nothing.”

“This was never ‘nothing’ Leon.” Ada’s gaze didn’t waver, she nodded briefly. “But yes.” she said. almost under her breath she added, “…and I’ll let you take me out on a date.” She said it casually.

Leon nodded once, setting down the half drank tea. “…Yeah,” he said again because his mind hadn't caught up to the words. His body was still in that warm, half-lazy state from holding her. When the word date actually processed, he blinked slowly in need of a full system reboot. After one month spent at wellness retreat. After all these years of distance and rules and “in-between” which curated the carefully undefined whatever-this-is— He would have to take Ada Wong out on a date. “…A date,” he repeated.

Ada hummed like it was obvious. “Isn’t that a good starting point?”

An actual first date. Leon’s grip on her waist subtly tightened. “…Oh,” he said. His eyes widened slightly as the realization fully took hold. Leon stared at her like she had just casually dropped a live grenade into his lap and called it romantic. “You—” He exhaled through his nose. “…I have to take you on a date.”

Ada tilted her head, watching him squirm. “What? You don't want that?”

“No!—I want that—” His voice came out too fast, trying to fix the damage before it fully registered. “I mean—yes,” he corrected, slower this time, exhaling through his nose. “Of course I want that.” His grip on her waist tightened slightly, “I just—” he started again, then stopped, dragging a hand down his face. “Okay, I have not thought since Racoon city.” His brain was now very much awake,Suddenly every single detail of this racked his mind at full force: Clothes, Timing, Location, Conversation, the fact that this was Ada, and the fact that this was supposed to be the first one. First of many, hopefully. Were flowers were too much?

“Since Raccoon city?”

Leon nodded once, genuinely offended with himself. “Twenty-one-year-old me had a plan.”

“A plan.” Ada smiled into her tea. “I need to hear this.”

He laughed softly, shaking his head. “Back in Raccoon City…” His smile grew fonder. “Before the god-awful sewers. Jesus—I remember thinking my plan went down the drain the minute I went down here.”

she smiled. “tell me,”

“It wasn't really a plan, it more of a… He looked away, embarrassed already. “'After all this is over, I'm going to ask her out for coffee.' ” Leon groaned into his hand. “Yeah, yeah, I know.”

“You practiced it, didn’t you?”

His ears immediately turned red. “No.” Leon sighed. “I just…” He laughed at himself. “God, I was so stupid. I kept trying to figure out how to ask without sounding desperate.”

“You had time for all that in the middle of Raccoon City?” Ada asked,

“I was quite the multitasker.” He looked away to his right, so Ada couldn't see him.

To his horror, he could hear the smile in her voice when she said: “That's adorable. You rehearsed asking me to coffee.”

“This is humiliating.” He said before he realised something and looked at her. “Wait a minute. This date is almost twenty years overdue.”

“Well?” Ada's smile softened. “Coffee sounds nice.”

Leon couldn't help but laugh at that. He shook his head, “Coffee is too simple. It'll be over in two minutes—The point is…” He groaned, rubbing his forehead. “Twenty years, Ada. Twenty years. I can't do coffee.”

“You’re exaggerating,” she smiled. “sixteen years doesn’t qualify as ‘almost’ twenty years,”

“It’s the same thing,” he said. “That’s not even the point,”

Ada rolled her eyes. “You just said it was”

“No, the point is, it has to be something else,” he shook his head. Can’t mess this up. “Well, you have a month to plan,” Ada reminded him, “but I don't think you should be focused on that when you're in New Jersey."

“I don't think I have a choice now.” he said quietly. “I used to think if I ever got this far, I'd know exactly what to do.”

Her smile softened. “And?” The woman who'd once kissed him goodbye more times than it was fair was sitting against him, talking about dates like they had all the time in the world.

“I don't,” he admitted softly. All he knew was: the version of him who'd been stupid enough to think there'd be an "after all this." somehow managed to end up right back where twenty- one-year-old him had started.

she leaned up, pressing a soft kiss to his cheek. “So don't rush it,” she murmured. “You still have a month.”

His hand lingered at her waist, trying to memorize where she fit against him. “…Yeah,” he said quietly, his voice didn’t quite match the agreement. “I should probably head out soon,” he admitted, though he didn’t move immediately.

The apartment was quiet behind him. Still smelling faintly of tea and pizza and something that didn’t belong in his life for very long but had somehow already started to feel like it did. Ada, who had long since settled against him, her cheek near his shoulder and practically tucked into the circle of his arm, shifted slightly at the words. He felt her pull back just enough to give him room to stand, making it easier for him. She was good at making things easier for him. Too good. But before she could get very far, Leon's arm tightened around her waist. Pulling her closer than before. Ada looked up at him, one eyebrow lifting slightly. “… hm?,” she said softly, almost surprised.

Leon wasn't entirely sure he'd meant to do it. He just knew that the second she moved away, every part of him had objected. The satin beneath his hand was impossibly smooth, cool and soft under his fingers, and somehow that only made things worse. She fit against him so naturally that letting go suddenly felt unreasonable. “Sorry,” he murmured, though he made absolutely no effort to loosen his hold. “I’m leaving.”

“You say that,” she observed quietly. “And yet. You’re making no move to leave.” She was right there, looking up at him, and he was painfully aware of how close she was. Of the warmth of her against him, that if he lowered his head even slightly— No. Absolutely not. “Then go,” she said gently.

“Working on it.” He didn’t want to comment on the fact his arm tightened just slightly again. “…I’ll call a cab,” he said, though he made no move to reach for his phone. “Actually…” He glanced toward the door, then back at her. “Will you show me where I could get one?”

Ada tilted her head. “You need me to show you where to find a cab?”

“Yeah,” he admitted softly. “Humor me.” He sat there looking at her, and she looked back at him with that same quiet patience she'd somehow mastered over the years. “This is your fault after all,” he said softly.

Ada hummed. “I wasn't aware I was responsible for your inability to leave.”

“You absolutely are.” His hand rose almost absentmindedly, brushing a loose strand of hair behind her ear.

“Mm.” Ada's eyes softened slightly. “Leon, you’re very obvious.”

“Mm?” He smiled. “Can't help it.”

“No,” she agreed quietly. “You can't.”

His thumb brushed lightly against her cheek. Before he could overthink it—Ada met him halfway. The kiss was warm, Leon felt her hand settle against his chest, and for a second, he forgot about everything else that was occupying his mind. “…I really don't want to go,” he admitted in the kiss.

Ada smiled, whispering back. “I can tell.”

He laughed softly against her. “This is your fault.” He kissed her again, slow enough he considered cancelling the train ticket.

His phone buzzed mid kiss, Ada laughed softly. "What is it?”

“Chris.” He pulled the phone out with a groan. Chris had texted him: where are you? Our train to D.C leaves at 5. “What?” Leon furrowed his brow, opening the attachment to the ticket that was sent to him earlier. The train's departure time glowed back at him.

AMTRAK E-TICKET BOARDING PASS

TRAIN: 2103 Acela

DEPARTS: 5:00 AM

FROM: New York, NY - Moynihan Train Hall (NYP).

TO: Washington, DC - Union Station (WAS)

ARRIVES: 7:45 AM

SERVICE: First Class

SEAT ASSIGNMENT: Car 1, Seat 3A

BOARD 15 MINUTES BEFORE DEPARTURE. TICKET VALID ONLY FOR THE

SPECIFIED TRAIN.

Ada leaned over slightly, Her eyes flicked to the screen then to the clock they were both looking at. 4:32 AM. “Leon.” She said, “You have to leave.”

He groaned dramatically and let his head fall onto her shoulder. “I hate that you're right.”

Ada stood up with all the gentleness in the world. “Come on.”

Leon looked up at her, she took his hand. He couldn’t even complain. “Cruel.”

“You have a train.” She tugged him up from the couch. He stood with a reluctant sigh, Before he could think better of it, his arms slipped naturally around her waist, drawing her close again. “Leon," she shrugged him off. “You’re seriously going to be late." He rested there for a moment, unwilling to let go, and couldn't help but smile at her no nonsense tone. “Go wear your shoes.”

“Bossy..” Leon sat on the bench by the door, bent over as he tied his shoes with far more concentration than the task required.

Ada stood in front of him with her arms crossed, watching quietly. “You’ve re-tied that one twice.”

He glanced up automatically. “No, I haven’t.”

Ada raised an eyebrow, then walked to the door and opened it, looking back at him. “Come on.”

He paused. “…You’re kicking me out?”

She didn't respond, instead she picked up the intercom. “Charles, please have a car ready for my guest heading to Penn Station… Yes…. thank you.” She hung up, turning to him as she wore a coat, taking her keys as she slipped on some short heels. “I’ll come down with you.

Leon stared at her for a second. “You called me cab.”

“Mh hm.” she nodded. “Before you stall even more.”

It was a compromise. And somehow, that made him smile. “…Yeah? Okay,” he said quietly. Down the elevator. Just that far… it was better than nothing. Ada walked out of the door with determination that made it seem like she was the one who was late, he straightened to follow her, the door closing behind him. The elevator doors in front of them, inevitable descent waiting.

.

.

.

When the elevator arrived, Leon stepped inside after her, leaning slightly against the rail, hands in his pockets, trying to look like a man who had fully regained control of his emotional state. He had not. “You could at least visit me before the yoga finally kills me,” he said.

Ada exhaled, rolling her eyes. “I’ll consider attending your memorial service.” she smirked. “You’ll have to schedule it around my meetings.”

Leon let out a soft huff of amusement, it didn’t fully hide the tiredness behind it. “Good to know.” His gaze lingered on her, something almost boyish, in a way that didn’t match the years on his face. Giddy, almost but in a careful way as though she was something he was already trying not to lose.

“You’re quiet,” she said lightly.

He looked like hadn’t realized he was being observed. “Yeah,” he admitted. “Just… thinking.” The elevator continued its slow descent. Leon’s hand settled lightly at her waist, she let him pull her into a hug, Ada felt him exhale slowly against her shoulder, some part of him finally relaxing after everything that had happened. Her hand rested on the back of his neck, half in his hair, similar to how she held him in Colorado. Ada could feel the small smile that appeared when she wrapped her arms around him in return. "There you are," he murmured softly.

Ada smiled faintly. "I've been here all evening."

"Mhmm." He sounded half asleep. "You smell nice." His hand absently rubbed her back, she chuckled.

She felt his shoulders relax further. “You need to get some rest on the train, once you're in D.C I doubt you’ll get the time.” The elevator hummed quietly beneath them.

Eventually, Leon nodded, chuckling softly. "You’re right" he pulled back enough that she could see the content look on his face. "Ada," he said quietly.

"Hm?"

"We really are okay, aren't we?"

Ada looked into his eyes, she couldn't help but smile at the question. "Yes, Leon." she said. “We have a date overdue.”

He laughed softly to himself. "Yeah," he whispered, his forehead nearly touching hers. “Long overdue.” His eyes dropped briefly to her lips Ada felt herself smile when she noticed. Another kiss followed shortly after. She felt his hand tighten ever so slightly against her waist as though anchoring himself, and she rested her hand against his cheek, feeling him smile faintly against her lips. His hand remained steady at her waist, his thumb brushing absentmindedly against her side as though reassuring himself she was really there. She felt the slight tremor in the breath he took between kisses, with a tenderness that made warmth settle quietly in her chest. For all the years between them, for all the times they'd met in chaos and parted with unfinished words, this felt impossibly simple. When they pulled back to her amusement, his cheeks had gone pink, helplessly smiling like a proud idiot. "You know," he said quietly, his voice rough with exhaustion and amusement, "twenty-one-year-old me would've passed out."

"Mm." Ada smiled, Her thumb brushed against the faint bruise along his jaw. "You seem conscious enough."

Leon nodded briefly, kissing her cheek, "Took him twenty years, but..." he murmured in her ear before placing another kiss on the curve of her jaw, followed by another under it. "He's having a pretty good night." Another one on her neck.

“Leon,” Ada said, her voice carrying a quiet warning trying to stay composed.

He paused immediately, still near her neck, and she could feel the faint exhale of a laugh there before he finally pulled back just enough to look at her. “…What?” he asked, entirely too innocent, little unfocused reeling in the lingering warmth of her mouth against his.

Ada’s eyes lingered on him before she sighed fondly. “We’re here,” she reminded him, eyes pointing toward the display.

“Before I go I wanted to ask,” he said, “Does this count as me winning you back, or is there paperwork involved?”

Ada stared at him in the purposeful silence before scoffing. “Don’t push your luck, you’ll miss your train.”

He smiled, raising his hands in mock surrender. Something about this moment felt familiar. “Worth a shot.” He said, as the elevator dinged. Ada exhaled softly through her nose, still watching him like she was deciding whether or not to let him get away with that expression. He looked… ridiculous. Hair still slightly disheveled from leaning in too close, mouth still carrying the ghost of her skin, eyes a little too bright for someone who had survived Manhattan collapsing only hours ago.

When doors opened, and Ada walked ahead without looking back, heels clicking faintly against the polished floor as she walked to the front desk. “You’re really going to walk me out in those?” he asked in half-awed exhaustion.

Ada didn’t slow. “I’ve done worse in higher heels,” she replied. “Charles, is the ride here?”

"Good morning, Ms. Wong," He said, "Yes, Your guest's transport is waiting at the curb."

"Thank you." Ada didn't break her stride, giving the man a smooth, effortless tilt of her head. Right before the doorman could reach for the handle to open the door, Leon caught her elbow, pulling her back just an inch.

“No goodbye kiss?” he asked, his voice dropping into a tired murmur, stubborn charm was still fighting through it.

Ada paused, raising an eyebrow as she glanced pointedly over his shoulder at the doorman, who had seamlessly turned his back to pull open the outer door, staring into the street with absolute, professional blindness. "Leon," she said, her voice smooth. "When did you become so shameless?"

"Charles is a professional, he’s not looking," Leon countered easily, stepping closer into her space completely unwilling to let the warmth of the last hour go just yet. "Come on. It’s a long train ride."

“Greedy.” She kissed his cheek, her hand sliding down his chest to give his jacket a firm, mock-stern pat. "Now go. Before I change my mind about that date."

Leon’s smile widened, his pink cheeks flaring just a bit brighter in the lobby's dim light. "Yes, ma'am."

Chapter 10: "I’m looking forward to it."

Chapter Summary

As he got into bed after changing his clothes and changing the dusty sheets, he ended up with his phone in his hand, absent-mindedly typing New York into the search bar. Apartments far outside anything he'd ever reasonably afford. He was just… looking, wasn’t he? So he kept scrolling… not because he was seriously considering moving. Obviously not. That would be ridiculous. He had a job in Washington. His entire life was here. His life … was it even his? The thought lingered unpleasantly because if he stripped away the assignments... the reports... the meetings... the apartment… What exactly was tying him here anymore? Work never really cared where he slept.

Chapter Notes

if you havent already noticed, i increased the total chapters to 15, which is what its going to be for now, that number will increase! believe it or not, this fic was supposed to be a one shot (by merging the first two chapters) but now it will be 15 chapters + i plan on writing pre - re:death island, during re:death island, and post re:death island. theres also something that i planned when it comes to the plot after which for now i titled as 'finale' but i havent decided when exactly to end the narrative (i would say the finale is actually like a narrative pivot which could be a realistic driving force for their relationship) ... sooo theres still room to grow! i am hoping for the requiem DLC to come out before i near the end of this fanfic so i can write with new source material and with new developed head canons, but, even if the DLC doesnt come out before that, i plan on returning to this fic when it does! im hoping to see Ada, like AT ALL because shes my favourite character <3 knowing capcom, anything that confirms them would be considered high expectations :') but as i promised. my fic wants to explore the relationship between them from the gap of re6 all the way to requiem, i do want to start dabbling in with the whole marriage aspect, however, i dont even know where to start when it comes to the ring, (although i dont believe they need a marriage certificate, they would pretty easily buy rings in this moment in my fic and it would line up what the director of Requiem said, 'place to go home to') only because i really want to tackle the whole mercenary x government agent thing and thats another whole ball game of research to begin to come up with canon accurate headcanons when it comes to the legality of their relationship, so for now i want to focus on their personalites and figure out the (As leon would say) boring paperwork parts, later!

Leon had only just made it time on the train. The city had been behind him for only a few minutes, but he could already feel it receding, the way New York always seemed to stay loud even after you left it. The train to D.C. was too quiet for his liking. Definitely way too long, especially because of the growing headache that settled after everything that had taken place. This was not how he thought the day would go at all. He thought he’d probably be still in Colorado, lazily packing for the “wellness” retreat, instead, he had ended up both destroying and saving Manhattan all to get a date with Ada Wong to look forward to. It wasn’t easy to pinpoint which part of this whole ordeal was the most shocking, but if he had to choose it would be the date.

Ada Wong was definitely to blame for the lack of sleep tonight. Not only did they stay up till four eating pizza and just talking, the rest of the time he spent thinking of times they kissed. He couldn’t help himself, it had been 6 months since he saw her, even longer since they kissed. Back then, staying away from Ada for 6 years was a piece of cake, something he did without much thought because that was what was “normal” between them, seeing each other only when their paths crossed, but ever since Leon asked Helena to drop him off so he could see Ada once again, everything changed. He chose to meet her, and that was dangerous because now, distance was getting less and less tolerable.

He snapped back from his thoughts and read the prompt on the report document more clearly. The pen tapped against the tray table with a kind of escalating irritation that was probably unbecoming of a federal agent and absolutely did not help anything. “Focus,” he said gruffly. Leon let out a slow breath and forced his attention back to the laptop balanced on the fold-out table in front of him. The cursor blinked expectantly beneath the heading, as if it had all the patience in the world while he had none.

Operation Summary.

There was something almost insulting about how every mission, no matter how catastrophic, eventually ended up compressed into sterile headings and neatly formatted paragraphs. Thousands of rounds fired. Entire city blocks evacuated. People lost. People saved. It all became bullet points for someone in an office who would never smell the smoke or hear the rotor blades or remember the exact moment a building began to collapse. Leon flexed his aching fingers before finally beginning to type. "Following confirmation of Glenn Arias' death..." He stopped after one sentence, rubbing both hands over his face before leaning back in the seat with a quiet groan. "I'm getting paid for this," he muttered to himself. "Not for thinking about her." Outside the window, New York had long disappeared behind stretches of forest, the lights becoming fewer the farther south the train carried him.... away from her... He caught himself halfway through the thought and sighed before resting his forehead briefly against the cool window. "You're in trouble, Kennedy." Leon forced himself to return to his work, ended up doing a mediocre summary of the encounter with Arias, the deployment of the trigger virus, Rebecca's work synthesizing the vaccine, and the coordinated response between the DSO and the BSAA. Deliberately leaving out the moments where success had depended more on luck than planning even though half of it was broadcasted live on the news.

By the time Leon finally shut the apartment door behind him, the empty silence greeted him before anything else did. It made him stand in the entryway for a moment without moving, keys still hanging loosely from his fingers taking in the apartment that hadn't changed at all. His boots from four months ago were still kicked carelessly beside the shoe rack exactly where he'd left them before Colorado. A jacket hung over the back of one of the dining chairs. Mail had collected neatly on the kitchen counter where his neighbor had occasionally slid it under the door. It looked less like a home and more like someone had pressed pause on his life. Leon let out a tired breath and shrugged off his jacket, tossing it onto the couch. "...Huh." Apparently even dust had more consistency than I do. He rubbed the back of his neck as he wandered toward the kitchen, filling a glass with water more out of habit than thirst. The apartment felt strangely unfamiliar despite every object being exactly where he expected it, though half of his exhausted brain expected to find Ada sitting on the couch with one of his books in her lap, or leaning against the kitchen counter making one of her dry remarks about how embarrassingly bachelor his apartment looked.

Instead, there was just the numbing hum of the refrigerator. Leon frowned slightly at himself because surely he had better things to do than compare his interior design skills with a woman who owns a penthouse in Manhattan. He glanced at the clock mounted above the TV, it was 8:03 AM, he had to be back at the DSO headquarters by noon, which meant four hours of sleep if he was lucky.

But as he got into bed after changing his clothes and changing the dusty sheets, he ended up with his phone in his hand, absent-mindedly typing New York into the search bar. Apartments far outside anything he'd ever reasonably afford. He was just… looking wasn’t he? So he kept scrolling… not because he was seriously considering moving. Obviously not. That would be ridiculous. He had a job in Washington. His entire life was here. His life … was it even his? The thought lingered unpleasantly because if he stripped away the assignments... the reports... the meetings... the apartment… What exactly was tying him here anymore? Work never really cared where he slept.

His phone buzzed in his hand, it was Ada, which thank god it was because it successfully distracted him from the listing. Ada : Reached D.C?

Leon smiled faintly to himself as he replied: yes, need to be at work by noon.

Ada: Did you get some sleep?

Leon: mostly wrote the report, I’ll sleep now. Why are you still awake?

Ada: I have work too. I’ll text you later, remember to keep an alarm or you’ll be late for work.

He stared at the screen for a moment, his thumb hovering over the keyboard as he reread her last text and chuckled because it sounded ridiculously domestic, making the apartment feel just a little less empty. Leon: Yes, ma'am.

Almost immediately, the typing indicator appeared and disappeared. For some reason, that amused him more than it should have. She's editing it. Ada: I won’t fly to Washington just to wake you up. Colorado was a one-time courtesy.

His smile grew. Leon: I wouldn’t dream of abusing my privileges.

Ada: Good. Get some sleep.

Leon: Good night to you too. Ada. He set the phone on the bedside table, turned onto his side, and pulled the blanket up without really thinking about it anymore.

-

At noon he was sitting in a conference room that smelled faintly like burnt coffee and printer toner, staring down a stack of paperwork that felt personally engineered to erase his will to live. Hunnigan was their lead for this debrief now who had left him to suffer with paperwork. A faint knock on the door interrupted the monotony, and Sherry stepped inside with a folder tucked under her arm. Leon straightened slightly without thinking. Something about seeing someone you’d watched grow up survive into adulthood made everything feel vaguely unreal.

Chris gave her a small nod. “Sherry.”

“Chris,” she replied easily, sliding into the seat beside Leon. “How’s it going?”

Chris shook his head, refusing to let the annoyance get to him. “Just put me on the field again I’ll be fine.”

Sherry smiled, “and is Claire still in Egypt?”

“Egypt?” Leon turned to Chris after the mention of Claire, it’s been forever since he’d heard about her.

“Something with her journalism stuff,” Chris replied, eyes still on the papers he was straightening. “Finally. I’m done,” he stood up, stretching his arms.

“There's more over there for you,” Hunnigan reminded from across the room. Chris’s grumble echoed slightly before he reluctantly sat down again in front of the new stack of papers.

Sherry leaned slightly toward Leon, lowering her voice just enough to avoid carrying. “Long night?” she asked quietly.

Leon let out a breath that was somewhere between a laugh and a groan. “You could say that.”

Sherry glanced at the paperwork in front of him. “Did you enjoy the bike?”

He paused, raising an eyebrow to look at her, tilting his head slightly toward her, voice lower. “That was you?”

“…Yeah,” she said at last, too casually. “I arranged it.”

Leon blinked. “Since when do you have access to DSO motor pools?”

Sherry gave him a look. “I have access to things you don’t want to know about.”

Chris, across the table, made a low sound of agreement without looking up from his notes. “You bet,”

Leon sighed. “Of course you do.”

Sherry’s expression softened slightly. “You needed it,” she said simply as if it wasn’t too thoughtful for how tired he already was.

He nodded once. “Thanks,” he said.

Sherry hesitated again before leaning a fraction closer, lowering her voice even further into a whisper. “Don’t mention it to anyone but…”

Leon frowned slightly. “but?”

Her eyes flicked briefly toward the glass wall of the conference room. “Let’s just say…” she paused, choosing her words carefully, “…it didn’t come from me.”

His fingers stopped moving on the paper. He put the pen down and slowly, he looked at her. “…Then who did it come from?”

It was now three in the afternoon, Leon had officially reached the point where paperwork became physically painful. The New York incident report had turned into three separate reports. Then there had been the debrief summary, supplemental statements, weapon discharge logs, casualty confirmations, and a request from another department asking him to clarify a sentence because apparently blown up by a motorcycle didn’t make sense. This was his last day in the office before the DSO officially removed him from active duty for a month and shipped him off to a beachfront wellness retreat in New Jersey—a sentence he still couldn't believe was attached to his personnel file. By tomorrow afternoon, someone else would be answering his calls and covering his workload because apparently yoga breathing exercises counted as therapy. Leon wasn't convinced the beach was supposed to fix whatever years of bioterrorism had done to his head. He stared blankly at the growing pile on the corner of his desk before letting his forehead fall into his palm. "...just a few more hours…" he muttered under his breath.

Around him, the DSO offices carried on, analysts hurried between departments carrying folders tucked beneath their arms, phones rang somewhere down the corridor, printers spat out another endless stream of forms waiting to be signed. Tomorrow, he wouldn't hear any of it, instead he’d be by a beach. Now, oddly enough, the part he was dreading wasn't the retreat itself. It was the resurfacing thought of Ada. whether or not calling her would be too much… He already missed her, and he hadn't even left yet. The realization was embarrassing enough that he immediately stood up. Coffee. He needed coffee before his own thoughts became any more ridiculous.

He pushed his chair back with a quiet sigh and stretched, feeling every hour he'd been awake settle into his shoulders. His neck cracked as he rolled it from side to side. The break room wasn't far, tucked around the corner from the main office floor. Leon walked there on autopilot, absently greeting a passing agent before pushing open the door. The familiar smell of burnt coffee greeted him immediately. Some things in government buildings apparently never changed. He grabbed a mug from the cabinet and filled it from the ancient coffee machine that sounded like it was fighting for its life with every cup it brewed. Steam curled lazily into the air as he leaned one shoulder against the counter, waiting.

When the machine finally stopped complaining, Leon picked up the mug and took a careful sip. Instant regret feeling bitter in his mouth. "...Still terrible." He wandered over to the window overlooking the employee parking lot, resting his hip against the counter as he stared outside. Government-issued SUVs came and went through the security gate below while agents hurried between buildings carrying briefcases and stacks of files. It should have been enough to occupy his attention away from the bitterness but instead, his mind wandered to the faint taste of chamomile. Why was he thinking about it now? He took another absent- minded sip of the coffee before the answer arrived. Ada’s apartment… the warmth of the mug she'd placed into his hands. "It's chamomile." At the time, he'd laughed quietly and joked that it was probably exactly what they planned on making him drink at the wellness retreat. His smile appeared at the memory before he even realized it. "Is that selfish?, wanting you around?" Leon closed his eyes for a brief second. "...I actually said that." He looked down into his coffee again, shaking his head with a quiet laugh. Yesterday morning he'd woken up in Colorado, with Ada who had flown out to see him. By the evening, he'd saved Manhattan, eaten pizza in Ada Wong's apartment, kissed her more than twice, and somehow left with plans to see her again

An actual date… he still wasn't entirely convinced that part had really happened.

He now recalled the faint taste of chamomile on her lips, warm from the tea they'd shared only moments before. At the time, he was distracted because of his impending departure, now standing alone in a break room with a mug of terrible coffee in his hand, that tiny detail was somehow the one his brain had decided to preserve with perfect clarity. Leon stared at the coffee in mild disbelief. "...Should've made chamomile," he murmured, smiling to himself as he took one final sip before deciding the coffee wasn't worth finishing after all. Carrying the mug over to the sink, he poured the rest down the drain and watched the dark liquid disappear.

The break room door swung shut quietly behind him as he stepped back into the fluorescent hum of D.S.O. headquarters. Leon returned to his desk, dropping into the chair with a sigh that made the springs protest. The report he'd abandoned earlier still occupied his monitor, he stared at the blinking cursor over the paragraph he'd written. "...Right," he muttered.

His fingers settled onto the keyboard and managed exactly three sentences before his phone vibrated against the desk. Instinctively, he looked down and a smile appeared before he'd even unlocked it. Ada. He opened the message. It was a photo of Ada dressed for work, black leather skirt that matched her boots and black tights. She wore a ribbed turtleneck top which had something on her usual harness that caught his eye, clipped securely to one of the straps was his Swiss Army knife. Ada: it matches.

He let out a quiet laugh. Unbelievable. Leon: That's evidence of theft.

Ada: Is it?

Leon: It's government property.

Ada: You left it on my couch. Her text was accompanied by another photo. The knife clipped neatly against the side of her chest, polished clean enough that it almost looked like it was brand new. Ada: Doesn't look forgotten anymore, does it?

He laughed quietly, earning a curious glance from one of the analysts walking past his desk. Leon cleared his throat, suddenly remembering he was, in fact, sitting in a government office. Leon: if you say so :)

Ada: If you have a problem, don't leave your things with me.

Leon stared at the message as his smile faded just enough for uncertainty to creep in. "...is she...?" He reread it. If you have a problem… "...Did I actually annoy her?" He rubbed the back of his neck with the realization it was impossible to tell over text. Ada had always been difficult to read in person, through a text? it was practically impossible. Leon:I wasn't actually complaining. no... Leon:You can keep it. that's not right either.... Leon: Sorry. Deleted all three different drafts. "...No." He frowned at his phone. "...I'm overthinking this." Regardless he slipped his phone into his pocket and wandered toward the empty hallway near the stairwell under the excuse of stretching his legs, and called her instead. “Ada?”

“Leon…?” She sounded almost surprised.

"Hey." He leaned against the wall, rubbing the back of his neck. "...Is everything alright?"

“Yes..?” She said carefully. "...Are you alright?" Ada was quiet for a second. "What happened?"

He looked down at the floor, shifting his weight. "...Nothing happened.... just..." He sighed. "...Are you mad at me?"

She went quiet in the line: "...What?"

He cleared his throat. "The knife."

"...The knife?”

"You said..." He glanced at his phone, reading the message back to himself. “ 'If you have a problem, don't leave your things with me.' So I thought maybe..." he trailed off. "...You were." Ada laughed softly, it was completely unguarded that made him close his eyes in mild embarrassment.

"...You thought I was upset."

Leon scratched the back of his neck again. "...Maybe."

"...Were you worried I'd call off the date?"

he hesitated. "...I mean.... It crossed my mind..”

Ada sighed softly, he could hear her open some door. "...Leon." Silence lingered for a second longer than usual, like she was choosing her words more carefully than she wanted to admit. "I’m looking forward to it."

He blinked, not expecting her to say that. "...Yeah?"

"...Too much," she added, quieter, like she almost didn’t want him to hear that part. "I'd still go on the date," she said, like it was the simplest fact in the world, "even if I were mad at you."

He shook his head, the image of a upset Ada Wong sitting across him, arms crossed wearing a glare. "...That's terrifying."

Ada spoke again, her voice carrying that familiar hint of amusement. "I was just teasing you Leon,” She laughed again, fonder this time. "I'll try to be more obvious next time,”

He snorted at that. "Please don't."

She chuckled, more amused now. "No?"

"I kind of like having an excuse to call you." He froze because the words left his mouth before he'd had a chance to stop them.

On the other end of the line, Ada was quiet... quietly smiling, if he had to guess. "So, I see” she said after a moment, "this was all an elaborate excuse?"

Leon groaned, dropping his forehead lightly against the wall. "...Can we pretend I didn't say that?"

“Anything else?”

he rubbed the back of his neck, smiling. “Well? You looked nice,”

“You didn’t even see me”

"That's- you know what? fair." he gave up. “I would be lying if I said I don’t want to see you” Leon added, suddenly aware of how quiet the hallway had become. "...That sounded worse out loud," he muttered immediately, almost to himself.

On the other end, Ada didn’t respond right away. He could practically feel her processing it. "...Define ‘see me,’" she said, tone even. “you require visual confirmation that I exist?”

He snorted. "That’s not what I said." His phone buzzed right in his hand, making him glance down instinctively, still on the call. “…Hold on,” he murmured without thinking, already unlocking the screen. It was a selfie. Of Ada. Soft lighting caught her face in a way that made everything feel quieter than it should have been. A few strands of hair were slightly out of place, like she hadn’t bothered fixing them before sending it. Her expression wasn’t smiling exactly—but there was amusement in her eyes that would occupy his mind for the next few hours of paperwork.

“Happy?” Ada said over the phone, which snapped his gaze away from the picture that he’d been staring at longer than he meant to. Leon let out a short, disbelieving laugh under his breath, cheeks warming. “…What,” she said, already knowing something had happened.

He swallowed, still looking at the image. “Yeah… Ada?” He sighed. "I was thinking…I've got a month in New Jersey."

He could hear her smirk over the line. "Mh hm, I remember..”

"And..." He searched for the words. "It's probably going to get... quiet." He laughed awkwardly. “So.. if you’re somewhere interesting or even doing something interesting…” he trailed off of that thought shaking his head. “…forget it.”

"No, tell me,”

"I just..." He looked down at the floor. "...Could you send me pictures?" He hurried to explain. "Not because I want updates on your missions or anything—“ he stopped himself. “So I know you’re… around?”

“I see,” she said, he could really imagine her coming up with a round anyway to tease him again. “but in return,”

He smiled. "...Yeah?"

"I expect updates on your wellness journey."

Leon groaned dramatically. ”I haven’t even started”

“I know,” she chuckled. “But I can picture it being very entertaining to receive.”

“And it would be nice to know what you're up to." He added, "it’ll make New Jersey a little less… quiet."

Ada laughed softly, the sound itself didn’t compare to how it sounded when her cheek was pressed to his shoulder. “Don’t worry Leon, I’ll keep you entertained… for the most part.” He could picture a wink paired with that response.

Leon let out a quiet breath through his nose, still leaning against the stairwell wall, staring out through the glass like the rest of the office wasn’t real for a second. “…That sounds vaguely threatening,” he said.

“That's how I intended it to be received."

He huffed a small laugh. “…Yeah. Figures.”

There was a brief pause, breaking her tease voice from whatever she was about to say. “…I’ll call you.”

Leon blinked slightly, shifting his weight. “…Yeah?”

“Mhm… probably in the evenings,” she added, casual again, like she was talking about scheduling a meeting. “Assuming you’re not asleep.”

He smiled a little before he could stop it, there was no way sleep would find him before Ada’s phone call did. “I’ll try not to be.”

“You say that now.” she said, "You'll have your phone right?"

"I think they only take it during the therapy sessions… I should have it at least to take photos and at night.”

“Well then, I’ll call you before bed.”

"...Okay," he said, voice quieter now. "I’ll be waiting."

“When are you heading out?”

“In a couple hours or so… still have paperwork to do”

"Don’t fall asleep at your desk by overworking yourself.” she added, making him smirk despite his best efforts to not let it get to his head.

“You worried of me Ada?” He gave a quiet, tired laugh. Something about the way she'd asked when he was leaving, reminded him to keep an alarm, and now was making sure he didn't work himself into the ground... though she'd managed to wrap concern in enough sarcasm that she could deny it if challenged, but it was still concern all the same. Especially as Leon found himself smiling at the stairwell window like an idiot. “…You know,” he said, unable to resist, “you’re getting awfully domestic.”

“Isn’t that where we’re headed? Domesticity?"

Leon found himself go quiet at her words, staring at the window in front of him, his own reflection looking just as stunned as he felt. “…You can’t just say things like that,” he said at last, a laugh escaping him before he could stop it. “I have reports to write.”

“And? I fail to see how those are related.” she huffed, he could picture her rolling her eyes, all sassy.

“They're very related,” Leon insisted, though he couldn't have explained why if she asked him to. “You say something like that and suddenly I'm supposed to go back to filling out incident reports?”

Ada's laugh was soft, warm enough that he found himself smiling even wider. “I have complete confidence in your ability to multitask.”

He let his head rest lightly against the cool concrete wall, closing his eyes for a second. Domesticity. It wasn't a word he'd never seriously imagined applying to the two of them. “You’re killing me, Ada.”

“I feel no remorse, Leon” her voice was calm, even as she dismantled his focus long ago. “Now,” Ada said, slipping effortlessly back into her composed tone, “go finish your reports before Hunnigan thinks you escaped.”

Leon let out a quiet laugh under his breath, shaking his head as he pushed himself off the wall. “Yeah, yeah,” he muttered, opening the stairwell door slightly, phone still to his ear. “I’m going.”

Big mistake. The first person he made eye contact with was Sherry, seated at her desk flipping through something on her tablet—until she looked up. A fraction of a second passed where neither of them moved, except her expression that shifted into something very specific: the look of someone who knew exactly what was going on but was choosing not to say it out loud yet.

Leon immediately looked away, clearing his throat as he walked past like nothing in the world was unusual at all. “…I’ll call you later,” he said into the phone, voice slightly lower now.

“I’m sure you will.” On the other end, Ada sounded faintly amused. “Don't keep me waiting for that date.”

Leon slowed for half a step without meaning to, he couldn't even respond, so he just exhaled through his nose, trying very hard not to smile in front of an entire federal office. it almost hurt. “...Wouldn't dream of it.” He said softly, hanging up before Ada said something else that hooked out a reaction. When he slipped the phone away, Sherry was still watching him… definitely suspicious.

Chapter 11: "...She's the person I think about first."

Chapter Summary

“Huh?” Leon said, squinting his eyes open. Looking at the masseur and then back the lady holding a tray of what looked like… needles. "You're putting how many needles in me?" He looked at the tray with suspicion, laying properly on his back now.

With a faint click Leon’s next month of his life starts. The dreadful wellness retreat. He stood in the doorway, door shut behind him, holding a duffel bag and a pamphlet that had tomorrow’s schedule set for him. The smell of some sort of essential oil—probably tea tree, not that he knew the difference anyway—hit him.

It looked basically like a hotel room. Spacious, airy, big wooden windows closed politely with linen curtains. He dropped his duffel bag and put the pamphlet on the desk as he passed by to let some air inside. The soft crashes of waves instantly followed once he opened the windows. “Beach view? Not bad, Ada.” He inspected the room further now that the windows were open. The desk he passed by had a note book on it that he actively chose to ignore the moment his brain registered the word : 'journaling' because apparently there was something else in the world that was worse than paperwork.

He sighed, opening the wooden wardrobe which had robes in different shades of light green… and a couple Hawaiian shirts? Seriously? “I’m not wearing that.” Leon took one of the robes and changed into it in the bathroom because of course this place would have a sun roof with plants and a fake waterfall surrounding a tub because vitamin D is important.

Never in his life did he have time for a bubble bath… Who even spends that much time in the shower? “I guess nothing around here is done because it’s efficient..” Leon ended up laying on the bed like a star fish after he finished taking a shower in the way he was used to, at least they had one of those. Because he couldn’t imagine needing to get in a tub and sitting and doing nothing for half an hour every time he needed to feel clean. The bed was nice. Clean sheets, soft, maybe even too clean, maybe even boring. Before he knew it, he was already reaching for his phone, opening Ada’s messages.

Leon: already don’t like it here.

Ada: made it?

Leon: Unfortunately.

Ada: thank me later.

Leon: so you’re owning up to it?

Ada: Sherry only booked the best suite on the coast. Don't be ungrateful.

Leon: I’m not..

When Leon opened the picture an automatic smile curved his face. It was a picture of the Ada pointing in the direction of the Jersey coastline past the Statue of Liberty and the distant skyscrapers of lower Manhattan. Knowing she was only a harbor crossing away somehow made the ocean feel less lonely.

Ada: Stop brooding. I'm looking right at you.

Leon: blow me a kiss then

Leon: ;)

Ada: I would, but with the wind speed over the harbor tonight, it would probably ruin your first outdoor yoga session tomorrow if you don’t catch it.

Leon: I'll take my chances.

Ada: Catch it yet?

Leon: Just did. Leon: Suddenly this retreat isn't so bad.

Ada: Consider it a reward for actually checking in.

Leon: I think it’s starting… I just heard a knock, someone's at my door

The staff member he held the bowl that looked like a coconut, out for him to take. “This a gift for our guests to start their relaxation with the right step.”

Leon found himself wake up drooling into the face cradle because he's apparently been carrying twenty years' worth of muscle tension. "...You had a lot of knots, sir." The room was so quiet he could hear the waves rolling onto the beach outside.

He looked back at the masseur, still half asleep when he nodded. "...Yeah. that’s sounds about right."

"...Mostly your shoulders." he added.

Leon huffed into the fabric of the face cradle."... blame the government for that..”

“We will be starting your acupuncture session now. I need you to lay on your back sir.” A lady said. “We will start with seven for now.”

“Huh?” Leon said, squinting his eyes open. Looking at the masseur and then back the lady holding a tray of what looked like… needles. "You're putting how many needles in me?" He looked at the tray with suspicion, laying properly on his back now.

The lady smiled, unfazed about anything. “Don't worry, they are thinner than a strand of your hair.”

“That sounds made up…” he mumbled. He instinctively tightened his core when he felt her cold gloves touch his upper abdomen, making him feel incredibly exposed. why did this suddenly feel… "...Weird," he admitted under his breath.

The acupuncturist glanced up from her tray. "A lot of people feel vulnerable during their first session."

"...Yeah," Leon answered automatically even though that's not really what he meant anyway.

"Take a breath, sir. If you keep your core this flexed, the needles won't go in smoothly. I need you to completely relax."

Leon swallowed hard, staring intently at the ceiling. "Easier said than done." It was the strange intimacy of having someone he barely knew standing this close, fingertips lightly resting against the edge of his ribs while searching for the correct point. Under normal circumstances, it shouldn't have bothered him… but now his mind wandered somewhere else entirely. Without warning, he remembered another pair of hands, painfully aware that the hands touching him now were nothing like hers. Colorado was barely a couple days ago. He'd been sitting against the headboard, too exhausted to keep pretending he was alright. He remembered instinctively resisting when Ada had reached for him—as if some stubborn part of him still thought he needed to hold himself together. With that memory he could vividly picture the gentle weight of her hands warm against his cheek, as she brushed away the tear he'd never intended anyone to see. It was too fresh of a memory to recall. The warmth of her fingers combing quietly through his hair, the slow circles she'd traced against his back after he'd leaned against her shoulder without thinking. Leon let out the smallest breath through his nose when he realized there were three needles were very much in him. "...oh."

The acupuncturist looked up. "Sir?"

He blinked, pulled abruptly back into the room. "Sorry."

She smiled reassuringly. "Just breathe, you were relaxing just fine right now."

"...Working on it." He tried again, forcing the tension out of his abdomen. The memory of that night in Colorado came back to him. "…Don't go yet." God. He'd actually said that. If only he knew at the time how much would change.

"There," the acupuncturist said softly. "That's much better."

Leon looked up at the ceiling again, thinking about why he'd relaxed so suddenly. "...Interesting," he murmured. "I was mentally preparing to survive medieval torture."

The acupuncturist laughed softly. "I'll try not to take that personally." She finished adjusting the final needle before stepping back to observe him. "There we go."

Leon looked down as much as he could without disturbing anything. Seven tiny handles protruded from various points across his torso and shoulders. "...I look ridiculous." He reached for his phone on the small side table before stopping himself. "...Actually...Would you mind doing me a favor?"

"Of course."

He held his phone out carefully. "...Could you take a picture?"

Her eyebrows lifted ever so slightly. "You'd like a photo?"

He cleared his throat, already feeling slightly embarrassed. "...Someone's expecting updates." A knowing smile crossed her face. "I'd be happy to." She accepted the phone, stepping back.

Leon looked down at himself."...Should I..." and with absolutely no dignity left to preserve, raised one hand and gave the camera a reluctant thumbs-up.

The therapist couldn't help laughing. "Perfect." Once she handed the phone back, Leon glanced through the photos. He laughed quietly to himself. Simply staring at the photo, shaking his head with a smile. Seven acupuncture needles paired with a crooked smile and thumbs-up.

Leon: (sent a photo)

👍 Leon: Therapy update: - turns out I'm part porcupine now.

Leon was still looking at the message thread when the acupuncturist gently cleared her throat. “Alright, Mr. Kennedy. You’ll need to put the phone aside now so your body can fully relax.”

“…Right.” He huffed softly through his nose putting the phone back. All that he had left to do was stare at the ceiling, thinking of what Ada' reaction would be, whether she would laugh at the porcupine comparison or smile at how much of an idiot he looked... regardless, he wouldn't be there to see it. “…she’s going to enjoy that one,” he murmured to no one in particular.

“Your wife?”

Leon’s eyes widened slightly, way too relaxed to get evidently flustered. “…Wife,” he repeated under his breath, almost absent-mindedly. He couldn't help but chuckle. “I wish.”
