.

Now, Leon was all curled up under the sheets phone in hand, waiting for his promised phone call from Ada. his room’s lights were automatically dimmed to promote sleep, and apparently that rule was followed everywhere because even his view of the retreat outside his balcony windows were now sparse candles placed at doors, everything else was lit by the moon and its shine only.

Leon lay on his side, absentmindedly turning his phone over in his hands before checking the time again. 7:58. "...Two minutes," he murmured. Even though he told himself he wasn't counting, he caught himself checking anyway, with a stupid smile. 7:59. "...Relax." He'd spent the afternoon having professionals repeatedly tell him to unclench his shoulders and regulate his breathing, only for his heart to inexplicably pick up because someone had promised to call him. “Pathetic..” he said dropping the burner phone beside him to stare at the ceiling. The vibration against the mattress startled him enough that he almost flinched. A smile spread across his face before he'd even looked at the screen. Ada. "...Hey."

There was the faint rustle of movement on the other end before Ada's voice reached him, “hello, you sound awake.” he could hear the smile in her voice.

"I've been awake," he replied.

"Waiting?"

Leon looked toward the ceiling, already smiling. "...Maybe."

"I thought so." A soft hum came through the speaker. “How's the first day going? I received your porcupine report.”

He laughed quietly. “Right. That.. I—“

“…Hold on,” she cut in. “I’ll call you back.”

Leon blinked. “…Hold on?” The line went dead. He pulled the phone away from his ear, staring at the screen like it might explain itself. “…Okay,” he muttered.

An entire minute passed and Leon decided listening to the waves had gone boring. After ten minutes Leon became typing a message. His phone rang from the desk. Not his burner, his personal phone that he used to take the photos for Ada. Grabbing it off the desk he read the caller id. Ada. W Incoming video call. "...What?" He actually laughed. "No way."

The screen shifted to Ada's softly lit kitchen. The phone was propped up on the island facing the frame to her back as she stood at the counter, sleeves rolled neatly to her forearms. "...There," she said. "That's better."

Leon just stared. "...What is happening?" He muttered to himself looking at Ada, she wore one of her dark silk nightgowns beneath a neatly tied apron. "...You're cooking."

Ada reached for a wooden spoon without turning around. "So it would seem."

He looked at himself in the small corner, only to note that his cheeks were already beginning to betray him. "...I wasn't prepared for this." Angling the phone away from his face.

She looked over her shoulder. "For dinner?"

"For..." He gestured vaguely at the screen before realizing she couldn't see the motion very well. "...Whatever this is." The retreat had somehow turned him into the human equivalent of a freshly bathed golden retriever. He immediately tried pushing his hair back. "...I look ridiculous."

Ada glanced over one shoulder with a soft smile. "I was going to say well-rested, no matter how you looked."

"...You're being generous." His eyes drifted back to the kitchen. "I didn't know you could cook."

She finally turned enough for him to catch the edge of her smile. "I know lots of things."

He leaned a little closer to the phone. "...This feels like classified information." Leon muttered.

"Perhaps."

"...Well?" Leon said, shifting a little higher against the pillows. "Show me what you're making."

Ada gave him a look that suggested he was being demanding for someone wrapped in a resort robe. "...Since you asked so nicely." She picked up her phone from where it rested on the island and carried it toward the stove. The view dipped briefly before steadying over a large pan the golden shrimp sizzled gently among ribbons of pasta, coated in a glossy sauce that clung to each strand. Fresh herbs were scattered across the top, bright against the cream- colored sauce, and a light snowfall of grated parmesan had already begun to melt into it.

the sight of steam curling lazily toward the camera made his stomach chose that exact moment to let out a growl. "...You know," he said, "you're making it really hard to appreciate wellness cuisine."

She placed him on the counter as she plated the pasta into a bowl. "I don't think you appreciated it to begin with."

Leon sighed dramatically. "I had grilled chicken with brown rice and broccoli."

"...sounds tragic." She said taking a bite.

"It wasn't even bad," he defended, swallowing the water that formed in his mouth after seeing those shrimps. "It was just..." He searched for the right word. "...responsible."

She carried the bowl over to the dining table and set it down. The camera jostled slightly as she propped the phone against the tiny vase on her coffee table, something he knew because he was there not so long ago. The angle gave him a view of her sitting down as she took a bite of the pasta.

Leon couldn't help smiling at the memory of two days ago when he'd been sitting at that very table with a greasy pizza box between them. “So Ada, you like to cook?”

She hummed chewing her bite with a slight nod, he waiting for her to finish. “I like to cook when I’m home, it’s nice to eat something that you made yourself.”

“I bet it is,” Leon said looking at the pasta. It looked delicious to say the least. Ada took another bite of her pasta while he absentmindedly picked at the apple that came in the fruit bowl earlier. Leon found himself smiling at nothing in particular. "I was just thinking..." He cleared his throat. "...If you're not busy.” Ada looked up from her bowl waiting for him to finish. "...could we do this again tomorrow?"

Ada raised a brow a smirk playing her lips. “You’re getting used to this,”

Leon let out a small sigh through his nose, leaning back into the pillows as if that might somehow make him look less transparent. “yeah I know, but can you blame me?” Ada was halfway through another bite of pasta, calm as ever, like she hadn’t just turned his entire evening into something dangerously domestic. He leaned back into the pillows slightly, then tilted his head. “…So what if I want you to call me every night?” Ada paused just enough to make it obvious she heard him. Leon continued, a faint smirk tugging at his mouth now. “Is that a problem?”

She set her fork down gently, smirk playing her lips because he knew she thought he was being cocky. “…No.”

Leon’s eyebrows lifted slightly. “No?”

“No,” she repeated, eyes steady on the screen.“…But it might turn into a problem.”

That made him sit up a little. “Why?”

“Well,” Ada reached for her glass of water, completely unbothered. “because I’m going to get busy by next week.” she said. “Zurich.”

Oh. “Work?” He asked.

“Mhm.” Ada tilted her head slightly. “You’re welcome to negotiate with my schedule.”

Leon’s voice softened just slightly. “…So you’re telling me I should enjoy this while I can.”

She chewed thoughtfully, narrowing her eyes on the screen. “…That’s one interpretation,” she said lightly.

Leon narrowed his eyes. “…What other interpretation is there?”

Ada glanced at the screen, and shrugged, utterly unfair about it. “That you’re being dramatic.”

He scoffed. “I’m not being dramatic.”

Ada paused, studying him with that calm, unreadable expression that always made Leon feel like he was being quietly evaluated and found… entertaining. “You sound disappointed,” she said at last.

“Thanks for stating the obvious, Ada,” Leon said dryly.

She held his gaze for a second before smiling. “I’ll still send pictures..”

“And call?” He added.

Ada chuckled, amused at the situation. "So bossy."

He shook his head immediately. “I’m not bossy…” I'm just going to miss you.

“I know one thing,” she said, taking another bite of her pasta, “you've definitely become very attached to our phone calls.”

Leon opened his mouth to object. "...Okay, no." he denied, with a crooked smile. "I think it's a reasonable amount of attachment."

Ada simply looked at him over another bite of pasta and hummed, thoroughly unconvinced. "Says the man who answers in half a ring."

-

The rest of the week settled into a rhythm before Leon even realized he'd found one. Every morning began with the ocean. The therapists insisted that walking barefoot along the shoreline before breakfast helped "ground the nervous system." Leon wasn't entirely convinced, but he couldn't deny there was something calming about the waves before the rest of the retreat woke up. By the fourth morning, he found himself taking the longer route without thinking.

Breakfast was healthy enough to make him miss diner food. The chamomile tea, however, surprised him. He'd never cared much for it before. It wasn't bad. It just wasn't nearly as good without Ada smiling over the rim of her own mug smiling beside him.

He didn't tell her that. Instead, he'd send a picture of his cup with a caption complaining that chamomile was conspiring against him, and she responded with something unhelpful: Perhaps it requires less complaining and more drinking.

Every afternoon ended with another journal entry. More accurately, twenty minutes of Leon staring at an empty page while holding a pen reading the prompt repeatedly. Describe how you feel. "...About what?" he'd mutter to nobody. Eventually, he'd manage a sentence. Most of them were observations, sometimes they were complaints about broccoli. More often than not… He somehow ended up writing about Ada like she just had a way of finding her way onto the page.

The therapy itself was another matter entirely. They hadn't even touched on Raccoon City yet, Leon knew it was inevitably coming. They'd begun with Washington… His unit, the people he'd lost. The therapist explaining they were working backwards, peeling away the years one layer at a time before reaching the wounds buried deepest. Some days he walked out emotionally wrung dry, wondering how talking could possibly leave him more exhausted than fighting. On one particularly difficult afternoon, his therapist had asked, almost casually, "Who do you reach out to when it becomes too much?"

Leon hadn't answered immediately because he knew there was only one answer to that question. "...her”

"You've talked about 'her' for several sessions."

Leon looked down at his hands, then back at his therapist. "...Yeah."

The therapist didn't ask for a name, he simply nodded once. "Tell me about her."

Leon rubbed his palms together, searching for somewhere to begin. "...She's..." He stopped. How did you summarize someone who had spent decades refusing to fit inside any definition? "...She's the person I think about first."

"When?"

Leon considered the question thoughtfully “...After missions." He frowned slightly. "...Before missions." He added. "...Actually..." He shook his head with a quiet laugh. "Most of the time."

By evening, he usually found himself in the gym. At first, it was a habit, but it turned into something simply because there wasn't much else to do after dinner while waiting for bedtime. He lifted heavier weights, ran farther than he intended. Worked until his shoulders burned instead of his thoughts. The staff, naturally, noticed. "Trying to escape therapy?"

"...Trying to wear myself out," Leon had replied.

They'd smiled like there wasn't much difference. The days became strangely full. So did his phone, because without fail Leon downloaded every photo Ada had sent him. Her rainy window, something she made for breakfast, a coffee from a café she'd wandered into, one of her face; she’d sent telling him that her cheeks had gotten puffy from all the pastries she had. That one made him smile and every time he thought of it. Ada was such a liar. Nothing about her face gave him the moment to think about anything other than how beautiful she was.

The collection on his phone made up a lot of the storage, just the thought that Ada must’ve gone: Leon would like this. It was enough to make him always smile, especially when he’d set his home screen to be the pictures of the Zurich sky line that Ada had taken for him and captioned: nice place for a date.

The date. He changed the plan at least once a day, because everyday he learned something about Ada that altered his ideas. Maybe dinner first. No, walking first. Somewhere with a view. He'd mentally rehearsed conversations that would never happen, discarded restaurants that didn’t fit the vibe, and somehow convinced himself that choosing where to eat with Ada Wong carried approximately the same strategic importance as a military operation. But Leon was determined to find a place where they could talk and spend time with each other with a good view and good food.

It was ridiculous, he still did it anyway.

Chapter 12: “No cute keychain this time?”

Chapter Summary

CONTENT WARNING: (this chapter contains leon reliving trauma of raccoon city, specifically the bridge (ada's death), there is also implied suicide, so be ware! if that's something that can be potentially triggering to you, skip until the text is not in italics.)

Chapter Notes

hi hellllloooo guys!!!! its so nice to finally have this chapter out! i was working on this since a looonnggg time, and it is quite lengthy so i hope you enjoy it :) Also ive been getting a few comments being excited for my edition of Death Island which i will say THANK YOU SO MUCH!! im planning alot of death island and i hope i can pull it off well, and have it canonically seamless :))))

Leon was in the middle of his nightly calls with Ada, the conversation sat in a comfortable lull that made him almost forget they were speaking through a phone in the first place. "I should tell you something."

He looked up from absent mindedly turning the corner of his blanket between his fingers. "What is it?"

"I'm flying to Zurich at the end of the week."

He chuckled. “You mentioned that last week,"

"I did." Ada replied calmly. "But, the assignment changed." He nodded once, waiting for her to continue. Probably a new place, or it was postponed- "I'll probably be out of contact for a week." well. “The estimate is unclear for how long.”

“How many days are we talking?”

“Could be a week, could be two..?”

He looked toward the window. "...Alright." He nodded once, more to himself than to her. "Just means I'll have to find someone else to complain to."

"Replacing me so soon?" Ada said in a tone that told him she was amused.

Leon looked back at the screen and scoffed. "I said complain to. I did not say replace you." She hummed, pretending to consider it. "Ada." He narrowed his eyes.

"Perhaps there's someone at the retreat." Ada said.

He couldn’t help but roll his eyes and give the phone a look even though she couldn't see him to receive it. "...Someone?"

She continued as if he hadn't spoken. "Perhaps the yoga instructor caught your attention."

Leon nearly laughed."The yoga instructor?" A disbelieving smile pulled at his mouth. "Are you hearing yourself right now?"

Ada's chuckle came over the phone. "So? Perhaps that's your type now."

He looked at the ceiling for a second. "Right.…Yeah. I spent two weeks at a wellness retreat and suddenly I'm abandoning all my terrible life choices for a yoga instructor."

Ada was quiet for exactly long enough that Leon knew he'd said something she was about to use against him."...Oh?” He closed his eyes, aware that he messed up.. "So I'm a terrible life choice."

Leon's eyes opened immediately. "...That's not what I—"

"In that case," Ada continued pleasantly, "the yoga instructor sounds like she'd be very good for you."

“Yeah well, If I wanted the yoga instructor to fix me, I wouldn't be spending every evening on the phone with a mercenary I'm definitely not supposed to be talking to."

Ada paused for a moment, clearly enjoying herself. "You are very defensive, Mr Kennedy."

"I'm not defensive. The question was ridiculous." He looked away with a sigh. "...You really enjoy making things difficult."

"I've noticed you handle difficult situations well."

"...Ada." Leon pinched the bridge of his nose. "You think I don't know you're trying to start a fight to distract me from the fact that I'm not going to hear from you for the next God-knows- how-many days?"

Ada laughed at his accusation, the sound soft through the speaker. "So you're self-aware enough to know you needed a distraction?"

Leon smiled to himself. "...yeah yeah." He let out a quiet breath, shaking his head. "...You almost had me worried about a yoga instructor." Leon looked out through the balcony doors. The moonlight stretched across the water, and for a moment neither of them spoke. "...It worked, you know." He rubbed his thumb along the edge of his phone. "I was starting to think too much."

"I know."

Leon smiled faintly. "...You always know."

Week 3 at the retreat, 8 days later.

Leon had to admit to himself that he'd gotten used to hearing her voice before bed and complaining about yoga. Used to knowing she was somewhere on the other end of the line, where it didn’t feel so… empty. And unfortunately for him, that was the exact mood that got him to spiral into the part of his thoughts he didn’t necessarily want to admit out loud.

The lights had dimmed automatically half an hour ago, once again leaving only the moon spilling through the balcony doors. Beyond the glass, the sea rolled quietly against the shore, the retreat wrapped in an almost unnatural stillness that felt like everything was designed to coax its guests toward sleep. Leon wasn't buying it, not with his gaze fixated on his phone that sat face-down on the bedside table. He'd turned it over three times already, the message hadn't changed.

Ada: //until notice That code was only used when the job was unpredictable enough where approximates was cancelled out.

He closed his eyes. She's done this before. A slow breath that he practiced during his yoga sessions. She always comes back. His eyes opened again, staring at the ceiling that stared back. What if something goes wrong? He rolled onto his side. “No.” She knows what she's doing. He squeezed his eyes shut again, hoping his mind would stop working over time and rest. His therapist had warned him about this that afternoon. "Notice when your mind begins filling silence with catastrophe."

Easy for them to say. They weren't the ones who'd watched Ada Wong slip from their hands into a void they couldn’t follow. He exhaled sharply through his nose and reached blindly toward the bedside table and read the message again as if it would change. He shut it and set it down with more care than necessary. "...She's fine." Saying it aloud to help him believe it more.

Outside, the waves rolled steadily against the shore. Inside, the silence seemed louder, so instead he focused on the sound of the ocean, counting each wave as it reached the shore till Leon didn’t remember falling asleep.

.

.

.

The first thing Leon noticed was the smoke of rotting flesh. Raccoon city. His hand was tight on his gun, his steps young, moving with the confusion of someone who hadn't learned what the world had become yet. His mind carried all the scars that his 21 year old body did it. He didn’t know when exactly he ended up on the bridge with Ada.

“Oh Leon,” she frowned, taking out her gun. “Why couldn’t you just hand over the sample?”

Ada was at the end of the barrel of Leon’s gun, knowing damn well he was actively controlling his hands from shaking. “As much as I wanted to trust you, I couldn't."

“I really hoped it wouldn’t end up like this,”

“Was I some pawn to you?” His voice sounded so naive he almost winced.

“Look, I’m just doing my job.”

“And I’m doing mine. Ada… please,” he stepped closer to her, Annette should be behind him any moment now. “Drop your gun.”

“Hand over the sample, Leon.” She said firmly, but he could see the conflict in her eyes, “I really don’t want to hurt you,”

Leon holstered his gun deliberately, staring back at Ada who still had her gun still raised, her expression unreadable. Back then, he didn't have it in him to register her expression to have any conflict she was trying to bury. Now, he knew better, which made the hesitation beneath the surface clear as day, which somehow made this hurt even more. Leon stared back at her with certainty, saying a variation of what he said to her years ago: “Then you shoot me, but I know you won’t.”

Ada’s eyes dropped briefly to the gun in her hand. Then back to him with her brows furrowed. “Leon..” His name left her lips softly as she took her finger of the trigger easing it down. For a second, Leon saw the woman behind the mission. Before either of them could think about what came next, he crossed the distance between them and pulled her into his arms, holding her tightly against him, turning his body between her and the rest of the bridge because he knew exactly what was coming.

A deafening gunshot cracked through the air which Leon felt a sharp burst of pain before he processed anything else. His body jerked slightly, his arms tightening around Ada instead of letting go. Ada looked up at him horrified, blinking rapidly as if to confirm he did exactly what she thought he did. "Leon?" Her fingers pressed against his chest, searching his expression before she noticed the blood spreading beneath the vest. "What—" Her voice broke. "What did you do?" Her hand shifted against him frantically searching his gaze as her breathing became uneven as she desperately asked. "Why would you do that?!" she couldn't understand how he could still choose her after everything.

Leon barely had time to answer before the bridge groaned beneath them. It took him a moment to realize what was about to happen, "Ada." She looked at him as he roughly grabbed her shoulders. "Move."

"What?"

"Come on." He pushed against her, trying to force her toward the other side of the bridge. Away from the section that was about to break. His body was already failing him, he could feel it, but It didn't matter when he just needed her across.

"Leon—"

"Go." His voice was firmer this time as he guided her forward, trying to rewrite the memory that had haunted him for years. But the dream wouldn't let him. When Leon looked over his shoulder with a cold glare pointed at Annette, who was now coughing on the ground, the bridge gave out. He looked back at Ada just in time as her body slid toward the edge, getting a hold of her before he could finish calling out her name. The sample slid out of pocket and fell into the gaping hole. He didn't take his eyes off of her. "Just hold onto me." Tightening his grip around hers. "I've got you."

Ada looked up at him, for a moment, she wasn't the woman from his memories. She was just Ada, the same one that he had pizza with, the same one that called him and told him he wouldn’t be able to reach her. The person he couldn't lose. So when he looked back at Ada all he saw was the acceptance of consequences in her eyes. That same expression that had haunted him. "Forget it." She shook her head slightly.

Leon felt his chest tighten. "No. Shut up." His voice broke slightly. "I've got you this time."

"You're bleeding..."

"No." His grip tightened, trying to convince himself that this time would be different. "It doesn't matter."

"Leon.” Her eyes moved over his face. “You need to get yourself out and heal yourself,” she said softly. “It's not worth it.” The same words that had followed him for years. He pulled harder, trying to lift her. Trying to rewrite the moment that had broken something inside him back in 1998.

"I'm not letting this happen again." His voice cracked. “You don’t get to choose what is and what isn’t worth it to me,” For a second, when everything felt like it stopped when he felt her slip slightly in his grasp.

The entire world went into a vacuum when Ada looked at him and said: “Take care of yourself, Leon." Something unbearably sad in her eyes. “I’m sorry for everything,”

"No." His voice broke immediately. "No, no, I've got you." He could feel his grip weakening. The memory was burning through him as he held onto the impossible weight of her in his arms, that felt entirely too real, even as her hand slipped, the warmth of her fingers leaving his in a flicker of a heartbeat. "I already lost you once..." His voice cracked. "Please."

With the same amount of helplessness that created fate, the same moment repeated, and the realization that no matter how differently he had done that day, Ada's fingers always slipped from his; caught up to him. For one horrifying second, Leon was back in Racoon city at twenty-one years old watching her fall. "No!" The scream ripped out of him the very same it did back then, except this time, it was followed by Leon diving in after her, in hopes of reaching her in time.

Every part of him wanted to tell her something. Anything, that he should have held on harder, that he had spent years wondering what he could have done differently and when it came to it he still failed. But the words wouldn't come.

All he managed to think was I’m sorry I couldn’t save… you.

.

.

.

Leon jerked awake shooting upright before he was even conscious of where he was. His breath came in sharp, uneven pulls as he stared into the empty space in front of him. For several seconds, he couldn't move, hands still clenched, holding onto nothing as his heart pounded against his chest as the last pieces of the dream faded. He looked down at his hands; empty and shaking like they did in 1998. He dragged a hand over his face, trying to steady himself as he leaned back against the headboard and closed his eyes. She knew what she was doing. He knew that. So why did his chest still hurt like he was twenty-one years old again?

Next day, 7pm

The walk back from the beach is as boring as watching the foam disappear from wet sand. The sun had climbed higher over the water, warming the boardwalk, and giving a killer view for the guests currently eating the dinner he intentionally skipped. Leon couldn’t stop thinking of his therapist's question that kept returning with every step.

How many times has she actually come back?

Every time; She always found her way back. So why was some part of him still waiting for the moment she wouldn't? He unlocked the door to his room almost absentmindedly, already thinking he should shower before the bed. "...You were right," he muttered under his breath, already imagining the look on her face. That small, satisfied expression Ada gave him when she knew she was right. He shook his head slightly at the memory. "Better off not telling you that part." He chuckled.

“Tell me what?”

Leon froze half way through the doorway to the bathroom, looking up to see his shocked expression staring back at him. “Ada?” He called out, taking a step back to look in the direction of the sound. And surely enough standing beside the balcony doors, bathed in the late-morning light spilling in from the ocean, was Ada.

She'd traded her usual mission clothes for a floral skirt that sat low on her hips, catching the wind as it complimented the frilly red top that moved gently in the sea breeze drifting through the open balcony door. “I thought you were used to seeing me around,” Her sunglasses rested on top of her head, "Yet you look like you’ve seen a ghost. Other than that?” She folded her arms, “I’d say therapy suits you.”

Leon simply stared as his brain tried very hard to explain why she couldn't possibly be standing there. "You..." His voice barely worked. "...What’re you doing here?" He let out a quiet laugh because he didn't know what else to do with the sudden relief rushing through him. "I thought … you were in Switzerland." He was walking toward her before he realized.

Her lips curved slightly. “Disappointed?” She tilted her head, which made a small flower catch his eye, one that had been tucked into her hair, almost certainly something the retreat staff had given her. Which meant she was very much real because his brain wouldn’t come up with Ada Wong wearing a flower in her hair and smiling at him. When he reached her, his hands settled around her hips, eyes moving over her face trying to take in the fact that she was actually standing there. After a few seconds, she tilted her head. "...Finished checking?"

Leon exhaled a quiet scoff, attempting a smile.. "...Almost." Normally, he would've had something clever to say. Instead, Ada watched the smile slowly disappear from his face. His shoulders, usually held so rigidly, seemed to sag all at once.

Her own smile softened immediately. "...Leon?" She had barely spoken his name before he wrapped both arms around her so tightly it almost knocked the breath from her lungs. Ada caught herself against him, surprised enough before she felt his shaky breaths. "...Hey," she said quietly. One of her hands slid instinctively up the back of his neck while the other settled between his shoulder blades. Leon buried his face against her shoulder and for a brief moment, he let himself believe the simplest thing in the world was that Ada was here. Somewhere along the way, a single tear escaped before he could stop it. He didn't even notice until Ada did. She leaned back just enough to look at him. "Leon.”

He gave a small shake of his head, laughing quietly at himself. "...I'm alright."

She studied him for a second, thumb brushed briefly beneath his eye, catching another tear before it disappeared completely. "I leave you alone for a week..."

“Eight days.” he reminded, “and I haven't seen you in basically a month.”

A smile curled with her lips. "...This is quite the welcome."

Leon huffed a laugh through his nose, just his grip tightening a fraction more. “Shut up,”

She smiled, "I'll be sure to thank the staff for taking good care of you,” That finally earned a proper laugh from him. Ada could practically see the weight leaving his shoulders. "Better?"

Leon looked at her for another quiet moment. "...Yeah." He kept an arm loosely around her waist while she took in the surroundings with the sort of practiced glance that assessed the space faster than most people blinked. "Go ahead. Laugh."

Ada's eyes drifted over the yoga mat folded in the corner, then to the stack of pamphlets on mindfulness placed on his desk. The mostly untouched fruit basket near the TV beside the tea corner with suspicious amount of herbal tea. "No hidden liquor?"

"You're hilarious." The room didn’t keep her attention for long so when her gaze ended on him, his grin spread immediately. “Checking me out now?”

She chuckled raising her brows as she looked at him properly, “you look like you’ve been spending a good amount of time at the beach,”

Leon looked down at himself, noticing the tan evident because of his gym shirt. His arms flexing noticeably the moment Ada's gaze landed on them. “Guess so,”

Ada nodded, looking up at him as if she’d made up her mind. “You look good.”

“That’s your conclusion?”

She smirked.“Mh hm. What else do you want me to say?”

“That my arms look good?”

She folded her arms. “Seriously? Leon S Kennedy, fishing for compliments?”

“You’re the one who was just looking at them, I’ve been working out you know?” He said, holding his arm out for her to touch, his other hand still at her hips.

Ada raised a brow, giving in poking his bicep before giving it a small squeeze. “Working out so you can fish compliments from me or because you’ve discovered pushups are good for you?”

Leon rolled his eyes. “Admit it Ada,” he folded his arms. "You like my arms."

She scoffed, looking away as if the suggestion was ridiculous. "Seriously?"

he shook his head. "No, no, I heard it."

"You heard nothing." She said.

"You touched the merchandise.” He said confidently. “The goods have been inspected, now I need a report back,”

"A report?" She laughed. “I didn’t come here to write a report on your biceps.”

His arms settled on her waist again. “Then why did you come here?”

She tilted her head. “Because I finished my mission and New Jersey was on the way?”

“You always say that,”

“Because it’s true,” she retorted.

Leon sighed shaking his head in disappointment. “So you’re not gonna say it.”

“Say what?”

“That you like my arms,” he shrugged. “Or… do you need more evidence that tells you that they’re not just for show?”

Ada nodded once, playing along. "I suppose so." Instead of answering, his hands settled more securely around her waist. "...Leon." she narrowed her eyes. "... what are you doing?”

"What?" His thumb brushed lightly against her side before he seemed to realize what he was doing and cleared his throat. "I’m just talking..." A faint smirk appeared. "Tell me, how much trouble would I get in for keeping you here?"

Ada raised an eyebrow. "You're asking?"

"Trying to be respectful."

She chuckled. "How unlike you."

He scoffed quietly. "That hurts." He bent his knees holding her gaze before she could finish, he lifted her clean off the floor.

"Leon!" Her laugh escaped before she could stop it, one hand immediately catching his shoulder. "You've lost your mind."

"Probably..." he smirked, "I'm never letting go of you now."

"You've been doing yoga for three weeks and suddenly think you can throw people around?"

"I'm not throwing." He started walking. "I'm transporting."

"Oh, is that what this is?" she said "Where exactly are you transporting me?"

Leon looked toward the only big surface in the room: his bed. "...There."

She followed his gaze. "...Leon." Ada looked from the bed, then back at him. A slow, amused smile started to form. “I appreciate the boldness,” she hummed. "But usually a little more ceremony involved, you're skipping a few steps."

"Ceremony?" He immediately became suspicious. "...What?"

Ada shrugged, "You're aware we haven't actually had our first date yet, right?"

Leon furrowed his brows, adjusting his hold on her where her arms wrapped around his neck as he just stared at her. A laugh escaped him when the realization caught up to him. The fact that he had very confidently announced he was ‘never letting her go.’ “Wow,” he said, because what was one supposed to say to Ada Wong when she just implied that you're 'too eager'? "For the record," he said, carrying her the remaining few steps anyway. "my intentions are completely PG."

Ada's smile widened slightly. "Are they?"

"yes," He reached the edge of the bed. “I have plans." he said before lowering her carefully onto the mattress against the pillows. Her skirt spread around her as she looked up at him, thoroughly amused. "Not those kinds of plans." He immediately corrected himself. The tips of his ears had started turning slightly red, which he knew Ada noticed instantly but was polite enough not to mention. "Maybe the visual argument isn't helping me."

Ada laughed quietly, looking at the space between them. "I was going to say," her hands settled on his shoulders as he still held her waist.

A small smile tugged at his mouth. "I'll admit maybe the timing could use some improvement." he leaned back, lying down propped on his elbow that went over her thighs.

Ada hummed. "Maybe?" she watched him as she settled, looking up at her as he rested his other arm on the her abdomen.

Chapter 13: “Welcome home, Gorgeous.”

Chapter Summary

Chapter Notes

hello!! im back from vacation :) enjoy.

With his considerably heavy duffle bag in his grasp, Leon pressed his index finger against the red piece of glass on the machine, confirming his admission as it disappeared into the wall faster than it took for him to realise. “High tech,” he chuckled, letting himself in. Except he stood there on the welcome mat, which did not say welcome. Looking at the penthouse before him, he half expected to see Ada standing there with her arms crossed; it had become a habit now, to look for her in the corners, expecting her to appear from shadows rather than disappear like she usually did for the first years he’d known her.

The penthouse looked different in the morning, a contrast to how he saw it the night after the New York City incident with Glenn Arias. He took off his heavy, scuffed leather lace-ups, carefully putting them into one of the shelves where it looked like Ada had a small collection of her heels. They looked more casual than the boot type he’d seen her wear on missions, - Leon wondered if these were the pairs she reached for the most as he put his duffel bag onto the floor. Though they might've been Ada’s ‘to-go’ heels, they still managed to look like a boutique display next to his. “So much for fitting in.”

With his bare feet now against the cool marble, he took in the morning light coming in from the two-story windows. It looked awfully empty. Empty in a way that he almost didn’t want to fill up the space, feeling oddly intrusive. “No flowers, huh?” He said to himself, glancing at the vacant vase at the centre of the counter. “Might need to fix that,” Leon recalled how this conversation went a week ago, the memory coming back to him vividly as he moved through the house. He turned the comforter slowly between his fingers, like if he moved carefully enough, the moment wouldn’t shift. “…You’re really going alone?”

Ada’s expression doesn’t change much. But her voice softens slightly. “I always do.” She studies him like someone she knows too well. “I’ll be careful,” she added.

Leon lets out a short, almost disbelieving laugh. “…Of course,” he let out a slow breath through his nose. “I know.” Leon nods once, accepting to himself that this is how things would be. “…Okay.”

“You’re going to be house hunting,” Ada reminded him, placing her hand on his thigh.

“…And I’m staying at your place.” Leon scoffed because it sounded like he still needed something to hold onto, in the way his heart beat faster when she dared to smile at him like that.

Ada’s mouth curves slightly. “Yes.”

He looks at her again. “…You sure about that?”

“I already made space.”

Leon just stares, well surprised because that was not what he thought she would say, and by the looks of it, some part of that admission surprised her too. "...Yesterday?"

"I assumed you'd say yes." She said, swiftly folding her arms.

He looked away, shaking his head. “I cannot be that predictable.”

Somewhere in the middle of the conversation, he’d ended up sitting on the edge of the bed again—and Ada had stayed close enough that the space between them didn’t really exist anymore. Her dress catches the light when she shifts slightly, soft fabric moving with her like she doesn’t belong in anything as harsh as missions or encrypted files or disappearing for weeks at a time. Which made Leon, very belatedly, realise something simple. He missed her. His forehead brushes her shoulder first—just a moment of contact—then he shifts closer, exhaling quietly as if the tension he’s been carrying for days finally has somewhere to go.

Ada pauses mid-sentence. “…Leon?” Her hand lifts almost automatically, settling against the back of his neck with that familiar ease she always has with him, fingers resting there like she’s anchoring him in place without needing to ask.

Leon closes his eyes at the touch, knowing he shouldn't be doing this. Definitely not supposed to be cataloguing how she smells familiar in a way that makes the last month feel unreal by comparison. “…You’re really leaving,” he murmurs, voice muffled against her. He wasn’t even asking her at this point. “…Go do your mission,” he mutters, like he’s negotiating with himself more than her. “But come back.”

Ada meets his eyes. “Yes.”

Leon nods once, like he’s locking that in place somewhere. “You are coming back,” He clears his throat, looking away for half a second like he’s annoyed at himself for saying what he’s about to say. “We have plans.” He said, looking at her now, with almost a glint of accusation in his eyes. “You don’t get to just… disappear overseas and ruin it.”

“I see,” She nodded, moving a little closer again, closing the space he created when he pulled back. “I wasn’t planning on skipping it.”

Leon studied her face for a second. “…You better not be.”

For a second, he didn't understand why everything around him felt so soft and warm. Then he realised he was lying face-down across Ada's bed. His cheek was pressed into what he assumed was her pillow, one arm tucked beneath it, and he had apparently been there long enough for the mattress to settle beneath him. When did he even reach the second floor?

He lifted his head slightly. The unmistakable faint scent of Ada followed him with subtlety. At the time, everything had sounded practical. Now, standing—or rather, lying—in the middle of her bedroom, Leon was beginning to question every decision that had brought him here.

He pushed himself upright so quickly that he nearly tangled himself in the sheets. "...Oh, come on." He had been in Ada's apartment for less than an hour, already committing to embarrassing himself. He stared at the bed with profound disappointment in himself. "Get a grip." He stood, straightened his shirt, and glanced toward the doorway as though someone might have witnessed the entire thing. "...Cameras…She definitely has cameras.” Because Ada Wong didn't own an apartment without surveillance. Leon made it halfway down the hallway before stopping to look back toward the bedroom. If she checks the cameras, I'm finished.

Leon was out and about in New York City following the timings given for the listings, but as the sun dropped lower, he decided stopping by the first grocery store he saw was a good idea. It wasn’t. It happened to be one of the fancier types of grocery stores that definitely came with an expensive price tag, and a side of hyperawareness to not look like a shoplifter. But then again, he was in New York. After thirty minutes of raised eyebrows and grumbling, he had decided to buy a steak cut because he knew he wouldn’t be able to justify the price of anything else. “I don't even know how to cook a steak,” he muttered, buying it anyway because he felt like he needed to buy something; leaving without buying anything would probably raise questions.

After returning from the store, he arranged the flowers he had bought from the old lady at the curb. They brought some colour into the otherwise polished room, and for the first time since arriving, the apartment felt slightly less like a place he had been temporarily occupying. He put the groceries away afterwards and made himself dinner. It was nothing impressive, though; he had considered making something more complicated, then decided against it when he realised he was cooking for one. He ate at the kitchen island, cleaned everything afterwards, and spent some time looking through the apartment listings he had saved.

Eventually, he found himself sitting on the couch with his phone in his hand, as his eyes grew heavy. “What time is it?” He yawned, glancing toward the clock, putting his phone down as he stood, stretching the stiffness out of his shoulders, before he headed upstairs to take a shower before bed.

The bedroom was still exactly as he had left it. Leon made a deliberate effort not to look at the bed after what happened earlier— he didn't need to think about that again. Instead, he noticed a door beyond the bedroom. Is that the bathroom? He had seen it earlier but hadn't paid much attention to it. The door pushed open to an enormous room. Definitely not the bathroom. There was a desk stationed near a corner with a chair positioned neatly behind it, and enough open space that the room could have passed for another bedroom if it had contained a bed. Instead, it looked like an office, with everything organised to make it look minimal.

Leon stepped inside. He looked around. "...Nice." He was about to turn around when he noticed another door. Bathroom? He walked over and opened it. "...Oh." It wasn't a bathroom. It was a walk-in closet almost as large as the office. “Wow.” He stayed in the doorway as the lights turned on automatically, displaying the rows of clothes hung neatly along the walls, most of them in dark colours. Black, red, deep burgundy, occasional flashes of white. Everything looked expensive enough that Leon instinctively kept his hands at his sides.

Then he noticed the shoes lined up on the shelf. He recognised the red soles immediately. They were the ones she wore when he saw her in Raccoon City. Simple black heels with red bottoms. Leon looked at the clothes again, seeing if he could recognise any of the dresses he’d seen her wear over the years. Instead, his eye caught on a dress hanging further toward the back. Gold. It was elegant without being overly elaborate, and for some reason he couldn't stop looking at it.

A thought surfaced before he could prevent it. What is she going to wear on our date? The glittering dress didn't help him hold back from picturing her in it far too easily. Leon looked at the dress one last time. Then shook away the thought of Ada in the dress. "Nope.” He said, turning around. Three weeks ago, he would have laughed if someone told him he'd end up staying in Ada Wong's Manhattan apartment, sleeping on her couch, buying flowers for her, and worrying about what she might wear on their first date.

He had come upstairs to shower before bed. Not to try to predict Ada's wardrobe choices. This was a new low. With a sigh, Leon walked back through the office, returned to the bedroom, and finally saw the other door in the room that must be a bathroom. Which was just as extravagant as the rest of the apartment. A large bathtub sat near the window, positioned so that whoever was bathing could look out over Manhattan. Leon stopped beside it, staring through the glass at the city.

Of course Ada had a bathtub with a view. He shook his head, turned on the tap, and splashed cold water over his face before he got into the shower and let the water run over his shoulders.

Eventually, He changed into clean clothes and decided there was no reason to sleep on the couch downstairs when he had an actual bed. So he pulled back the covers and got in. He settled on the right side, leaving the left untouched. It felt significant, somehow.

It was more comfortable than he'd expected. The sheets smelled faintly like Ada, although Leon deliberately didn't entertain that thought for too long, even when it seemed to be persistent. He turned onto his side and stared out toward the windows where Manhattan glowed beyond the glass. The space on his left was just a reminder of what the future held. For now? He had a place to stay and apartment listings to look through, as work was waiting for him eventually.

His eyes were already beginning to close when another thought occurred to him. They'd never actually done this before. Never simply gone to sleep in the same room and woken up the next morning with nowhere else they needed to be. "...That's new." He let out a quiet laugh to himself before turning to stare at the ceiling again. He wasn't going to overthink it, just a thought for another day.

Next morning at 5:38 AM

Leon woke slowly, mostly because he couldn’t open his eyes. He hadn't slept that deeply in a long time.

Soft gold had begun spilling through the windows, creeping across the bedroom and catching on the edges of the furniture. Manhattan was beginning to wake beyond the glass, the city still quieter than it would be in another hour.

Glancing toward the empty side of the bed made the thought of Ada come so naturally that he didn't even question it. He wondered what she was doing wherever she was. Maybe because he'd spent enough nights imagining Ada beside him that the arrangement had already become familiar in his head. Especially the dreams which made him smile when they came back to him. One night it felt like a curse because it wasn’t real; the other felt like it was bliss to experience it without stress.

It was always something understated. Fleeting memory of a moment not yet made. They were married, somehow. God knows how. As much as he’s ashamed to admit. The details had been hazy in the way dreams always were, but he remembered the feeling. The smell of coffee and the cool marble of the kitchen. Morning sunlight had been coming through the windows, and Ada had been standing at the counter making coffee while Leon had been trying to convince her of something she wasn’t buying. At some point, he'd wrapped his arms around her from behind, the usual. She'd complained that he was in her way, and he’d insisted he was helping as he kissed her cheek for no real reason at all.

That was it.

Leon stared at the ceiling. "...Wow." He rubbed a hand over his warm face. It was almost ridiculous how happy the dream had made him. He didn’t know whether to be proud of his brain for the first dream— after months of nightmares- or question his brain’s choice that had apparently decided what he really needed was to dream about making coffee with Ada Wong. He certainly wasn't complaining. If anything, it was probably the nicest dream he'd had in years.

He pushed himself upright, running both hands through his hair. "You're getting ahead of yourself,” he muttered to himself gruffly. He turned his head toward the window. The sun was rising over Manhattan now, painting the buildings in warm light. He sat up and rested his back against the headboard. He hadn't expected to like sleeping here; He certainly hadn't expected to sleep this well.

After sitting there for many minutes, Leon got out of bed and walked toward the window. He stood there for a while, watching the city wake beneath him— his stomach growled. "...Coffee." Leon turned away from the window and headed downstairs, still carrying the last remnants of the dream somewhere in the back of his mind.

He filled the coffee maker, waited for it to finish, and poured himself a cup. The first sip was hot enough to wake him properly, and he leaned against the kitchen counter with a quiet sigh.

The buzz of his cut through the quiet morning. Leon picked it up with his free hand, expecting a message from one of the apartment listings. Instead, he saw Ada's name. His eyes moved over the notification as he took another sip of coffee without thinking.

Ada: Mission's over. Heading to the airport now. I'll land in New York this afternoon.

Leon reread the message—and immediately spat the coffee back into the sink. "Jesus—" He grabbed a paper towel, coughing once as he stared at the phone.

Mission's over. And back this afternoon. Processed in his mind as reality crashed into him. He checked the time. She was leaving Hong Kong at six. She'd be back in New York around… four. Which meant— He did the math again— She'd probably be back here around seven.

Tonight.

Leon stared at the phone. "...Tonight?" He looked around the kitchen as though the apartment itself had just delivered the news. She’d be back by Dinner. He put his— her mug down and looked toward the flowers he'd bought earlier. At least that had been a good decision, because he knew that attempting to make a steak wasn’t.

- 7:02 pm

a sixteen hour flight was no joke. Ada had been awake for far too long. Sixteen hours in transit had left her with a dull, persistent headache that sat behind her eyes, and by the time she finally made it through the airport and into the car, she had stopped pretending she had the patience for anything. By the time Manhattan came into view, she was exhausted enough that she even took off her heels in the car. All she wanted was a shower and cool sheets cushioning her without a worry for the next morning.

She let herself into the apartment, one hand still holding the strap of her bag. The door clicked shut behind her. Ada stood there for a moment with her eyes closed. It was fairly quiet, maybe Leon wasn’t home? But she smelled something. She opened her eyes and located the unmistakable smell of something being cooked.

She slipped her bag from her shoulder and left it near the entrance with her heels before walking farther into the apartment.

Leon was standing at the kitchen counter wearing… an apron, looking at something on a paper with a frown. He hadn't noticed her yet; too focused on the pan in front of him and the paper on the counter, sleeves all pushed up, occasionally checking whatever was cooking with the concentration of someone who had decided this was a very important operation.

There was nothing much on the pan, maybe garlic, definitely something that didn’t warrant this much focus. Her headache somehow became the second-most confusing thing she'd experienced that evening.

Leon finally glanced over his shoulder, his expression changed instantly. He put down what he was holding. "Ada?!”

She gave him a tired smile. "Hello..”

He walked toward her without hesitation. "I was starting to wonder if you'd—" He stopped himself when he got close enough to see her properly. The usual check of injuries came negative, so he decided that the exhaustion was obvious. "Long flight?"

"Sixteen hours."

Leon winced. "That's brutal."

Ada sighed, reaching up to rub her temple. "And a souvenir of a headache." She raised an eyebrow, looking past him toward the kitchen. "...Are you cooking?"

Leon glanced down at himself as though he'd forgotten what he was wearing. "Yeah, dinner.”

Ada stared at him. "Why?"

He hesitated. "...I thought you'd be hungry when you got back." A tired laugh escaped her. Leon automatically smiled at the sound.

The irritation she'd carried through the airport hadn't disappeared completely, but something about coming home to this made it considerably easier to ignore. "You didn't have to." Ada's gaze dropped briefly to the apron again. “There’s plenty of restaurants around,”

"I know." He said it easily. "But I wanted to."

She shook her head. "apparently learning how to cook comes with apartment hunting,”

"Let's not oversell it." Leon stepped closer, pulling her closer by the waist as he kissed her cheek. "Welcome home, gorgeous." Normally, she would have made some comment in response, but instead she just rested her head against his shoulder. The moment his arms settled around her, she simply leaned into him, releasing the tension she'd carried through the door all at once. "Yeah," he murmured with a chuckle, one hand moving gently over her back. "You're tired."

Ada blinked, pulling back at him, tired amusement appearing in her eyes. "...Gorgeous?"

Leon cleared his throat, giving her a grin. "Yeah." He held her for another moment. "You should probably go upstairs and get some rest,”

Ada raised a brow. "What?"

"Take a shower, lie down; whatever you want until the food’s ready.” He glanced over his shoulder toward the kitchen. "The potatoes are taking longer than I expected anyway."

Ada looked past him. "And you're going to finish cooking?"

He nodded. "That was the plan."

"You?"

Leon narrowed his eyes. "Very funny." She smiled faintly. "I'll bring the food upstairs when it's done," he continued. "You can eat in bed."

Ada stared at him. “I didn’t think I needed your approval to eat in bed?”

“You look like you're about five minutes away from falling asleep standing up."

She narrowed her eyes. "I'm not."

He laughed. "Sure you don’t want me to carry you?"

Ada stared at him as if he’d suddenly suggested having a picnic on the moon. "No."

"Not even a little?" He teased.

She hated that she was smiling. "I'm really tired," she admitted eventually. "But I can make it upstairs myself."

Leon nodded. "Alright." He gestured toward the staircase. "Go."

She made it three steps before looking back. “Don’t burn down my kitchen."

Leon put a hand over his heart. "I would never." He laughed again.

She had expected something very different when she'd given Leon permission to stay, something closer to Colorado: Takeout containers and mess. She had not expected to come home to a clean apartment. The flowers in the vase caught her eye as she glanced back at the kitchen. Chrysanthemums. Her gaze went to Leon tossing the potatoes in something that looked very garlicky.

She stopped for half a second. He'd bought those. And, now apparently, Mr. Welcome Home Gorgeous was downstairs cooking dinner for her.

Ada shook her head. "Unbelievable." She entered her bedroom and closed the door behind her. A shower sounded heavenly. She changed out of her travel clothes in her closet, her eyes catching the shelves she had cleared for Leon, of which he occupied half of one shelf.

Getting into a warm fall of water soothed her headache considerably. She changed into silk pajamas and put up her hair back loosely in a clip, and got into her bed. Or rather now, her side of the bed. Because it seemed as though the nightstand on the right occupied Leon’s things.

It made her smile, just a little. He really had made himself at home. Somehow, comfortable enough to start cooking for her.

-

Ada hadn't meant to fall asleep.

The first thing she became aware of was warmth against her cheek. Something gentle moved along her face, barely there. Ada's brow furrowed, eyes remained closed. A thumb brushed carefully beneath her cheekbone. "Hey..." it was Leon, voice hesitant.

When Ada slowly opened her eyes, Leon was sitting beside the bed, leaning slightly toward her. His hand had stopped against her cheek, as though he'd been caught in the act. For a moment, neither of them said anything. She watched him give her a small, almost sheepish smile. "I made dinner,” Ada blinked sleepily. “You should eat something before going to bed.”

The smell reached her before she even moved. Quickly realizing how hungry she was. Ada sat up fully and looked at the tray properly. “You cut the steak?”

"I figured smaller pieces would be easier to eat."

He'd actually neatly cut it into bite-sized pieces and arranged them on the plate. Beside it was a salad, roasted potatoes with garlic, and a small dish of dip. Everything looked surprisingly good. Ada looked at the plate. "You did all of this?"

"Yeah." He sounded genuinely proud of them, the least she could do is give him the satisfaction of eating it with a smile.

Ada shifted back against the pillows while he carefully set everything down. The smell of garlic immediately filled the room. She picked up her fork and took the first bite. Leon watched her expectantly as she chewed. She looked up at Leon, who was trying very hard not to look like he was waiting for approval. "...It's good,” Ada leaned toward him and pressed a quick kiss to his cheek. “Thank you." The words were simple, but she meant them.

Leon smiled, his expression softening. "You're welcome." He popped one of the pieces of roasted potatoes in his mouth. “I had to call Chris to make it, don’t even get me started.

Ada glanced down at the plate with a smile. “I’m impressed,” she said softly because she couldn’t really admit what was really happening here. She liked coming home and finding him here. "Don't let me get used to this," she murmured, even with a small curve of her mouth, this meal meant more to her than she was letting on.

Don't let me get used to this, if I can’t keep it forever.
