Chapter 14: "Your job really doesn't like sharing you, does it?"

Chapter Notes

i completed this chapter faster than i thought! so here we are :)

Leon had been awake for at least ten minutes pretending to sleep, with no intention of admitting that. Morning light already filtered softly through the curtains, turning the bedroom pale and warm. It had only been two weeks since he'd moved into his new house. Two weeks of him spending several nights a week at Ada’s apartment anyway. Many months of postponing their first actual date because apparently neither of them could manage to get through the logistics of moving without something else happening.

It was the weekend of their date; which was settled for tomorrow, and that meant there was nowhere he needed to be other than in Ada’s arms. More importantly, she had her head rested against him, so close that he could feel the steady rhythm of her breathing. And not only that, she also had her palm loosely resting on his chest; and that was enough reason to remain exactly where he was, one arm comfortably around her waist. He had enough nights at Ada’s to know that if he moved, she would wake up because Ada was a light sleeper. It turned out he didn't need to move, because his godforsaken phone started ringing.

Ada stirred beside him almost immediately, lifting her cheek and pulling her warm palm away from his chest. "Leon..." she said, her voice soft with sleep. Maybe he could just pretend to be asleep? "Your phone.." Leon remained perfectly still, feeling Ada prop herself to look at him properly. Maybe she would consider leaving him alone. Even better, maybe his phone would stop ruining his perfect morning. It didn't. The phone continued ringing from somewhere on the nightstand. He heard her sigh softly. "Leon..." she tried again, her warm hand touching his shoulder. "You have a call."

"Mhm..." His arm tightened around her waist, pulling her back against him. "It's probably Hunnigan," he mumbled, shifting closer, burying his face against her neck.

Ada's voice immediately became muffled beneath him. "Okay, I can't breathe." She laughed quietly, patting his back. "Leon." The phone finally stopped ringing. Then it started again. Just great. Leon sighed against her neck, reaching blindly toward the nightstand until his fingers found the phone. He barely even glances at it and declines it. "...Did you just hang up on Hunnigan?"

He set the phone back on the nightstand. "Mhm." Before she could say anything else, her phone started ringing too. Leon stared at her. "Don't."

"Leon."

He gave her an entirely shameless look. "It's the weekend."

Ada glanced toward her phone facing up. “Sherry.” She sighed. "Alright,” she freed herself from his hold and picked up the call anyway.

"Finally!" Sherry's voice came through immediately. "Ada, where's Leon?"

Ada glanced down at him, which had coincidentally made him bury his face against her shoulder. “Traitor,” he grumbled.

Sherry continued before she could say anything. "There's an emergency. Where is he?"

Ada raised an eyebrow, sitting up. "An emergency?"

"It's important.” Sherry cleared her throat before she dropped her voice in a whisper. "Ada, please make him answer his phone."

Right on cue, his phone started ringing again "He's going to answer it." Ada said, hanging up.

Leon looked horrified. "No." She reached over and picked up his phone. His eyes widened. "Ada. You were supposed to be on my side."

“Answer the phone." She said simply.

He looked at her as though she'd personally betrayed him. Leon sighed heavily and finally took the phone. "Kennedy here."

For a second Hunnigan didn’t say anything, making his brows pull together. But first thing she said was enough material to ruin his entire week. “A helicopter is on the way. We need you in the DC office at this moment now.”

"What?" His eyes narrowed. "Now?"

“Fill you in the details later, the helicopter will arrive in 30 minutes, be ready.”

"30 minutes?!" He sat upright. "You're serious?"

“It’s important.” that's all she had to say.

“Yeah, I understand it's important." His tone suggested that he very much did not appreciate having to understand it. "but you see im in the middle of something-"

"im sure you are, Leon, but we need you here as soon as possible."

he rubbed his head, knowing he wasn't going get a say in this. "alright, just tell me how long I'm going to be in D.C."

Hunnigan sighed, typing something over the line, “not long, there will be a secondary carrier. We are still scouting the coordinates of the data.”

Leon closed his eyes, pinching his nose bridge with his fingers. "You're kidding." He didn’t even want to say ‘I’ll be there’ he just hung up. He'd finally gotten a full weekend to spend with Ada, And, more importantly, take her out to dinner. He dropped the phone onto the mattress with more force than necessary to the point where it bounced off the bed and Ada caught it.

“What’s going on?” She asked.

"Apparently, the entire federal government has collectively decided I don't deserve a weekend."

- 12 minutes later

Ada was sitting at the kitchen island with her laptop open beside her coffee, when Leon came downstairs after changing. A dark grey suit jacket hung over an untucked white shirt with no tie in sight.

Ada watched him from across the island. He looked ready enough to leave for the White House, but the expression on his face suggested he would rather be doing almost anything else. Nonetheless, the helicopter was coming in less than thirty minutes. "You're going to HQ looking like that?"

Leon looked down at himself. "What's that supposed to mean?"

“Come here." She held out her hand. "Bring the tie."

Leon looked at the tie sitting on the counter.

He picked it up and walked over with a frown cemented on his face. "Getting a little late for formalities."

She simply turned the bar stool toward him, letting him step between her knees. As she looped the tie around his collar. Her fingers moved with practiced precision, pulling the fabric into place and tightening the knot. Well aware of Leon looking down at her with all the bit of irritation he can muster while being in close proximity with her. Still frustrated. Ada adjusted the knot. "Tuck your shirt in."

Leon exhaled through his nose. "Yes, ma'am."

She gave him a faint look of amusement, but her hands remained gentle as she straightened his collar. “There." She looked up at him with a small smile. "Now you can go." Ada noticed Leon didn't move. "You'll miss your ride."

"Sounds tempting." He looked at her for another moment before glancing out toward the window. "I was looking forward to this weekend."

Ada's expression softened because she knew exactly what he meant. "I know." Their date, the one they'd finally managed to schedule. She reached up and adjusted his collar one last time, even though it didn't need adjusting. “The sooner you go the sooner you’ll be back.” she smiled faintly. "Maybe you can still make it."

Leon gave her a small, tired smile. "Yeah." Neither of them knew how much that promise would be tested.

"Just come back safe,"

"I will." He still didn't move.

Ada raised an eyebrow at the cheeky smile that curved his mouth. "What?"

“I think i deserve a kiss, don’t you think?”

She almost smiled, determined to keep a straight face. "You're getting late."

His smile widened, charming her unfortunately. "Never too late for a kiss."

Ada rolled her eyes, but there was warmth in them at she looked at him. Giving his tie a short tug as she leaned forward and kissed him. It was obvious Leon had decided It was too brief because when she pulled away, he stayed close for another second, stealing another kiss. "Be safe," she repeated.

"I will." This time, he stepped back with eyes full of guilt and headed toward the door. Ada watched him go until the doorway, he looked back to give her a soft smile anyway. "I'll be back before dinner."

She couldn't help but smile at that. "That's optimistic." she said, folding her arms.

He pointed at her. "Have a little faith."

Then he was gone. Ada remained at the island for a moment, staring at the closed door.

- At DSO Headquarters, Washington, D.C

"I was having a good week, Hunnigan," Leon said, his voice carrying that familiar, dry edge.

"I know. And I’m sorry," Hunnigan sighed, her fingers flying across her tablet. "But the Division of Security Operations panicked, which means you needed to be called in." She tapped the screen, and the massive monitor on the wall flickered to life with a picture of a middle-aged man. "Meet Dr. Antonio Taylor. He’s a heavy hitter at DARPA, specializing in advanced robotics and micro-drone technology."

Leon crossed his arms, staring at the face on the screen. "What’s a DARPA tech guy got to do with me? That sounds like a cyber-security problem."

"It became your problem an hour ago," Hunnigan explained, switching the screen to show a chaotic, grainy satellite feed of an interception on a highway. "Taylor possesses highly sensitive knowledge regarding autonomous weaponized delivery systems. An armed group just ambushed his transport, killed his security detail, and took him by force."

Leon’s eyes narrowed. He knew exactly what kind of people hijacked government scientists, and it never ended well. "Let me guess. Bioterrorists."

"Worse. We believe they targeted him specifically for his drone expertise to create an untraceable infection vector," Hunnigan nodded, pulling up a map with a blinking red tracking dot. "The kidnappers didn't stay hidden for long. Our intelligence network just intercepted the signal from the kidnappers' vehicle. They are moving fast." The digital map zoomed in, locking onto the crowded, hilly streets of California. "San Francisco," she said flatly. "They are transporting him through downtown right now. The local field office can't get teams there fast enough. You are the closest asset."

Leon stared at the blinking red dot over San Francisco. “I see.” The peace of his Manhattan apartment felt thousands of miles away now. The heavy weight of his reality settled right back onto his shoulders.

"The transport is prepped," Hunnigan added softly, her tone shifting from director to friend. "You fly out in twenty minutes."

- almost 2 hours later

Ada was halfway through reviewing a task she just received when her phone rang. She glanced at the screen. Leon. Her expression softened before she even answered. "Hey." There was a pause over the line, long enough that it made her glance at the screen to make sure the line was still connected.

"Ada…"

Something in his voice made her sit back, lowering the tablet in her hand. "What happened?" she asked.

"I got to HQ," he sighed. Ada already knew where this was going. "The situation is… bigger than I thought.”

She closed her eyes for a second. Of course. “I assumed,”

Leon sighed again, taking his time before saying; “I fly out in 10 minutes. San Francisco." Ada leaned back in her chair with a sigh, which Leon picked up. "Yeah." He sounded devastated. “I know.”

She knew he had been looking forward to this weekend. They'd been trying to get this date to happen for what felt like an absurd amount of time. Ada could practically hear him mentally calculating how many times they'd failed to make it to one dinner. "So," she said quietly, "the date."

"Yeah." He sounded even more miserable. "I'm sorry."

“You don't have to apologize."

"I was thinking maybe… If the assignment isn't too long, I could still make it back in time and..” Leon hurried on. "I mean, I don't know how long they're keeping me there, but if it's just a few hours, maybe I can get back tonight. Or tomorrow morning."

"Leon."

He cut her off. "I could take a different flight…. Listen I know this keeps happening."

She could hear the guilt in his voice now. "It's not your fault." She said again.

"It feels like it."

"It isn't." she countered.

"I asked you out before I left for New Jersey." he said. “This dates already twenty years over due! I could something—"

"Listen." She said, stopping his train of thought with a single word. He went quiet on the line, hopefully realizing that the date wasn’t happening. "I don't think this is going to work out."

.

.

.

Everything inside him stopped. Leon stared through the glass. For a second, he couldn't breathe. The words seemed to echo in his head. I don't think this is going to work out. His stomach dropped so suddenly it made him dizzy. "What?" There was silence on the other end. Leon swallowed. His throat had tightened. "I—" He cleared his throat and swallowed, but it did nothing to help. "I should've known." he said roughly. "I should've known this was going to happen."

"What?"

He looked down at the floor. The helicopter crew moved below, completely unaware that Leon's entire world had just tilted sideways. "The retreat." He swallowed again. "And then you were gone when I got back. — And then the move." His eyes burned when he blinked quickly. It hurt in that awful way it did when something got caught behind his ribs and he was trying desperately not to let it reach his eyes. "I know I've been putting this off." his head hung low, grasping the phone a bit tighter as he felt everything untangle. "I…I really wanted this to work." Leon squeezed his eyes shut. "Just..." His voice broke slightly cracking with his tightened chest, words struggling to come out. “I understand."

The words were a lie. He didn't know how he'd handle it. He didn't want to imagine walking back into his apartment where they had woken up together and seeing all of Ada's things there knowing she didn't want him anymore. Couldn't imagine waking up and not reaching for her. And the worst part was that they hadn't even officially started, yet somehow built their life around each other and their jobs that got in their way.

Ada finally spoke. "Leon." Her voice was softer now. "I am talking about the date."

He blinked. "...What?"

"The date."

Leon stared blankly at the window. "Tomorrow?"

"Yes." Ada continued. "Stop trying to make tomorrow happen." she said it softly, her voice going straight into his ear. “I don't want you to make it to the date all exhausted because you spent the last 48 hours dealing with bioweapons.”

Oh, God. For several seconds he couldn't bring himself to say anything, feeling as though his entire body sagged with relief at once. "Jesus Christ." Leon rubbed both hands over his face. "You said you didn't think it was going to work out."

"The date." she said. “You didn't let me finish.”

"I know that now." Leon groaned, his throat still felt tight. "I thought I'd screwed this up."

"Well, I'm sorry I scared you." Ada chuckled softly.

“More like, scared myself,” He laughed too. “Though I would prefer if you never say the words 'I don't think this is going to work out' to me ever again."

"I'll keep that in mind." Ada laughed softly. "I'm not going anywhere, Leon." Her voice was softer now. "Neither is the date. I gave you my word, didn't I?"

Leon closed his eyes, leaning his head against the wall behind him. “You did..”

"So I'll still be here when you get back,” she continued. "Then we’ll have our date."

Leon smiled. "Promise?"

"Promise." He hadn't realized how badly he needed to hear that.

With a stupid smile on his face he continued. “I didn’t know it was possible to cancel our first date before we've even had one."

Ada smiled again. "Technically, you haven't canceled it."

He shook his head. "That's generous."

"You've postponed it." She said.

"Again." He added, annoyed at himself. She could hear the little wince in the word. “you have every right to be angry,”

Ada leaned her head against the back of the chair. "You want me to be angry with you?"

He considered it, thinking of being on the receiving end of Ada’s anger, didn’t sound great. “...Maybe."

Ada laughed. “Seems unwise,”

"Don't let me get away with postponing it again." Leon continued, almost encouraging her.

“Why should I let you get away with it now?” Ada hummed thoughtfully. “I am mad at you.”

“You are?” For some reason, the admission sent a ridiculous little spark of warmth through him. She was actually mad at him. And hearing her say it so plainly made the whole thing feel strangely real. The thought sobered him almost immediately. He could hear the faint smile threatening his voice anyway. “I suppose I deserve that.”

“Mhm.” Ada sounded entirely too pleased with herself. “Mhm, In fact, I’ve decided that I don’t want to see you until we have that date.”

The smile disappeared. Leon went quiet, staring at the floor. "...Wait, what?" That was considerably worse than he'd anticipated. He'd been prepared for her to be irritated and maybe even give him the silent treatment for a few hours. Surely she didn't mean literally. She did. He could hear it in her voice. “But…” His mind immediately started scrambling for loopholes. He should've known better than to challenge Ada Wong when she was annoyed with him. “That's cruel.”

“I’m mad at you,” she chided.

Leon rubbed his forehead. Great. Already feeling the urge to plead his case. I'll make it up to you. Just let me come home. He could probably think of twenty more arguments in under a minute. None of them were going to work. All defeated, he leaned against the wall, looking down at his shoes. “So what, I'm supposed to go home to my empty apartment every night until this date happens?”

“Your apartment isn't empty, we spent a week arranging things.”

“Well, its still empty is my book if you're not there with me.” he pressed his lips together, knowing that would get a reaction out of her. And by the sense of all the quiet over the line he couldn’t stop a chuckle from escaping. “What? I sleep better at your place.”

She wasn’t having it. “Stop trying to charm your way out of the consequences you asked for.”

He could help but tease her. “Is it not working?”

All he received was a flat “No.”

“Tough crowd.”

Ada sighed over the line. “Go to San Francisco, Leon.” and stop bothering me. He imagined her adding the words, but he could hear the amusement underneath her irritation. She wasn't really trying to get rid of him, he knew the difference. And, frankly, he was already looking forward to making it up to her.

Leon let out a laugh, apparently loud enough that the pilot who was passing by, gave him a look.. “You’re right,” Leon said, “I should probably get ready to go, I’ve got like five minutes.”

“Then stop talking to me and cut the phone.” There was no bite behind it. If anything, he could practically hear the smile she was trying not to let into her voice.

He smiled at her words, despite the situation. “You're kicking me off the phone too?”

"You'll miss your ride."

“I fear they wont leave without me… well? Can I at least call you when I land?”

“You better.” Her voice softened, just slightly. “Be careful.”

His smile faded into something gentler. “I will.” He should have hung up. Instead, he stayed where he was, phone still pressed to his ear.

A brief silence that only lasted until Ada said, “Go.”

“Going.” He didn't.

“Now.”

“Okay.” he said, but made no move to cut the call, “Just making sure you don't miss me.” There was a pause that was cut very softly, with a laugh from Ada. It was brief—barely more than a breath of amusement. Click. She hung up. “That’s cheating.” he chuckled.

Even as the call ended, Leon still stood there for another moment, staring at the screen as it now displayed the calendar reminder again.

Upcoming: Dinner with Ada — 7:30 PM Tomorrow

He deleted it. Because there was no point keeping a reminder for a date that apparently had a personal vendetta against actually happening. He pushed away from the wall. "One day," he muttered. "One damn day."

-

The call had ended exactly when she'd wanted it to. She could still hear Leon's voice in her head saying: Just making sure you don't miss me. For someone who had spent the last several minutes insisting he needed to get on a helicopter, he had been remarkably reluctant to actually end the call. But then again, he’s always been like that, reluctant to let go of things first.

Ada set the phone down on the kitchen island and leaned back against her chair, glancing toward the empty stool beside her where Leon had been sitting that morning, barely awake, hair a mess, drinking coffee she’d made with a frown on his face. Same place where she fixed his tie. He really didn't want to go. She thought, a trace of fondness kissing her face at the thought of him. "Your job really doesn't like sharing you, does it?"

The apartment received her words quietly. She looked around the kitchen before turning to her laptop again. That was why she was here in the first place. Work. She pulled up the files she'd been reviewing before Leon called, skimming through the first few pages, her expression gradually becoming more focused. Paper trail linking the financial transfers between private holdings of shell companies.

Ada opened it with a quick code, her eyes moving across the information and halting abruptly at the words: based in San Francisco. A billionaire with connections to several of the shell companies she'd been tracking. Her next assignment was indeed in San Francisco. What a coincidence, but then again, it wasn't her first time being in the same place as Leon at the same time.

Ada leaned back slowly. "Well, Leon." For several seconds, she simply looked at the screen. "Looks like your luck isn't that bad after all." A small smile returning. "Apparently it can't even keep us in different cities."

With a shake of her head, she shut her laptop and stood up, slipping it into her purse. Ada's steps paused as she passed his jacket that he’d left hanging on the chair. “Consider this collateral, Leon.” Ada slipped into his jacket, tugging the sleeves down over her hands. She glanced at herself in the mirror, adjusting the collar. The memory of doing same thing back in Spain came back to her with a smile. “I'll keep it safe.”

Chapter 15: “Always thinking ahead, aren’t you Ada?”

“So, what’s the deal with this Dr. Antonio Taylor guy?” he asked, keeping his eyes on the footage Hunnigan just sent him.

“He’s wanted for espionage and aiding enemy states. San Francisco PD sent SWAT to his last location, but they were attacked by an unknown group with a lot of firepower.” she responded, typing something in the background. “Whoever these people are, they have Taylor now.”

Leon sighed to himself, the video showed a group of men dressed as ‘dry cleaners’ shoving taylor into the back of the van. “That can’t be good.”

“That’s an understatement. He was involved in top-secret research for our military. And now foreign agents are after him too. We need him in custody ASAP. Like, yesterday ASAP.”

“You love to rush me, don’t you?” he shook his head, raising his watch to check how much time he had left.

“We know the route the van took.” a loud screech of a vehicle caught his attention as Hunnigan continued: “If our calculations are correct, it should pass you in a minute or two.” The van, striped with an unmistakable orange the same one from the video, passed by the intersection.

“They’re early.” He said dryly, revving the engine as he ran the red light to tail the van.

Leon only caught up to them just as they entered the highway, he had to act fast. “The tires,” he said to himself, reaching for his gun at his back. Only to be rammed onto the side by another biker.

He looked over to see a woman with a black helmet. Deciding to give her the taste of her own medicine, he shoved her back, unfortunately, it didn't do much to deter her. He had to get his gun out, she dodged every bullet, catching him off guard with a wheelie where the tires scraped his thigh. “Fuck!”

Once they entered the tunnel, she decided she had enough of him and for a second he thought maybe she would just follow the van and leave him alone, but no. Instead, the heavy duty open-deck auto transport in front of him collapses its ramp, letting go of the cars on the top row. “Shit.” Coming at him at full speed, leaving him only time to duck out of way. “Good luck explaining that to insurance.” he quipped.

Once they were out of the tunnel Leon steadied his gun with one hand making sure the bullet struck her front wheel. “Gotcha.”

She jerked the handlebars, the damaged wheel wobbling beneath her before it tore off onto the road behind her.. With no other choice, she pulled into another wheelie, lifting the front tire completely off the ground as she raced alongside a heavy-duty car carrier.

Leon watched as she rode straight up onto the carrier's lowered ramp, disappearing briefly behind the vehicles stacked on its upper deck. He accelerated to get around it, barely having time to see her again before she launched herself off the carrier and straight onto his motorcycle. “What the—” She landed directly in front of him, her body blocking his view of the road.

There was no time to realise what she reached for as the bike screeched violently, pitching forward as he rolled onto the pavement. For a moment, he couldn't hear anything except the ringing in his ears.

When he pushed himself up and looked around, the woman was gone.

Staring at what remained of his motorcycle, his shoulders slumped. “Man, I loved that bike.”

He rubbed a hand over his face as he dialed Hunnigan in. “Kennedy. The bike was totaled on the freeway, send local support for the cars.”

“We’ll take care of it. Are you hurt?”

“Just a few scrapes and bruises—“ A sleek black car slipped through the traffic and slowed beside him, tinted glass sliding down. His brain stopped working for a second because he could see Ada behind the wheel. “Hold on.” He disconnected from Hunnigan, stepping closer to the car to get a better look.

There she was, Ada, wearing oversized sunglasses, one hand resting casually on the steering wheel as though she hadn't just driven through the aftermath of a freeway chase. "Need a ride, handsome?"

Leon just stared. "...Ada?"

She raised an eyebrow, a tiny smile curving her glossed lips. "I thought you were getting used to this."

His eyes did a back n forth between the sleek car and Ada behind the wheel wearing black to match it. "You—" He glanced toward the freeway behind her. "You're supposed to be in New York."

She smiled. "I was, except I had a change of plans." Ada glanced past him at the ruined motorcycle. “Are you getting in or not?”

Leon didn't move, he stared at her for another second, still trying to reconcile the fact that Ada Wong—who was supposed to be hundreds of miles away—was sitting in a car beside him on a San Francisco freeway.

“Alright, you’ve definitely hit your head.” She reached across and unlocked the passenger door. "Get in, you’re going to need a ride."

As he climbed in, he looked at her again. "I still don't understand." He said faintly, buckling the seat belt.

Ada pulled back into traffic. "You don't have to."

“Leon? Leon! Come in! Are you alright?” Hunnigan said in his ear after a moment.

“Hunnigan, I’m heading to the DSO center now,”

“Do you need me to send you a ride?”

“No, I’ve already got one apparently. A Good Samaritan” He glanced sideways at her, making her smirk faintly at that.

Hunnigan was silent for a moment. “Good Samaritan?”

“Long story.”

“I'll get the details later. We found something. The van was abandoned near the shore.”

Leon straightened slightly. “Abandoned?”

“Completely. No sign of Taylor or the people who took him.”

He looked out through the windshield, his expression turning serious. “Which means?”

“We think they're heading for Alcatraz.”

Leon sighed. “Of course they are.”

“Exactly. We'll have transport ready for you after your medical examination. Given that you were just involved in a motorcycle accident, you're going to need to get checked out before we send you anywhere.”

He glanced at Ada. She was watching the road, but there was the faintest hint of amusement on her face. “Fine,” he conceded. “I'll get checked out.”

“I'll contact you later.”

The call ended. For a few seconds, there was only the sound of the engine and the city moving around them.

Then her eyes traveled over the fresh scrapes along his arms, the dirt on his jacket, the bruise already beginning to darken across his shoulder.

"Next time?" She paused. "You should wear a helmet."

Leon turned toward her. A smirk tugged at his mouth."It ruins the hair."

Ada gave him a sideways look. "Of course." Lifting one hand from the steering wheel just long enough to reach over and slip into his hair, making him immediately go very still as he caught up with what she was doing. She carefully ran her fingertips along his scalp, checking for any swelling or bleeding, a shiver going straight down his spine when her nails lightly grazed his skin.

Leon swallowed, staring very determinedly through the windshield.

Ada moved her fingers again, parting his hair to inspect a small scrape near his temple. "Does this hurt?"

"No." Her fingertips shifted. The light drag of her nails sent another involuntary shiver through him. "I’m fine," he repeated, slightly less convincingly because his voice decided to crack.

Ada glanced at him. "You're tense." She examined the spot one last time before gently smoothing his hair back into place. "There.” She withdrew her hand. “I was just making sure you weren’t bleeding or anything.”

Leon exhaled, leaning his head back against the seat as he snuck a short glance at Ada who was already focused on the road again, completely unaware of what she'd just done to him. Thank God I'm not driving. He thought. Would’ve crashed twice.

She caught him staring. "What?"

"Nothing."

Ada narrowed her eyes. "You're smiling again."

Leon turned toward the window, unsuccessfully hiding it. "Just glad you're here."

She rolled her eyes. “I’m sure you are.”

Leon decided that he was perfectly happy to let her believe that was the only reason he was smiling.

“So.” She kept her eyes on the road. “What was the occasion for causing such a mess?”

Leon looked over. “What?”

“The freeway.” She said plainly, looking at the rear view mirror. “I’m sure you didn’t miss the multiple-car pileup in the tunnel, did you?”

The sarcasm made him smile. “Pleased to report it wasn’t me this time.”

“Mm.” Ada finally glanced at him. “So what happened?”

Leon hesitated, suddenly wishing Hunnigan had stayed on the phone longer. “There was a woman.” Ada's eyebrows lifted slightly at his words. “Crazy lady.”

Ada's expression remained perfectly neutral, obviously the requirement being to continue. Leon looked out the window. “She was chasing me.”

“Why?”

He sighed. “She clearly didn’t want me to follow the van.”

“No, I think she was trying to slow you down.” She said, “Or at least kill you so you wouldn’t be a problem later.”

“I don’t know, but the looks of which she kept trying to knock me off the bike? She would have been there for days. I tried to get around her, but she wouldn't back off.”

“And then?”

Leon paused, because this was where the story became difficult. “She jumped onto my motorcycle.”

Ada's eyebrows rose a little higher. “She what?” Turning to him briefly.

“She jumped onto my motorcycle.” Leon immediately added, “At full speed.”

Ada's expression became suspiciously amused. “On the front of the bike? So was she the one controlling it then?

“No...” Leon shifted uncomfortably. “She was... right there.”

“Right there? You mean she was facing you?” Ada glanced at him again. “So she was sitting on your lap.”

Leon stared at her, mortified at her conclusion. “That is not what happened.”

“It sounds like that's what happened.” There was this eerie tone to her perfectly calm voice.

He rubbed a hand over his face, giving up on making it sound any better than it was. “Well, it didn’t matter because then she grabbed the brakes and the bike pitched forward, and I went flying.”

Ada was quiet. “Interesting woman.”

Leon glanced at her. “Interesting?” He gave a short laugh. "That's one word for it. She's dangerous."

Ada's fingers tapped lightly against the steering wheel. “She’s bio-engineered.” She said, “which is why she doesn't care about getting hurt herself.”

Leon nodded in agreement. “Seems about right.”

“Where was she after the crash?”

“She was gone.” He said.

Ada stared ahead for a moment in silence then said something that caught him off guard: “Sounds like something I would do.”

Leon turned toward her. “Ada. You wouldn't do that,”

“You don't know that.” She gave him an innocent look. “I’d love to be the reason you crash.”

She'd done far more improbable things in her life, and if she really wanted to distract him while he was driving, Leon had a feeling she wouldn't need much more than a smile and a few well-placed words. He could already picture himself forgetting to operate a vehicle under the circumstances of Ada Wong as a distraction in his vicinity. I’d probably crash, he concluded pretty fast. He looked at her again, calmly taking a turn with a smile. She's batshit crazy. And also, why am I smiling? He probably shouldn't encourage this. “You know,” He said carefully. I’d prefer it if you didn’t test that theory.”

Her smile deepened. “You know I’m kidding Leon.” she said, adding a wink at him for no good reason.

“How reassuring.” He laughed under his breath. Yeah, good thing I’m not driving. “I'm not sure whether I should be concerned.”

“You should be.” Ada said casually.

Leon shook his head, looking back out the window, trying not to smile too much. “Noted.” He cleared his throat. “As I was saying, she was trying to kill me.”

"And apparently decided that you're worth chasing across San Francisco." She added. “That's quite the effort.”

He fought a laugh. “She wasn't exactly chasing me because she liked me.”

Ada’s brows faintly creased in offense. “I never said that,”

Except there was something in her voice. Something he knew better than almost anyone else. An almost invisible piece of possessiveness. Enough that Leon had to admit— He found it adorable and also that it was worth having a death wish. "You jealous?"

Ada didn't even look at him at first when she scoffed, but when she did, she gave him a look that knew he should probably stop talking. "Keep talking and I'll put you back on the road."

Leon laughed. "Okay, okay. Still mad at me I suppose." He leaned back in his seat, knowing better than to push it, still fighting another smile.

-

Leon was still smiling. She could see it from the corner of her eye even though she refused to look at him. "Stop smiling."

"I can't help it."

"You have five seconds to stop smiling." He grinned wider. Ada's fingers tightened around the steering wheel. "Four."

Leon turned toward her. "Ada."

"Three."

"You wouldn't."

"Two."

Leon laughed. "Okay, okay."

"One." She flicked on the turn signal.

Leon looked out the window. "You're actually going to leave me on the side of the road?”

"I picked you up off the freeway, I can put you back."

"Point taken." The car moved smoothly through the city. Leon glanced over at her. "So..." Ada didn't look at him, but she knew Leon was smiling again. "I'm guessing you didn't come to San Francisco for me, Though I'd be flattered."

She smirked. "You're not that important."

Leon choked into a laugh. “Wow.”

"It's true."

He laughed. "So why are you here?"

Ada was quiet for a moment. "I'm chasing someone."

Leon raised an eyebrow. "Someone?"

"A man." That made his expression change. "With too much money to spare.."

Leon stared at her. "A billionaire?" Sighed when she nodded. "Of course." She pressed a few buttons on the console, pulling up a picture of the man in question. She glazed at leon in the rearview mirror, he looked like he had decided her target had the sort of face that belonged on the cover of a magazine rather than in a classified intelligence file. "What exactly are you chasing him for?"

Ada nodded briefly. "He's been moving money through several shell companies. I'm trying to get access to his private records."

Leon frowned. "How?"

Ada looked at him. "I'm going to get into his house."

"...His house. How do you plan on getting in?"

"I have my ways." Ada kept her attention on the road, though she could feel his eyes on her. He was quiet, quiet enough that when she glanced at him she realized that Leon was connecting the pieces in the silence as his gaze had dropped briefly to her clothes. She'd dressed for the meeting. Simple black pencil skirt, black tights that matched her heels and the silk blouse she was wearing.

“Are you going to meet him after this or—”

“I just came from a meeting with him.” She corrected him.

Apparently, now that Leon had just realized that, his eyes immediately moved back to the road. Interesting. "So," he said after a moment. “ What's his deal?”

She raised an eyebrow. “His deal?”

“Yeah.” Leon looked outside. “What's he like?”

“Very well connected.” She said, suppressing a smile. “Intelligent.”

“Of course.” Leon nodded slowly, staring out the window with an expression of profound disinterest. “Sounds unbearable.”

Ada's lips twitched. “He's actually quite pleasant.” She looked at him briefly. “Even invited me to dinner.”

He turned to her in half a second. “You declined,” he said. It wasn't quite a question.

She fought a smile. “Naturally.” she couldn't help but chuckle quietly.

Leon looked pleased with himself for approximately two seconds before remembering he was supposed to be pretending not to care. “Good.”

Ada looked at him. “Good?”

“Yeah.” He shrugged. “Would've been a shame if you wasted a perfectly good evening with someone like him…” She smiled at the way he said someone like him. “Especially when you were supposed to spend it with me.” Leon mumbled.

“Mh hm.” she nodded, “but now we're both working.”

Leon sighed, slumping a little deeper into the seat with a fold of his arms. “Yeah.”

He looked genuinely disappointed. It was almost cute. “You'll see me again.” She said, hoping to reassure him just a little bit. Well aware of the fact that she was nearing the DSO facility.

“I know…” Leon glanced through the windshield. "So this is where you abandon me."

Ada pulled smoothly toward the curb. "For medical evaluation." She brought the car to a stop.

Leon looked at her, and she could tell by the look on his face he’d already been dreading getting out. She turned toward him soon realizing that he was already plotting something. "What?"

"Can I get a kiss?" Ada stared at him, her expression going completely flat as she remembered she was supposed to be mad at him. "Come on."

Ada sighed. "You've decided it's mandatory."

Leon smiled. "You make it sound like a bad thing."

"Maybe it is."

"It's not." Leon barely had time to smile before she kissed him. She had reached over and caught the front of his jacket, pulling him toward her. Just warm enough that when she pulled back, Leon looked entirely too pleased with himself.

Ada immediately regretted giving him the satisfaction. "I’m still mad at you." she patted his jacket, pushing him away.

Leon chuckled, catching her wrist. "You’re the one who kissed me."

“Only because you asked so nicely,” she said with a shrug. "It doesn’t mean anything.”

"Still counts." He said, looking down at his jacket. "You put something in my jacket didn’t you?”

She looked out the window, making sure he didn’t see her smile. "Something you'll need later." When she looked back at him, she schooled her expression. Leon shook his head, knowing better than to ask for an explanation. "And Leon?"

He looked back. "Yeah?"

"Change into something more practical." She gestured toward his battered leather jacket. "For my sake.”

The medical exam took longer than Leon wanted. Nothing that required more than some ice, rest, and the usual lecture about not treating motorcycles like disposable equipment because apparently the DSO has a tight budget on vehicles. He didn't know the details as he just had nodded through most of it. By the time he reached the DSO locker room, his shoulder was aching again, more than ready to get out of the battered leather jacket he'd been wearing since the crash. Leon opened his assigned locker taking out all the equipment he'd needed. His vest, holsters, and more importantly, weapons.

When he reached into his jacket pocket for his phone but instead, his hand brushed against something else. Paper. He pulled it out, figuring it was what Ada had slipped into his pocket. It was a folded note with a small device taped on it. “Huh?” A small smile appeared as unfolded the note and read it. Don't keep me waiting, handsome. She had written under what seemed to have been coordinates. Naturally, she had kissed it. Leon removed the device carefully, it was barely bigger than his thumb but it had one red button which he was not about to press until he read the other note she had written on the other side of the paper. Press the red button until the green light starts blinking. It’s an SOS device for emergencies only.

Leon stared at the note for a moment. “She must’ve been worried…” he said softly. He rubbed his thumb over the edge of the paper.

Over the years where they were mostly apart, Ada had always made sure he had what he needed. Whether it was a weapon, information, or just a way out. Now, a tiny emergency beacon that could call her to him if everything went wrong made it to that list. After everything they'd been through, he still wasn't entirely used to this side of her. “Always thinking ahead, aren’t you Ada?” He smiled faintly.

Leon turned the device over between his fingers. It was almost absurdly small. “Only in an emergency,” he murmured. He could practically hear her saying it. Don't press it just because you miss me. A quiet laugh escaped him before the quiet thought settled. He'd spent years wondering whether Ada cared, whether he'd ever know where he stood with her.

And now here he was, standing alone in a DSO locker room, holding something she'd slipped into his pocket mid kiss without saying a word.

He folded the note carefully, making sure the edges lined up before slipping it into the inner pocket of his vest, which would be tucked safely against his chest once he wore it. The SOS device went into the front zipped pocket of his tactical jacket where it could be easily accessed. As Ada had said: Something more practical, For my sake. Leon shook his head, a smile returning. “Bossy."

Time to work.

He shrugged out of the battered leather jacket and hung it on the hook. The bruises from the crash were already making themselves known, particularly around his shoulder, but nothing felt broken. Good enough. Leon pulled on his tactical vest, tightening the straps across his chest until it sat comfortably, checking each holster next, securing them against his thighs before sliding his weapons into place, muscle memory taking over.

The DSO facility's corridors were quieter than usual. Leon made his way toward the briefing area, still expecting someone to hand him a set of coordinates and point him toward a helicopter or boat.

Instead, Hunnigan's voice came through his earpiece. “Leon.”

“Yeah?”

“Your transport is ready.”

He kept walking. “Where?”

A brief pause over the line. “Head to the Dock.”

Leon stopped. “Dock?” He stared down the corridor. “Since when does the DSO have a dock?”

“We don't.”

He frowned. “Then what exactly is waiting for me?”

“A jet ski.”

“Hunnigan.” He rubbed his forehead. “You're sending me to Alcatraz on a jet ski?”

“It's the fastest way to get you there without drawing attention.” Hunnigan added. “You'll need to approach the island from the eastern side. There's an underground sewer access point.”

Leon sighed. “Of course there is.”

“Your entry route is marked on your map.”

“Anything else?”

“You'll need to leave the jet ski offshore and enter through the sewer.”

Leon looked toward the elevator. “Will do.” He started walking again.

“And Leon? Try not to destroy this one.”

He stopped. “Very funny.”

“I wasn't joking.” The line went dead.

Leon stared at his earpiece. “Everybody's a comedian today.”

A few minutes later, Leon stepped out onto the so-called dock. He looked toward the water, which was currently floating the jetski he was about to use.

It was almost insulting how familiar the setup felt, his mind briefly flashing back to Spain. And Ada standing there with that infuriating little smile as she held up a key she stole. Looking for something?

Leon almost laughed as he climbed onto the jet ski and started the engine, listening to it roar to life. He adjusted his grip. “Here we go.”

The file transfer reached eighty-seven percent. Ada leaned against the edge of the desk, scanning the information appearing across the screen as it transferred into her hard drive. Nothing particularly surprising came up. Her target had been careful, but not careful enough. That was until a name caught her attention. Umbrella. The name should have meant nothing now. “Why am I not surprised?” Ada's eyes narrowed as she scrolled back. Former U.S.S operative “Umbrella security service?” her fingers moved across the keyboard, opening another file. The connection was indirect. Money had moved between accounts belonging to shell companies controlled by her target and intermediaries connected to this one ‘Mr. Blake’ where had she heard that name before? She dug deeper finding encrypted correspondence containing a series of private transactions made over several months. All connected to one location: Alcatraz Island. All under the name: “Dylan Blake.”

It seemed like he wasn't simply some former operative hiding in retirement. He had been building something under Alcatraz. Ada's eyes moved rapidly through the emails until she saw a familiar name: Licker variation experimentations. “Lickers? Haven't heard of that since ‘98” she said, a ping catching her attention. The transfer was complete, which meant that it was her cue to leave.

But as she was clearing up her tracks she found a live, encrypted data pipeline beaming straight across the bay from Alcatraz Island. “Ofcourse.” She tapped her wireless earpiece, patching into the raw audio stream. Instantly, a wall of harsh static and subterranean echo flooded her ear.

The signal was terrible—buried deep underground. She leaned forward, resting her elbows on the mahogany desk and adjusting the audio filters on the screen to clear out the sound.

Through the heavy distortion, fragments of a distant conversation bled through the static. "Well……done it," a cold, commanding voice rasped. Dylan Blake, probably. "We …. prototype now. We can ….. straight to the m….production phase." Ada narrowed her eyes, pressing the earpiece deeper into her ear as the signal threatened to completely cut out. "Nice work… Dr. Taylor."

The line went dead, dissolving into pure white noise. “Taylor was kidnapped by Dylan..” Ada concluded that whatever was happening on that island was connected to the same network she'd just uncovered. And judging by the information in front of her, it was already underway.

She glanced toward the floor-to-ceiling windows. Alcatraz sat across the bay, almost peaceful from this distance; except, the fog was rolling in, and it seemed like a storm was on the way. And Leon was headed right in the middle of it. “Looks like I've got homework to do.”

Leon stepped onto the rocky shoreline of Alcatraz and secured the Jet ski where it wouldn't drift away. He checked his weapons one more time before looking toward the narrow opening built into the concrete wall ahead, he crouched slightly as he approached it, barely large enough to make the descent comfortable. A metal ladder disappeared into darkness below, the faint sound of running water echoing somewhere underneath the island. “Nothing says welcome to Alcatraz like a sewer.” He climbed down until his boots hit the damp concrete at the bottom, immediately reaching for his earpiece as he moved deeper underground. “Hunnigan, I’m in.” He winced when static disturbed the frequency, tapping the side of the earpiece before trying again. “Hunnigan, come in.” Leon glanced up at the concrete ceiling. “Of course…” he sighed. “Must be the underground interference.”

The air was colder down here. He moved forward, flashlight sweeping across the tunnel’s concrete walls. Leon kept one hand near his pistol as he walked, his footsteps echoing through the tunnel, for a second, the tunnel went silent except for the distant dripping of water from rusty pipes. A faint sound… Footsteps. He immediately killed his flashlight. Someone was coming from around the bend. He moved quietly toward the wall, positioning himself just before the corner.

The footsteps came closer. Before he knew it, a figure rounded the corner, his hand moved toward his holster, but before he could draw, a figure lunged around the corner with a knife in hand. Leon caught her wrist and used her momentum to roll her over his back, sending her toward the opposite side of the tunnel. She recovered quickly and came back at him, driving her shoulder into his chest.

He staggered but managed to catch her wrist before the knife could reach him, simultaneously as she grabbed his wrist, forcing his gun hand upward and over her shoulder. Leon blinked as adrenaline settled down, putting a name to this familiar face. When he relaxed his grip, her expression changed with recognition. “Leon?”

He stared at her for a moment before a grin appeared. “Well, what do you know? Jill Valentine. How’s it going?” Before they could even properly greet each other, an unnatural groan came down the tunnel, echoing through the walls, instantly catching both their attention. Leon furrowed his brows…. were those..? “So, uh, Jill, what are those things doing here?” Pointing at the guy haphazardly limping towards them.

“They were tourists.” She had positioned herself behind a knife, very well, knowing that these are the type of creatures that weren’t worth having close encounters with.

“That’s one fucked-up tour…. You got a gun?” He glanced.

“Nope. Lost it.”

Almost without thinking, he pulled the handgun out of his chest pocket rolling it through his fingers in a quick, practiced shuffle before extending it toward her. “Here.”

Jill took the handgun from him, giving him a slightly awkward look. “Thanks.”

Leon winced. Oh, for God's sake. It was a stupid little shuffle he'd picked up from Ada a while ago, and apparently his hands had decided now was the perfect time to remember it. He'd never admit it, but she had made him annoyingly fond of the trick, though Ada would probably have made it look better. Thankfully the several zombies staggering toward them were enough of a distraction, forcing both of them to raise their weapons. They fired together, dropping the infected as they backed themselves farther into the tunnel. That was except when something splashed in the water. Just in another second, one of the zombies suddenly dragged backward by something beneath the surface. He recognized that slithering flesh anywhere. Lickers. “Uh-oh.”

A half-chewed body flew out of the water and landed right in front of them, causing Jill to drop to the ground, startled, as he crouched himself with an instinctive hand on her shoulder pulling her as they slowly backed away. As a licker emerged from the water glistening flesh with some sort of a mutation that he couldn’t name. What the hell ? Its exposed brain glistened beneath the dim light as it passed in front of them. They were practically pressed up against the wall remaining perfectly still as the licker stopped in front of Jill. He couldn’t help with squint as the lickers tongue extended and faintly grazed Jill's leg. She moved her head back as its tongue almost reached her jaw. For a moment, it seemed as though they had gone unnoticed, that was until a metal piece from a gas canister fell right in front of Jill with a sharp clatter. Fuck.

The Licker immediately growled standing up to attack only to be kicked by Leon, grabbing Jill by the elbow. “Move!” They ran down the tunnel as more Lickers emerged from the water. They both fired over their shoulders as the lickers started crawling towards them at an uncanny rate; all over the ceilings, the walls, some even in the water. Jill fell, and when he turned around sharply, he saw that a Licker had wrapped its tongue around her ankle, dragging her backward. “Leon!” She said, sliding across the wet concrete toward the creature.

Leon sprinted back, leaping forward to get a hold of her hand. “Hold on!” He pulled her toward him while firing at another Licker approaching from the side. The creature dropped, but before Leon could aim at the one holding Jill, a cold wet rubbery muscle slapped against his wrist, wrapping around his hand from above, pulling it away.

Jill saw it first. “Duck!”

Leon dropped his head as she fired narrowly from his head, at the Licker clinging to the ceiling. One his hand was free, he immediately turned his gun toward the one still holding Jill and fired several shots. It finally released her, collapsing into the ground. “Let’s go.” He huffed, grabbing her arm and pulling her upright. They were running, again. This time the number of Lickers behind them seemed to increase with every step, forcing them to fire whenever they had an opening.

Jill glanced behind them. “How many of these are there?!”

Leon kept running. “I ain’t stopping to count!”

Once they finally reached the end of the tunnel, where a passage opened to the left. They stopped before the turn and looked back. The Lickers were pouring toward them from every direction, crawling through the water and climbing over one another to reach them. “They’re faster than I remember!” He said over the sounds of the shots, shooting at whatever was closest. Thankfully, he spotted another one of those gas canister near the wall, immediately pushing Jill toward the left as he kicked the canister into the tunnel. “Move!” The bullet struck the canister mid air, a huge explosion following soon after juts in time he tucked himself around the corner himself. The explosion tore through the sewer, sending flames and debris down the tunnel.

Eventually, Leon lifted his head and looked back toward the smoke-filled tunnel, only the smell of gasoline occupying it. “Zero.”

Jill looked at him, breathless. “What?”

Leon glanced over. “You wanted to know how many there were.”

Jill stared at him before rolling her eyes. “Oh.”

“Looks like we're clear.” He said his voice echoing. The explosion had left the tunnel eerily quiet. “Let's keep moving.”

They continued through the passage, leaving the sewer water behind as the tunnel gradually changed. The cramped concrete walls gave way to a wider corridor with old brickwork and heavy structural supports. Leon swept his flashlight across the musty walls. The place looked old.

Jill looked around. “What even is this place?”

“Thought you’d never ask,” he smirked. “It’s to move munitions, back when Alcatraz was a military fortress.” He swept his flashlight over a section of the wall. “Most of the original fortifications are buried under what's here now. But the tunnels, magazines, and some of the old defensive structures are still around.”

Jill gave him a sideways look. “Thanks for the history lesson.”

“You'd be surprised how much useless information you can absorb on a plane when your morning was stolen from you.” Leon continued walking. He knew things and he wasn't particularly happy about knowing them. “So after this, I’m putting tour guide on my résumé.”

Jill continued beside him, nodding slowly. “Right.”

Leon glanced over, she was staring straight ahead. “...That was a joke.”

“I know.” Her tone suggested that was precisely the problem.

Leon sighed. “Okay.” He decided not to explain himself.

After another moment, Jill finally spoke. “Why are you here?”

Leon straightened slightly. “Dr. Antonio Taylor. He’s a specialist in robotics engineering. He tried to sell his research to another country, but got kidnapped by a bunch of terrorists. Intel said they brought him to Alcatraz.” He glanced toward Jill. “What are you doing here?”

“A new virus is on the loose. Clues pointed here, so me, Chris and Claire came to have a look around.”

Leon frowned. “Claire? What’s she doing down here?”

“TerraSave located a whale that apparently had odd bite marks and contained a strain of T- Virus in it.”

Leon slowed. “Our cases seem to be connected.” His brow furrowed as he looked down the corridor.

Jill noticed. “What’s wrong?”

He tilted his head slightly, raising his hand. “You feel that?”

“What?”

“There’s a draft.” He turned toward the wall. “It’s coming from here.” he pushed the blocked further into the wall, and surprisingly slid with ease.

Jill stepped closer, examining the old passageway. “We don’t know where it goes. Maybe outside or farther into this maze.”

Leon shrugged. “Either way, it’s better than here. Come on. It’ll be like we’re breaking out of prison. It’ll be fun.”

She stared at him for a good second. “You really think this is fun?”

Leon started toward the opening, he glanced back to see she was following. “Not yet.”

Jill sighed. He turned forward again shaking his head. Definitely not his best audience.

The tunnel gradually widened. Leon slowed as they approached it, stepping carefully through the opening which emerged into a massive industrial chamber. A huge body of water stretched through the facility, surrounded by metal walkways, platforms, and old military infrastructure. Large equipment occupied different sections of the dock, while cables and pipes ran along the walls. And scattered throughout the facility were things that had absolutely nothing to do with an abandoned military fortress. Laboratory equipment.

Jill stepped beside him. “This is not good.”

“At least we know we’re on the right track.” he shrugged, walking ahead.

“Noo. Ya’ think?” she said.

The sarcasm wasn't lost on him, but he decided to ignore it, nodding instead. “Yup. Come on.” She followed, apparently deciding that correcting him wasn't worth the effort.

As they approached the edge of the dock, Jill looked toward the massive structure built into the far side. “What’s a submarine dock doing here?”

Leon studied it. “Probably another holdover from when this was a fortress. They’re using it for an armory now. Let’s check it out.” They moved toward the upper section of the facility, climbing the metal stairs until they reached a heavy metal door that stood ahead, its surface scratched and rusted around the edges, though the electronic lock beside it looked considerably newer. “Looks like someone did a little renovating” he said, examining the panel.

“Someone's been maintaining this place.” Jill glanced at the wiring running into the wall. “Recently.” She pushed the door open, and to his surprise, it did in fact open, with no keycard or puzzle bullshit this time.

A section of the wall beside them shifted, revealing a narrow metal elevator. Leon stared at it. “Well, that's convenient.” Jill stepped inside first, keeping her weapon raised as she checked the corners. Leon followed and pressed the only button. The doors slid shut, immediately beginning to rise with movement almost unnervingly smooth compared to the rest of the facility. Whatever this place had once been, someone had spent a considerable amount of time converting it into something else.

“Where do you think it goes?”

“Guess we're about to find out.” Once the doors opened, enormous glass windows that overlooked the entire dock immediately greeted them as they walked inside, from up here, they could see almost everything. Including, the unmistakable signs that someone had been using this old military facility for something far more disturbing than storing weapons. “That's one hell of a view.”

Jill walked toward the glass. “Must be used as some sort of observation and command center.”

Leon scanned the rest of the room nodding. Banks of monitors lined the walls, several displaying live camera feeds from different sections of the facility. Others showed schematics, security systems, and various status readouts. In the center of the room sat a large metal desk covered in equipment. Multiple monitors were arranged across it, along with keyboards, communication terminals, security controls, and what looked like an entire surveillance system dedicated to monitoring the dock below and the entirety of the island. “Someone's been busy.”

“Looks like they're monitoring everything down there.”

When he leaned closer, one of the monitors caught his attention: A data of the water system appeared on the screen, with several sections highlighted in red.

Jill moved beside him. “What are those?”

Leon studied the display. Small icons were moving through the underwater system they had just passed through. “Bio-drones.” he nodded toward the monitor. “That room we passed through before is the incubation tank. The guy I’m here for, Antonio Taylor, this is his area of expertise.” He zoomed in on the display, watching the number of active units. “And these drones look ready to go. So he’s gotta be around here somewhere.”

Jill looked back through the glass at the dock.“He can wait. Let’s burn it all.”

“Yeah. We’ll start with the incubation tank-”

A crackle suddenly came through the speakers, a voice filled the room. “Well, if it isn’t Jill Valentine and Leon S. Kennedy!”

The pair of them immediately shifted back-to-back, each covering the opposite side of the room as Leon's eyes swept across the monitors and darkened corners with his raised gun. “Well, if it isn’t… whoever you are.”

Jill kept her weapon trained ahead. “Why don’t you come out and talk?”

The voice chuckled faintly through the speaker. “How about you come to me? I’m in the main prison block. Ground floor. Chris and Claire are waiting for you.”

-

Ada left the penthouse with everything she needed. The connection to Dylan Blake was no longer a theory, and the information she'd found about Alcatraz had given her more questions than answers. Once she checked into her hotel room that conveniently was not so far away from where the Alcatraz tour boats would depart, she set her bag down on the table and opened her laptop. Her first priority was Leon.

Though she had told Leon to wear a helmet to drive safe, she had tried his phone again on the drive over. Not once did his phone even ring, let alone be picked up.

“If you're not going to answer your phone…” She connected a small interface to her laptop. “…I'll find another way.” Ada opened the equipment she'd brought with her and connected it to her laptop. His earpiece was encrypted, but she'd already encountered DSO communication protocols before. She wasn't trying to break into their entire network. She just needed his channel. “Come on, Leon.”

She worked through several layers of security, bypassing one after another until a faint indicator appeared on the screen. The system finally gave her a partial connection.

Ada leaned closer, there was some sort of an error; she hadn't managed to establish a direct line to Leon, but she'd intercepted the channel feeding information to his earpiece, only enough to hear what Hunnigan was saying to him. “Leon? Leon, come in. Do you copy?” Hunnigan repeated. “Leon, this is Hunnigan. If you can hear me, respond.” except other than her voice, there was nothing but static. Hunnigan tried again. “Leon? Come in.” More static, evidently clear that he wasn't receiving them. Hunnigan continued trying for several seconds before finally giving up. “Still nothing.” Ada listened carefully as Hunnigan switched channels.

“Did you reach him?” a man said on the line, probably a superior.

“Negative. Agent Kennedy is currently comms-MIA. FOS is actively attempting to re- establish a secure link, I’ll update his status the second he checks in.”

“Check his location.” he said.

“Yes sir, Telemetry indicates his last known position was entering the subterranean infrastructure beneath the Alcatraz complex.”

Underground Alcatraz?

The superiors voice became muffled for a moment until he returned onto the line. “It was just in that the Department of Defense was breached. Notify them immediately.”

A breach?

“I'm going to send this information to Rebecca Chambers. Hopefully she can reach Chris.”

Ada's fingers stopped above the keyboard. Rebecca. The S.T.A.R.S medic all the way back from spencer’s reign. This keeps getting more complicated.

A moment later, Hunnigan made the call. “F.O.S., this is Hunnigan.”

A woman's voice answered. “Hey, I’m Rebecca. So Leon’s working with you on this?”

“Yes, but I still can’t reach him, so let’s save the chitchat. Did you look at the info I sent you?”

“Yeah, I checked it out.” Rebbecca said. “But I’m having no luck getting through to Chris and the others either. I’ve been trying for two hours now. What do you want me to tell them?”

Ada's eyes narrowed. Two hours. If everyone was having the same problem, that wasn't interference.

“Someone accessed the DoD’s server. They were looking for top-secret files on previous bioterrorism cases, going back to Raccoon City.” Hunnigan continued.

Ada went still at the mention of Raccoon city, she already knew what kind of information would be buried in those files.

Rebecca spoke again. “You think this might have something to do with the op we’re on?”

“Most likely. They managed to steal data on key personnel on those cases: Leon, Chris, Jill, and Claire.”

Rebecca sounded confused. “That’s oddly specific…why would they do that?”

Ada reached over and disconnected the earpiece interface, silence falling in the room around her. “Because they're looking to infect them specifically.” For a moment, she simply stared at the dark outline of Alcatraz across the bay. Leon, Chris, Jill, Claire. Someone had gone through the trouble of obtaining their histories, their vulnerabilities, their identities. “It’s a trap.”

There was no point staying where she was. If Leon was already on the island, she needed to get there. She picked up the landline and called the concierge. “Good evening, I'd like to take one of the Alcatraz tours this evening.”

“I'm afraid the tours have been suspended for the rest of the day, ma'am.” he said sympathetically.

Ada's eyes narrowed. “Suspended?”

“Yes. There was some kind of incident on the island. We haven't been given many details.”

“When did they stop running?” Ada glanced toward the clock.

“They closed departures at ten am this morning actually. The final tour was supposed to leave at six, but all remaining trips were cancelled.”

It was four o'clock. “Are they saying when they'll reopen?”

“Nothing official yet, they said there seems to be an issue with the infrastructure.” he said, “would you like me to read the notice?”

“Yes?”

“‘Alcatraz Cruises regrets to inform all passengers that all remaining departures for today have been cancelled until further notice. During a routine safety inspection this morning, engineering staff discovered a critical structural failure in the main docking pier coupled with a major subterranean water main break on the island. Due to the immediate loss of fresh water and potential instability of the boarding areas, the National Park Service has issued an emergency closure order for public safety. Full refunds will be processed automatically. We apologize for the inconvenience.’ ”

“I see.” Ada thanked him and hung up. For a moment, she stood silently in front of the window with a sigh. “Infrastructure failure, seriously?” yeah right. “I guess I’ll have to find another way.”

Chapter 17: “Stay with me… please.”

Chapter Notes
