Dylan’s big long speech

The hotel rooftop gave her a clear view of Alcatraz sitting beyond the water, dark against the evening sky, and from this distance if she didn’t know better, it looked almost peaceful. She crouched beside the equipment she had arranged across the rooftop, adjusting the receiver connected to her laptop while a compact signal analyzer filled the screen with encrypted traffic. She had already tried three different frequencies, but Leon's earpiece remained inaccessible..

The DSO's communications equipment was hardly primitive, and with the right equipment she should have been able to reach his channel. Unless something was interfering with it. Ada glanced toward the island, concluding there was a jammer involved. It explained the silence, and if Dylan Blake had anticipated outside interference, it was exactly the kind of precaution she would have expected from him.

"Of course." Ada closed the connection and reached for her laptop instead. If she couldn't warn him directly, she would watch. Her laptop chimed as a new network had appeared: Alcatraz's internal surveillance system. The first few cameras showed empty corridors, locked doors, and stretches of concrete that told her nothing. She moved through them quickly until one feed stabilized.

Leon by the bars of a cell which looked like it contained Claire and some other guy. She routed the camera's audio through her headset, and static filled her ears before the voices became clear. "Son of a bitch. Antonio Taylor." Ada's eyes moved to Leon's image.

"What?" Claire asked, visibly struggling to breathe."His name's Davis."

"No. That's a fake name. He's Taylor, all right."

The man— well, Taylor looked startled. "How did you...?"

Leon stepped closer to the cell. "This scumbag's wanted for leaking national secrets to the enemies of the U.S. of A."

So that was what this was about. The stolen data had been part of Taylor's involvement after all.

Then another voice entered the feed. "Welcome to Alcatraz."

Ada went still as Dylan Blake appeared on another camera angle. "I'm Dylan Blake. It's an honor to have you all here. Together. I bet you're wondering how people are being infected without being bitten." Ada's attention shifted immediately back to Leon when he jerked, covering the back of his neck. "That was one of my prototype bio-drones. I tell it who to infect, and it always finds its target."

Ada's breath caught as she watched him sink to one knee. "No." She said leaning closer to the screen.

Dylan continued speaking through her headset. "The mass-production versions are what you saw in the other chamber, and they're ready to go. By tomorrow morning, my virus will be all over the world."

Ada's jaw tightened as she watched Leon try to steady himself.

Jill's voice came through the speakers. "Leon?"

Someone dropped down from the upper level, recognizing the woman who had been chasing him on the free way. She watched her land and turn toward Leon as he weakly looked up and raised his weapon to her.

So this was her.

Maria approached him with a smile before kicking the gun from his hand. It skidded across the floor, and before Leon could recover, she struck him across the face, collapsing against the bars of Chris's cell.

Ada's fingers tightened around the edge of the laptop as Dylan continued explaining himself. "I thought this was a good opportunity to settle the score. For both of us. Right now, they're probably cheering us on from the afterlife."

Leon looked toward Maria again. "I get it now. The tech... the virus... you got them from Arias. That's why she's here."

Ada's eyes shifted toward the other camera as Dylan confirmed it. Then came the speech… ugh. The accusations against the B.S.A.A., the D.S.O., and TerraSave. The corporations. The executives. The corruption. The claim that the people they were supposed to protect were the ones continuing to suffer.

Ada had heard variations of the argument before. She knew exactly how easily legitimate failures could be twisted into something darker.

"We... We protect... the innocent." Chris spoke, his voice rough with pain.

Dylan laughed. "Hardly."

Ada barely listened anymore, zoning out Dylan’s voice as she focused her attention on Leon; He had one hand against the floor now, and his breathing was getting worse. Even through the grainy surveillance feed, she could see the darkening veins beneath his skin. His shoulders tensed as another wave of pain passed through him, and his breathing became increasingly labored.

"What do I know?” Dylan's voice came through again. "Confusion. Betrayal. Guilt."

“God, his voice is annoying.” She said pushing herself upright and moved back toward the rifle, leaving the laptop running behind her. Dylan's voice continued through the headset as she walked, his speech carrying across the rooftop while she adjusted the scope and checked the harbor beyond it.

"I know I'm sick of this world and how the ones with all the money and power get to decide what's right and wrong, good and evil." That part, unfortunately, she understood. The people with money and power did decide far more than they should. Governments decided what counted as justice. Corporations decided what could be bought, buried, or quietly erased. People who had never been anywhere near the consequences of their decisions could sit behind polished desks and determine who was expendable. Ada had spent enough of her life moving between those worlds to know that Dylan wasn't entirely wrong. "There’s no such thing as justice in a world like that." She adjusted the scope again as Dylan continued. "So I'm going to use my virus to clean the slate."

"Give me a break." Ada rolled her eyes, she just couldn't believe that was where he had decided to take the argument. She had no interest in listening to another man with a god complex explain why the world needed to be destroyed so that he could rebuild it according to his own vision.

Wesker had already done this particular routine. Dylan was simply less polished.

Ada crouched beside the rifle and opened a small compartment on the side of her equipment case. Inside was a compact reconnaissance drone, no larger than her palm. She attached it to the launcher and brought up the control interface on her tablet. If the jammer was strong enough to interfere with the DSO frequency, it would be emitting a localized signal. She couldn't reach Leon's earpiece because the interference was overpowering the connection, but the interference itself could be tracked and that’s exactly what Ada was planning to do once she’d activated the drone.

—

"I know I'm sick of this world and how the ones with all the money and power get to decide what's right and wrong, good and evil. There's no such thing as justice in a world like that. So I'm going to use my virus to clean the slate."

Leon stared at him through the pain, managing a bitter laugh. "Sure. Kill everything. Like a terrorist."

Dylan shook his head. "Not everything. With these bio-drones, I decide who gets infected and who doesn't. Example, Ms. Valentine. I left her alone so she could experience what I went through so many years ago, when I thought I was on the side of justice."

Yeah, justice and Umbrella? You should have known better. Leon glanced toward Jill as Dylan talked about control. That was what it always came down to. People like him convinced themselves that the world was broken, then decided that gave them the right to become the ones who determined who deserved to live in it.

Jill's expression hardened. "You're insane."

Dylan ignored her. "Instead of pointing your gun at me, you should be worried about him, trapped in that cell with Claire. She's not looking too good. It won't be long before Claire turns. And when she does, she'll rip the not-so-good Dr. Taylor's guts out. And another innocent life will be lost."

Leon looked toward Claire who was barely holding herself together.

Taylor's voice came from behind the bars. "You heard him. Shoot her before she kills me!"

Chris reacted immediately. "Goddamn you!"

Leon tried to push himself upright, but his arms shook beneath him. The infection was making everything harder. His muscles felt heavy, his vision occasionally blurring at the edges, but he forced himself to stay conscious.

Dylan's voice continued. "Here we have Leon S. Kennedy, working hard for people who lie and cover up the truth, who propagate the endless cycle of battles he keeps fighting."

The words should have rolled off him. Instead, one thought surfaced before he could stop it. Ada. He thought about all the times she had told him that the people giving him orders weren't necessarily the people telling him the truth. About how many times he'd walked into another operation knowing there was more to the story than anyone was willing to admit.

"And you're all burnt out from it." Dylan continued.

Leon swallowed against the dryness in his throat. "Yeah, well, it's a living." It was the only answer he had. Because what else was he supposed to say? That Dylan was wrong? That Leon hadn't spent years wondering whether any of this actually changed anything? He'd been doing this too long to pretend the system was clean. But that didn't mean Dylan was right.

Dylan turned his attention toward Claire. "And Claire Redfield. You think your group helps people. But they don't do anything to stop the actual cause of all the suffering. They offer bandages instead of real solutions." Dylan waved his hands. "I bet you keep doing it out of pride. Because it makes you feel like you're doing good."

Claire's voice was weak. "Nothing wrong... with helping people."

"Sure. Keep telling yourself that. We both know you prefer sitting back and letting others do the dirty work."

Leon frowned, He knew Claire. She had spent her entire life putting herself between innocent people and the things trying to hurt them. She didn't need anyone's approval for it.

Dylan continued before anyone could answer. "And Chris Redfield. Despite the fact you lose team members on every single mission, you keep coming back with more. How many lives have you sacrificed in the name of justice, I wonder."

That was unnecessary. For once, Chris didn't have anything to say. Leon knew that look. He had worn it himself. The weight of names that never stopped following you. Faces you remembered even years later. People you had promised you'd bring home and couldn't.

"And finally... we come to Jill Valentine." Jill's expression remained cold. "You could shoot Claire right now and save a life, but you hesitate. I get she's a friend and all, but still, I thought you want to protect the innocent. That's hard to do when you let your emotions get in the way, isn't it?"

Jill didn't even hesitate this time. "Go fuck yourself." Leon almost smiled. He would’ve if his lungs let him breathe without pricking him with pain.

Dylan into her. “Like all of you, I did my duty, protecting the sons of bitches in power. The ones who forced me to watch my best friend beg for his life! And then ordered me to kill him anyway!" Dylan's voice had changed, the anger underneath it was no longer hidden. "None of you know what it's like to live with that hanging over your head, do you?" Leon knew that feeling. Maybe that was the part Dylan wanted them to understand. The feeling that no matter what choice you made, someone was going to die. He knew it, too well. But Dylan had taken that pain and turned it into an excuse. "But soon..." he said, "you will know that kind of suffering."

Leon looked up. "My bio-drones will deliver it to the world."

The words settled over the room. Leon could hear Claire's breathing behind him, Chris shifting beside the cell. Jill's grip tightening around the gun he’d given her. And underneath all of it, the pounding of his own heartbeat.

Dylan smiled. "And when people start killing their friends and family, there won't be any more cover-ups."

Leon froze when the room disappeared into his mind. He saw Ada, as she had been in every nightmare he'd had about losing her. He forced the image away. No. He couldn't let his mind go there. But Dylan's voice continued. "And there won't be any more use... for you."

Leon looked at him through the haze. It was the thought of surviving long enough to become the thing that killed someone else. Someone he couldn't bear to lose.

He looked down at his hands. The skin along his arms had begun to darken beneath the surface, veins standing out in places they shouldn't, trembling against the floor, as he felt something inside him changing, moving deeper, settling into his body.

Once the virus took control, the person inside would disappear beneath it. Leon swallowed at the thought, his stomach turning as Ada returned to his thoughts, it came so quickly and violently that Leon almost flinched.

He forced his eyes shut for a moment, but that only made it worse. He could see her clearly: The way she'd stood in his apartment that morning, fixing his tie while telling him he was going to be late. Be safe. He'd promised her he would. Leon opened his eyes again, his hand moving instinctively toward his chest. A stupid little piece of paper that, only hours ago, had meant something incredibly simple. Come find me.

Leon felt his throat tighten. The paper in his pocket suddenly seemed unbearably heavy, the thought wasn't just that he could infect her. What if he looked at her face and saw nothing familiar?

The idea that Ada could become meaningless. Leon shook his head sharply. "No." That was the part he couldn't accept. Whatever was happening inside his body, whatever the virus wanted to turn him into, he couldn't let make Ada a stranger.

His hand trembled toward his jacket. The little device was still there. Something you’ll need later. Leon fumbled with it as his fingers wouldn't cooperate, holding it tighter when he nearly dropped it. "No..." For one brief second, he imagined her again, tying his tie.

come back safe.

Leon hesitated, his throat tightening because pressing it meant admitting what he had been refusing to accept. He might not make it out.

The device sat under his thumb quietly for a moment before he pressed the button. Receiving a tiny confirmation pulse.

As long as he could still think of her, he was still himself.

—

Her drone lifted silently from the rooftop and disappeared into the darkness. She watched its telemetry as it moved toward the island, allowing the signal strength to guide it. The farther it traveled, the weaker the interference became. Ada narrowed her eyes, watching it through her binoculars. The drone altered its course and moved lower, skimming along the shoreline before circling the eastern side of the island.

The signal spiked again. Ada brought the rifle to her shoulder, following the drone's feed on the small display mounted beside the scope. The image was grainy, but she could make out a structure partially concealed behind the concrete perimeter. A communications relay. Someone had installed the jammer close enough to the prison to blanket the area.

Ada adjusted the magnification until the relay came into focus. "Got you." The shot cracked across the rooftop, the relay erupting in a brief flash of sparks. The interference dropped on the telemetry but it was slow, not immediate. She allowed herself a small, satisfied breath. "Much better."

When she glanced toward the laptop again, Dylan was still talking, Of course he was. Ada had left the headset behind, but the surveillance feed continued to show his mouth moving while he delivered what appeared to be another endless explanation of humanity's failures. She had no intention of putting the headset back on.

Her attention drifted instead toward the harbor as something moving across the water caught her eye. Ada lifted the scope again as it adjusted on a boat approaching Alcatraz from the dock. Several figures were aboard, dressed in tactical gear. SWAT. Her gaze moved to the center of the group, and though Ada had never met her, she knew enough about the B.S.A.A. to recognize the woman immediately: Rebecca Chambers, holding something resembling a briefcase close to her chest.

Ada kept her eye on the boat as it cut through the water toward Alcatraz, given everything happening inside the prison, she could make a reasonable guess about what might be inside it. She tracked the boat until Rebecca and the SWAT team reached the island, watching them disembark and move toward the wooden dock.

All things were going fine until she noticed something beneath the water. Ada switched to a thermal lens, which at first, only showed a distortion in the heat signature. She adjusted the scope until the shape became an unnaturally large marine animal moving beneath the surface directly toward Rebecca.

Ada caught the first flash of light through the scope. Muzzle flashes flickered along the shoreline near Alcatraz as the SWAT team opened fire at something moving through the water. Ada adjusted the magnification and followed their line of fire until a massive shape broke through the surface. A shark. Its body surged toward the dock, forcing the SWAT members to scatter as they continued firing. The rounds struck its hide, but the creature barely slowed. It lunged toward the nearest man, catching him before he could get out of the way.

Ada's eyes narrowed as she watched him disappear between its jaws, she saw his hands move. Two grenades. Ada understood what he was doing a second before the explosion tore through the water. The blast lit up the harbor and sent a column of spray into the air. For several seconds, the thermal scope showed nothing but heat and debris.

Determined to make sure the shark stayed dead, she kept watching. The creature began to move again, its body badly damaged, but the wounds were already closing. Torn flesh pulled itself together with unnatural speed as the shark turned back toward the dock.

Ada adjusted her aim and fired letting the shot go directly through his eye into its head. The fog over the bay was thick, but the heat signature of the creature moving beneath the black waves now, still not dead. "Hold still," Ada murmured, her finger tightening on the trigger. She exhaled, pausing her breath between heartbeats, and fired again.

The heavy rifle kicked against her shoulder followed by a massive geyser of black blood and water erupting. The creature finally screeched and sank back into the abyss of San Francisco’s waters.

She used her binoculars once again and followed Rebecca’s silhouette until it vanished behind the rocky surface. It’s a good thing she made it. She couldn’t say the same to the SWAT members.

Ada set the binoculars down and turned back toward her laptop when she felt the receiver of the SOS device clipped on her harness flash in warning.

She connected the received to her laptop seeing the biometric feed populate across her screen. Her eyes moved rapidly across the readings, taking in the numbers as they updated in real time. His heart rate was dangerously elevated. His oxygen saturation was falling. His breathing was shallow and irregular. All of it was wrong.

Ada's hand tightened around the edge of the desk. "No." She opened the DSO frequency and began forcing the system through the remaining interference. The jammer was gone. So why wasn't she getting through? "Come on."

When she glanced at the cameras to get a look at the situation, Dylan was still talking. "You waited too long, Jill. I'm disappointed." A gunshot rang through the prison, Taylor collapsed, blood blooming across his shirt.

Jill shouted, "You bastard!"

Before she could raise her weapon again, Maria moved in and knocked it from her hands. “I should have gotten rid of you when I had the chance,” Ada said to herself, her attention shifting immediately to Leon when he yelled: "Jill!"

In the next second, everything on screen turned white, she took off her head set before the ringing damaged her ear. “What did you do, Leon?”

The connection attempted to establish. Failed. Ada tried again, switching channels, bypassing another layer of DSO encryption, and forced the system to reroute through the closest available relay. Answer me. Ada's jaw tightened. "Leon." She tried again. Her eyes flicked back to the biometric feed, watching his oxygen level drop again.

She immediately opened a second connection, this time routing directly into the DSO emergency channel. She didn't care anymore if someone noticed the intrusion. She could deal with questions later. Right now, she needed to get to him.

She swallowed, forcing herself to breathe before speaking. "Hang in there, Leon." Her voice was steady, but only barely. "Help is on the way."

Ada pushed herself away from the table, taking several steps across the rooftop, putting distance between herself and the laptop before reaching into her pocket for her phone. There was a reason she didn't use it for things like this. A call created a record, a record created a trail. And Ada had spent years making sure nobody could follow hers.

Her thumb hovered over the contact. Sherry.

She hesitated knowing she was creating a trail, she’d have to get rid of this phone soon after. It didn’t matter now. The phone began ringing, until it connected. Ada lifted it to her ear. "Sherry?" Ada's eyes remained on the surveillance feed. "I'm going to need a favor." —

Leon hit the floor hard. The flash from the grenade still burned across his vision, leaving everything washed in white. His ears rang so badly that for several seconds he couldn't tell whether anyone was speaking or whether the sound was only inside his own head.

The second the smoke cleared, a boot struck him across the side of the face: automatically rolling with the impact, shoulder slamming into the floor as his vision finally began to clear. He tasted blood against his tongue, swallowed against the nausea rising in his throat. Maria.

Leon tried to push himself up, but his arm trembled beneath him, even more with her heel now digging into his chest as she pushed him lower.

Dylan's voice carried through the prison block as he remained unfazed. "Don't worry about them. They'll suffer for a while, then turn soon enough." The infection had spread farther since the last time he'd checked. His chest felt too tight, every breath dragging against something that seemed to be filling his lungs from the inside. Leon forced his eyes toward him. Dylan checked his watch almost casually. "As for Jill... She can do whatever she wants. We're at the final stage now."

Leon stared at him, he already knew where this was going. "Once the bio-drones hatch from their Licker hosts, we'll have millions. They'll leave the island and then there's no stopping them."

For a moment, Leon forgot about the pain. Millions. He dragged in another breath as his mind immediately went to Ada. If those things left the island...

Dylan stopped, looking at his watch again. "What?" He said, "No...The signal—” His voice sharpened. "Who could've done this?!" He began moving away, Maria following him.

Leon couldn't see from where he was lying. There was only one person he could think of: Ada. He didn't know if she was actually here, maybe somehow, against every reasonable expectation, she had found a way through.

His breathing became the loudest thing he could hear until he winced when his earpiece crackled. Hunnigan? It was static as he stared at the floor, "Leon." For one impossible second, he thought the infection was finally getting to him as his heart seemed to stumble. "Can you hear me?" Her voice came again.

Leon wondered if this was what dying actually felt like. Her voice was one of the last things his mind had chosen to give him. "Ada…" he said, instinctively reaching for the earpiece.

"I'm here."

Leon let out a breath that almost became a laugh. Of course it was her. He should’ve known it the moment Dylan started panicking about the signal. Somehow, she'd done it.

Ada always found her way to him, even now.

He could almost picture her beside him, brushing away his hair to blow on a bruise that was starting to form. The quiet curve of her mouth when he said something stupid. He remembered her standing in front of him that morning, straightening his tie with those impossibly steady hands.

Be safe. He'd promised her he would.

Leon touched the earpiece with trembling fingers, barely able to believe it. “Ada?"

"I'm here," she repeated, softer this time. "Stay with me… please."

Leon shut his eyes again, a breath escaping him, shaky and almost disbelieving. She was real. He didn't know how. He couldn't make himself care about how. The fact that her voice was in his ear was enough, so close that he could hear the deliberate steadiness she used when she refused to let herself sound afraid. I know you too well. He thought, smiling faintly.

She was scared.

He looked down at his hands. The veins beneath his skin had darkened considerably. His fingers were shaking now, and he could barely feel them. "Ada..." Leon took a breath. "I'm sorry."

"For what?"

"This morning..." Leon swallowed against the tightness in his throat. "I told you I'd be back before dinner." He shook his head, the movement making the room tilt violently.

“You were right." He tried to smile, though not quite. "That's looking a little optimistic right now."

Ada didn't answer immediately, she didn’t need to as he could imagine her expression perfectly. That tiny narrowing of her eyes was the look she gave him whenever he tried to make a joke about something that wasn't funny. "I'm sorry," he whispered again. "I really am."

"Leon."

"I was supposed to come back." His hand closed on his chest, over the paper tucked inside. "I was supposed to make it up to you."

“You will.”

He'd been looking forward to making it up to her. That was the stupid part. He'd been thinking about what he would say when he saw her again. He'd tease her about banning him from the penthouse. He'd apologize for postponing their date yet again, even though he knew she wasn't really angry when she should be. He'd get dinner. He'd see her smile, give kisses until she gave up the frown. Maybe she'd give him that look that said she couldn't believe she'd ever agreed to any of this.

All of it had felt inevitable this morning. Now it felt impossibly far away.

“I don’t..” he wheezed. “…know.” He could hear his own breathing becoming increasingly shallow. "I don't want the last thing you remember about me to be..." He couldn't finish.

"It won't be."

Leon squeezed his eyes shut, a tear slipping down the side of his face. He didn't bother wiping it away, Chris and Claire were too far away to notice. "I'm scared, Ada." His voice cracked. "I'm really scared."

"Listen to me." There was silence on the line. "You're alive," she said quietly.

He almost smiled, breathing in shakily. "Yeah."

"You're going to stay conscious. You're going to keep breathing. You're going to keep talking to me until I get you out of there. Do you understand?”

Leon stared at the ceiling. "Mhm… You sound pretty sure."

"I am.” Her voice, unwavering, as though she couldn't allow herself even a second to consider anything else. “So stay with me… okay?”

He nodded weakly, knowing she couldn’t see him, or maybe she could. “You know…” His eyes barely opened. “Thought you were mad at me.”

“I am.” She said, “Even more than I was before.”

Leon closed his eyes again, a smile tugging his face. “Good to know.” He let out a broken laugh that barely sounded like one.

"Keep breathing.” Ada's voice softened, he could hear the slight shake in her voice, the fear underneath it all. “Help is on the way,” Ada's voice was steadier now, though the strain beneath it hadn't disappeared. "I've made sure of that."

He forced his eyes open. "Yeah?"

“Yes.” She said, “So you need to hold on a little longer."

Leon swallowed. "A little..." He had to stop and breathe. “...longer."

"That's all I'm asking from you." Her voice soft. “Please.”

His heart ached at the plea. "We were supposed to be out on that date right now..." The words came out barely above a whisper. "Didn’t exactly work out…”

“You’re going to see me again,” she said. “And when you do, we’re going to have that date we’ve been waiting for.”

Something in his chest loosened at her words, a small, almost helpless smile pulling at the corner of his mouth. He couldn't stop it. Not even when his eyes burned and the tears he'd been fighting finally slipped free, tracing slowly toward his temples.

He stared at the ceiling, trying to hold onto her voice. "...Promise?"

There was no hesitation. "I promise.”

Chapter End Notes

Chapter 18: “Something that makes me feel like you're actually there.”

Claire's worried voice echoed through the corridor. "You're bleeding bad." She pressed down harder against Taylor's wounds. "I have to stop it."

Leon opened his eyes. He was still lying several feet away from them, separated by the wreckage and distance between the two sides of the corridor.

Taylor's eyes shifted toward him. "What's the point?" he asked weakly. "After what he said, don't you feel like giving up?"

Leon swallowed against the lingering ache in his throat. "He made his choice." His voice carried across the corridor. "We made ours." When he spoke, he had to raise his voice so they could hear him.

Chris looked over at him. "That's all." His voice was quieter, but Leon could hear him clearly. "The world's fucked up. No denying it. Destroying it is easy. The hard part's doing the right thing..." He paused. "Regardless."

Leon looked down for a moment. "Can't save the world..." He had to take a breath before raising his voice again. "By killing it."

Taylor stared at them. "We're all dying." His voice trembled. "How can you be so calm?"

Chris looked toward the direction where Jill had left. "We got Jill."

Leon smiled faintly at Chris’s words. It was small and tired, barely more than a curve at the corner of his mouth, but it was there. Because Chris had Jill.

And he had Ada, She hadn't said anything since promising him he'd see her again, but he knew she was listening. His fingers brushed absently against the earpiece. Leon lowered his voice, just enough that it wouldn't carry across the corridor. "Got you, too."

He smiled again, everything hurt— but he could still picture the look on Ada's face as he heard a faint sound somewhere between a chuckle or a sniffle. “Yeah, yeah,” Ada murmured, almost immediately, as if she could hear the thought forming in his head. “It’s not over yet, Rebecca should be there anytime now.”

Of course she was trying to brush it off. Leon let his head rest back against the wall, breathing as steadily as he could. His chest still burned with every breath, but his attention shifted to a faint sound echoing from the direction where Jill had headed for the armory. Unmistakable footsteps echoed through the corridor, Leon turned his head, his vision still swimming at the edges when he did. “Rebecca,” he breathed.

A moment later, Rebecca appeared around the corner, moving quickly with the briefcase clutched tightly in one hand. “Leon!” she called. “I have the vaccine. I saw Jill out there too.”

Chris immediately looked toward her. “Jill?”

“She’s headed for the armory.” Rebecca dropped beside Leon, already opening the case. “This will sting a bit.” He barely had time to process what she was doing before she pressed the injector into his neck.

Leon hissed sharply, his hand instinctively tightening against the floor.

“Easy,” Rebecca said.

“Yeah, well…” Leon managed, swallowing hard. “I've had worse.”

Rebecca gave him a look that suggested she wasn't interested in hearing about it, moving over to Chris and Claire.

Leon stared ahead, breathing hard, waiting as the vaccine was pushed into his system. Ada remained silent in his ear; he could hear her breathe over the line. “Is it working?” She asked, with a note of impatience that was new to her.

“Yeah,” he said, his voice still rough, breathing dreading slowly as the crushing heat beneath his skin began to recede.

The change was gradual, but Leon could feel it. The pressure in his chest began to ease. His lungs no longer felt like they were fighting him with every breath, and the awful heat beneath his skin slowly faded. He took another breath, deeper this time, and felt something close to relief.

“How do you feel?” Rebecca asked, looking back at him from Claire’s cell.

“Better.” Leon planted one hand against the floor and pushed himself upright despite his legs wobbling slightly, needing to steady himself to take a breath.

“Leon—”

He cut Ada off. “I’m fine.” Still exhausted, but the strength was coming back quickly now that the virus was no longer tearing through him.

Rebecca knew better than to tell Leon to not rush it, she sighed instead administering the vaccine to Chris through the cell.

The earpiece crackled softly. Ada’s voice came through. “You just got injected with an experimental vaccine Leon, can’t you take a minute.”

Leon glanced down at the earpiece, a faint smile pulling at his mouth. “I’m afraid I gotta run,”

The sigh that came through the earpiece after he picked up his pace told him exactly what he assumed. Ada could most definitely see him. “That was not funny, Leon. You almost died.”

He smiled at her words. “Do I hear worry in your tone?”

“No.”

Leon raised an eyebrow. “Ada.”

“I’m simply observing your complete inability to rest.”

He gave a quiet laugh, though it turned into a small cough. “I'm fine.”

“I heard it the first time.” A faint tension under her word she didn’t bother to hide, one which Leon recognized immediately.

He smiled. “You really are worried.”

Ada sighed quietly in his ear. “You’re giving me a headache.” The answer came so evenly that he could practically picture her expression. “I'll deal with you later.”

Leon’s smile widened. “Looking forward to it.”

Jill was heading to the armory. Whatever Dylan had planned, they still had a chance to stop it. He had barely made it a few steps down the corridor when Ada spoke again. “I’m getting another call.”

Leon glanced toward his earpiece. “Another emergency?”

“Sherry.”

That got his attention. “Okay.” He continued toward the armory, footsteps echoing against the concrete floor. “Interesting.” Leon smiled faintly. “Leaving me so soon? I just only recovered”

There was the slightest pause. “You seem to be doing remarkably well without me.”

“I almost died five minutes ago.”

Ada let out a quiet breath that sounded suspiciously like she was trying not to laugh. “I’ll still be connected. I just won't be speaking.”

Leon raised an eyebrow even though she couldn't see him. “So you're going to listen to me get into more trouble?”

“Something like that.” Her voice shifted as another connection opened. “Sherry,” Ada said, and suddenly she sounded farther away.

-

Ada had barely finished telling Leon she would remain on the line when another voice came through her phone. “Finally.”

Ada glanced down at the screen. “Sherry.”

“Yeah. We’ve got a problem with comms.”

Ada straightened slightly. “What kind?”

“We can hear you and Leon perfectly. His frequency is coming through, too. But Hunnigan’s voice isn't making it through to him.”

Ada frowned, turning slightly away from the laptop as Leon’s footsteps continued faintly through the other connection.“Can she hear us?”

“Everything. She just can’t transmit back.” Ada considered that for a moment as Sherry continued. “Tech says maybe twenty minutes. They’re trying to isolate whatever’s interfering with the channel.”

“Then I’ll stay with him until they fix it.”

“That’s what I was going to suggest.”

Ada slipped one hand into her coat pocket, listening to the faint sounds of Leon entering the elevator on the other end of the connection.

Sherry, with unmistakable amusement, said: “He seems pretty determined to keep you on the phone.”

Ada's expression barely changed.“You have no idea how hard it was to get him out of bed this morning.”

“Seriously?” Sherry laughed. “So he's clingy.”

Ada looked down at her laptop, the corner of her mouth lifting despite herself. “Don’t tell him I said that,”

Leon’s voice came through her headset: “I heard you!”

Sherry heard it too. “Clingy.” Ada could hear the smile in Sherry's voice. “Well, I’ll keep you posted.”

“Oh and,” she said simply. “I believe Rebecca is having trouble breaking through the lock system for the cells, Chris and Claire have already had the vaccine administered and it seems to be reacting well,” she said , “in the meantime check if you can do something about that, Jill would need backup soon enough because it seems like things are going to go south soon.

“Why? What can you see?” She asked.

“Seems like Dylan has triggered some large scale water system in the main area, obviously up to no good.”

He pushed the large metal doors and let himself into the observation deck where Dylan had spoken to him and Jill earlier. Leon looked toward the control panels. “Why's he letting all the water in? Huh?” He moved farther into the armory, looking around as he listened to the faint static in his earpiece. Ada hadn't said a word since she'd told him she was staying on the line, but knowing she was there was strangely reassuring.

The sound of heels against the concrete floor cut through his thought. Immediately the thought came instinctively, even though he knew better. She was still on the frequency, somewhere outside the island, and there was no way she could have suddenly appeared inside the armory so soon. Still, his mind had already made the connection.

Leon stopped beside one of the panels, his expression flattening as he slowly turned around. Maria stood by the entrance, looking at him with a cold glare. “I am glad the virus didn’t kill you,” she said. “I wanted to be the one to do it.”

Leon stared at her for a moment, his hand tightening around his weapon. “You don’t always get what you want.”

Maria's expression hardened, and before he knew it, she was driving both feet into a large metal plate mounted beside the equipment rack, swinging loose with a violent metallic shriek. It tore across the room like a giant frisbee, smashing into a bank of technical equipment as he barely got to doge. Sparks burst from the consoles as monitors flickered and went dark. Maria was already coming at him, launching herself forward and swinging for his face.

Leon raised his arm, but the force of the strike caught him off balance. His body twisted with the blow, and before he could recover, Maria seized him from behind—one hand locking around the back of his neck, yanked him backward and slamming him against the railing. His head struck the metal, struggling to wrench himself free, but Maria drove him into it again. Twice now. His vision flashing white with that. “F—”

She twisted his arm behind him and dragged him toward the control panel, slamming him against it hard enough that the buttons and switches dug into the back of his skull. He grunted, bracing both hands against the panel as he fought to keep his balance.

Maria flipped up a metal pip bringing it up sharply between them. Leon saw the movement just in time to jerk his head sideways to miss the aim, the pipe slamming into the panel beside his face.

She pulled it back and struck again, the metal scraping past his cheek close enough for him to feel the wind of it.“Hold still!” She screamed.

“Not really my thing.” Leon said, twisting against the control panel, forcing his shoulder into her arms.

“This is for my father!” She said, pushing against him.

Leon shoved back. “He was Arias's guard dog.” The words came out strained between clenched teeth, muscles straining as he tried to keep her from driving the pipe into him. “You were his bitch.”

Maria's face twisted with rage before she kicked him hard in the abdomen causing him to double over just enough for her to wrench herself free of his grip.

The pipe came around, slamming into the side of his face. Pain exploded across his cheek and jaw, and for a second his legs nearly gave out beneath him.

Maria didn't even give him the chance to recover before she grabbed him by the jacket and drove him backward into the wall. Her forearm locked across his throat, her bicep crushing beneath his jaw as she forced him back against the concrete. He grabbed at her arm with a weak attempt as he tried to create enough space to breathe as she leaned into him with all her weight. “You should've died from the virus.”

Leon struggled for air, fingers tightening around her arm. “Yeah…” He swallowed painfully. “Yet here I am.”

Maria suddenly threw him sideways with apparently enough force to send Leon stumbling into a table. Before he caught himself, she climbed onto his shoulders, and used her weight to force him forward while one hand went immediately for his weapon. Leon twisted and tried to wrench his arm free, but Maria caught his wrist, tore the gun from his grasp, and tossed it aside before he could reach for it again.

He reacted on instinct, immediately curled his fingers into fists and threw his weight sideways, attempting to swing her off his shoulders. Maria stayed locked onto him. Fucking hell! He tried again, twisting harder until his boots scraped against the floor, but she clung to him with brutal determination. “Would you mind letting go?!” Leon grunted.

“Not until you're dead.”

He wheezed trying to swing her off.“You’re really not big on personal space, huh?” Leon grunted and made one final attempt to throw her. This time, the momentum managed to slam Maria into the table with enough force to make its metal frame groan beneath them. He did it goddamn twice and she was still holding on. Leon braced over her as he tried to regain control of the situation. “You know,” he wheezed, “this is getting … a little too close.”

Maria tightened her chokehold.

Leon grimaced when he heard a soft “I can see that,” come from Ada over his earpiece.

Her heel struck him across the face, snapping his head to the side and sending him stumbling backward. He barely managed to stay upright, blinking rapidly as the room swayed around him, before Maria was already coming after him again.

Leon charged to meet her rather than waiting for the next attack, slipping beneath her first strike and caught her leg as she stepped forward, hooking his own foot behind hers. The sudden shift in balance sent Maria stumbling, and Leon shoved her hard enough to knock her backward. She fell, but she recovered almost immediately, catching Leon around the neck as he tried to move past her and dragging him down with her. He kicked her once in the side, then followed with another strike before she could fully recover. She staggered backward, and Leon started toward her, but Maria straightened and drove her foot into his chest with everything she had. Which turned out to be a big mistake because she threw him through three separate glass boards.

The blow sent him flying across the room, each panel shattering around him as his body tore through it. He landed hard on the other side's wall, until he finally dropped onto his knees.

Small pieces of glass glittered across the floor around him, and several cuts had opened along his face, arms, and hands. Blood trickled down from his cheek as he planted one palm against the floor and forced himself to stay upright. Across the room, Maria stood among the shattered partitions, standing without breaking a sweat. “I will make you pay for killing my father.”

Leon stayed on one knee for another second, drawing a slow breath through his nose before wiping the blood from the corner of his mouth. “Yeah, well…” He took another breath and pushed himself upright. “Your dad was kind of a dick.”

She charged before he had even finished speaking.

Leon raised his forearms just in time to block the first strike, “You're really committed to this.” Maria drove both hands against his forearm, while Leon planted his boots and pushed against her with equal force.

“Behind her.” Ada said quietly in his ear. “The pipe.”

Through the corner of his vision, he caught sight of it: a jagged length of broken metal protruding from the wreckage behind Maria, angled upward just enough to do serious damage.

“Stop trying to overpower her.”Ada's voice came through again. “Use her momentum.”

Leon understood immediately.

Instead of continuing to push against Maria, he deliberately loosened his arms and allowed her to force him backward. She took the opening, driving forward with more of her weight behind the attack as Leon gave ground one step at a time, keeping his eyes on her until he was almost directly in front of the broken pipe. At the last possible moment, he shifted his weight to the side and let Maria's momentum carry her past him. He caught her by the arm and shoulder as she stumbled forward, using her forward motion to redirect her with everything he had left in him.

Maria flew backwards, slamming into the jagged metal behind her with a sickening crack. Her body going rigid as the force resulted in the jagged end protruding from the front of her chest. Blood trickling down into her suit before she even processed it. She looked down, her hands slowly moved toward the pipe as she hesitantly touched the blood spreading across her chest and stared at it in disbelief.

Leon remained where he was, chest rising and falling rapidly. He stared back as she slid herself off the pipe and looked at the gaping hole in her chest. Staggering toward him with a step, and a glare that told him she refused to lose. For one horrifying second he prepared himself for another round of fight.

Instead Maria fell to the ground as stiff as a doll, blood pooling under her as her eyes remained open with that glare directed at him.

He looked at her for a long moment. “Now you can be with your dad again.”

Ada's voice came through his earpiece after a moment. “That was a little harsh.”

Leon looked back at Maria. “I thought she'd appreciate it.” He exhaled.

Ada was silent for a second, pretending to consider it. I'm sure she'll treasure the sentiment.”

Leon almost smiled. “You're judging me.”

Ada sighed. “Come on, Leon.”

He glanced toward the doorway. “Yeah.”

“Your friends are waiting.” Leon started walking. Then Ada added, almost casually: “And try not to get yourself into another fight before you make it to them.”

Leon smiled faintly. “Wasn't planning on it.” He started toward the exit, stepping carefully around the broken glass.

Ada spoke again, her tone shifting slightly. “I’m going offline.”

Leon stopped. “Now?”

“I have something I need to take care of, and Hunnigan should be back on your frequency shortly.” There was a small pause before Ada added, “Chris, Claire, and Rebecca are heading there now. Better not keep them waiting.”

Leon looked down at himself—bloodied, bruised, covered in cuts, and still trying to convince his legs that they hadn't been through enough for one day. “You’re starting to sound like Hunnigan,” he frowned.

Ada gave a quiet hum through the earpiece. “Is that a complaint?”

“Maybe.”

She seemed to understand what he meant, but she didn't comment on it. “I think Hunnigan sounds great.”

”cmon Ada,” He looked toward the doorway again, then back at the ruined room around him, reluctant to take another step as his heart caught up.“Say something before you go,”

“Something.”

Leon let out a tired laugh. “That's not what I meant.”

“I know.”

He rubbed a hand over his face, wincing when his fingers brushed one of the cuts along his cheek. “I just…” He hesitated, suddenly unsure how to explain it without making it sound like more than it was. “Give me something,” he said finally. “One of those things you say.”

Ada's voice softened with amusement. “You're going to have to be more specific.”

“You know…” he trailed off, very well knowing he shouldn’t waste time. “Something that makes me feel like you're actually there.”

A longer pause followed.

Then Ada said, perfectly calm, “Leon, you're standing in the middle of a destroyed prison surrounded by bio weapons, and you're asking me for reassurance.”

Leon smiled. “Is that a problem?”

“As long as you don’t make it a habit.”

“Noted.” His smile grew because that meant Ada was giving in.

She relented and said: “I haven’t finished being annoyed with you yet so come back in one piece. You're letting me patch you up. No arguments.”

He laughed, despite his ribs aching. “That’s better.”

Ada sighed quietly. “You really are clingy.”

“I'm injured. I think I deserve it.”

“That isn't a defense.”

“It should be.”

“Don’t forget, you still owe me a date. You’re not getting out of it that easily.” She gave another quiet sigh, but Leon could hear the smile in her voice when she finally said, “Go.”

Leon ducked his head, trying—and failing—to hide how pleased he was. “Mhm…” He looked toward the doorway. “Okay, I’m going.”

“See you later, Good luck.” she said, the line going quiet.

Leon grinned. “Figured.”

Ada remained seated beside the laptop, one hand resting against the edge of the table as the last traces of Leon's voice seemed to linger in the headpiece anyway.

She closed the laptop, then began dismantling the equipment she'd used to take out the mutated shark. The sniper rifle came apart first, each piece handled with practiced efficiency before being secured inside its case. She gathered the ammunition, emptied the spent magazine, and checked the chamber before slipping everything into the hard-shell case she'd brought with her.

By the time she was finished, the rooftop looked almost exactly as it had before she'd arrived. Nothing that suggested she'd spent the afternoon intercepting communications, breaking into Alcatraz's security system, and shooting a genetically modified shark from several hundred yards away.

Ada picked up the laptop case and looked toward the city. She still needed to get to the island. That was the problem she'd asked Sherry to solve.

She had never expected much from the request. A boat, perhaps. A contact who could get her close enough to slip onto the island unnoticed. Maybe a DSO transport if Sherry could find one that wouldn't raise too many questions. It didn’t matter: just anything that got her there.

Ada wasn’t planning on interfering with whatever Jill was planning, they were all more than capable of handling themselves. But there was the urgent need in her that needed to see Leon through the lens of her own two eyes rather than choppy camera footage with weird angles.

The elevator took her down several floors before opening onto the hotel's quiet hallway. She walked toward her room, passing two guests who barely glanced in her direction. By the time she reached her door, the familiar composure had settled back over her features.

Her phone vibrated as soon as she stepped inside : Sherry.

She answered immediately. "Sherry."

"Hello." There was something in Sherry's tone that made Ada stop what she was doing.

"What did you find?"

"A way to get you onto Alcatraz."

Ada leaned against the edge of the bed. "Mh-hm, let’s hear it.”

"I've arranged a helicopter."

Ada's eyebrows lifted slightly. "A helicopter?”

"DSO."

Ada stared at the phone. "You've arranged a DSO helicopter for me."

"Not exactly for you." That sounded more like Sherry.

Ada crossed her arms. "Go on."

"It's being dispatched for a classified recovery operation. The passenger manifest doesn't list a name. The crew has been instructed to retrieve an authorized asset at a designated location."

Ada's expression sharpened. "And the authorized asset?"

"Isn't identified."

"Sherry."

"I'm serious. There's no Ada Wong anywhere in the paperwork."

Ada looked toward the window. "How did you manage that?"

"I have access to DSO resources."

"That doesn't answer the question."

"No, it doesn't." Ada could hear the smile in Sherry's voice."Besides, you saved my life in China."

Ada was quiet. "That doesn’t—"

"Back in China," Sherry continued. "You didn't have to do that for me and Jake… especially considering your history with Wesker.”

The word, history and Wesker was enough to take her back to the memory buried deep in the chaos of Lanshiang. "You didn’t have to do that," Ada said.

"Still." There was a brief silence between them. "So," Sherry said, her voice lightening again, "consider this me returning the favor."

“You know what?” Ada allowed herself the faintest smile. "You're becoming reckless."

"I have no idea what you’re talking about."

That earned a quiet breath of amusement from Ada. She shook her head. "How long do you think it’ll take for them to handle the mess?"

"To be honest? Sunrise."

Ada glanced at the clock. "That's not much time."

"Perhaps." Sherry gave her the pickup coordinates, followed by a temporary authentication code.

Ada memorized both. "Anything else?"

"The pilot has been told to retrieve an authorized DSO asset. You give him the code, he verifies it, and you get on the helicopter."

"Well." Ada picked up Leon's jacket she picked up from his apartment. "that's certainly one way to solve the problem."

Chapter End Notes

Chapter 19: “Thank you for taking care of me, Ada. ”

— After the fact, SUN RISE.

The Death Island had finally gone quiet. The sun was beginning to rise beyond the horizon, casting a pale gold line across the water. Leon looked around at the ruined facility, letting out a tired breath as he leaned more properly against the stone with folded arms.“Well, I know what I’m gonna take away from this experience.”

Rebecca glanced over at him. “What’s that?”

“Prison tours suck.”

“So do giant shark monsters.” Claire added.

Chris folded his arms. “So did this op.”

Jill gave a tired laugh. “Yeah.” She turned to Chris, giving him a smile. “But we sure did kick some ass, didn’t we?”

Rebecca nodded. “We sure did.”

“All of us.” Leon watched the way Chris said it, the way his gaze met Jill’s. There was something in his expression that made him look at Jill, too.

She smiled back at Chris, tired but genuine and Leon couldn't help the small smile tugging at his mouth. After everything they'd been through, it was nice to see them like this.

Chris had gotten her back. Good for him.

Still, he couldn't help thinking about what it must have been like for Chris when Jill was taken by Wesker. He knew what Wesker was capable of. He knew what he'd wanted from Ada, what he'd expected from her, and how little regard he'd ever had for anyone who stood between him and whatever objective he'd decided mattered.

Death was final. Cruel, but final. But someone disappearing from your life when you knew they were still somewhere out there and you couldn’t reach?

That was something he understood a little too well. And today had reminded him exactly how fragile that was. A few seconds of exposure to something that could have taken away everything he was.

If the vaccine hadn't reached him in time, he might have survived without really being him anymore.

Everyone looked toward the sky when the distant sound of rotor blades reached them. Three helicopters appeared over the water, flying toward the island from the same direction. “There they are,” Rebecca said.

There was only one landing area, so they had no choice but to wait their turn. They started walking together toward the pad as the first aircraft came down, the growing roar of the rotors drowning out everything else.

The first helicopter began descending toward the helipad, boarding Chris and Jill first. Once their helicopter lifted, the second B.S.S.A helicopter landed. Claire and Rebecca followed, exchanging a quick glance with Leon before climbing inside.

By the time it was his turn, the first two choppers were already far away. Leon touched his earpiece as static crackled faintly through it. “Agent Kennedy.” The transmission was rough, distorted by the helicopter's engines and the interference still lingering over the island. “DSO extraction has arrived. You coming?”

He stared toward the approaching aircraft. Something about that voice. The transmission was too brief, too distorted, and the helicopter was already descending before he could make sense of it.

“Yeah, I hope so.” The helicopter settled onto the helipad, its rotors continuing to thunder overhead as dust and loose debris swept across the concrete as he waited for the side door to open.

It slid back. Finally. Leon climbed aboard, ducking his head beneath the frame and stepped inside, gripping one of the overhead rails as he sighed. “Thanks,” He turned toward the pilot. “Where are we—” He stopped because someone was already farther back in the cabin.

Dressed in black tactical gear was Ada. And for some reason, his exhausted brain simply refused to make the connection. His eyes immediately burned, and before she could say another word, Leon crossed the cabin and pulled her into his arms. She barely had time to catch herself before his arms were around her, his face pressed against her shoulder as he held her tightly. Her arms came around him in return, one hand settled between his shoulder blades while the other rested against the back of his neck. “Leon..”

Leon closed his eyes, tucking his head in the warmth of her neck. She was warm, and very much the real deal. He had spent hours wondering whether he'd ever see her again, and now she was in his arms, the contrast left him with a feel of overwhelming relief he didn’t know where to direct. “You're here.” He let out a shaky breath that sounded more close to a laugh.

“I am.” Ada tilted her head slightly, her eyes were glistening. “Missed me?” His voice caught as Ada's hand moved to the back of his head, her fingers sliding gently through his hair.

“Yeah.” Leon squeezed her tighter, he'd thought he was dying on that prison floor, wondered whether he'd ever see her again, now he could finally allow himself to stop holding everything together and just kiss her. “I missed you,” he admitted quietly.

Her hand settled against his cheek as she kissed him back. She reached up and brushed her thumb beneath one of his eyes, wiping away the tear before it could fall. “I promised you I'd see you again.”

Leon smiled. “You did, and you took your sweet time.”

She shrugged. “You were the one who was busy, Leon.”

He gave her an incredulous look. “You're really going to give me a hard time about that right now?”

“When else would I?” Ada's mouth curved in the question. “I’m still mad at you.”

“I’d hope you are,” he murmured before he leaned in to kiss her again. “I was looking forward to making it up to you,” when he pulled back he had to look at her for another second, still slightly stunned that she was actually there, standing in a DSO issued helicopter. What are the odds? “How did you even get the helicopter?”

“Wouldn’t you like to know?” She smirked. “I have my ways.” She pat his chest lightly. “let’s sit down and get buckled up before you fall,”

Leon gave her a tired grin. “Yes, ma'am.” He laughed and finally lowered himself into the seat beside her, his shoulder pressing against hers.

Her arm curled around his bicep looking up at him with worry. “You're all right, yes?”

Leon laughed quietly, wiping at his face. “Yeah, I'm okay.”

Her eyes moved over the cuts across his face, the blood on his clothes, the bruising around his neck. “Don't scare me like that again.”

He looked at her for a moment, and nodded honestly. “I'll try.”

“Try harder.”

A small smile pulled at his mouth. “Yeah. Okay.”

Ada’s expression was tight in seriousness. “I mean it, Leon.”

“I know.” He said, leaning into her a bit as the helicopter lifted. "I knew you'd find me."

"I always do." She sighed.

“Of course.”

Her eyes moved to the handwriting. Don't make me wait, handsome. “You're late.”

Leon looked at her. “I had a few complications.” He folded the note and slipped it back into his pocket as the chopper descended lower. “I'll see you at the hotel.”

She'd be waiting and he intended to make it.

“Yes, Leon. I sniped it.”

He stared at her for a second, his hand settling gently against her waist. “You saved her life,” he said softly.

Ada's smile faded. “The SWAT team didn't make it.”

Leon was quiet. “But because of you, the vaccine reached us.”

Besides the kiss he pulled her into, of course. It was careful enough that she could feel the effort he was making not to jostle his ribs. Which made her smile against his lips.

When they pulled away, leon make it his duty to catch the other tear that was nestled in her lashed. “This is your idea of taking care of me?”

He smiled, “I think it's working.”

Ada let out a quiet laugh, shaking her head. “You nearly get yourself killed, then you come back and make me cry about it.”

He smiled, his thumb brushing once along her waist. “I'll try to do better.” he whispered, kissing her once again. She knew better than to believe that completely, neither of them had particularly safe professions. Ada leaned into his touch. “I know.” She simply closed her eyes and let herself have it—the warmth of him, the steady heartbeat beneath her hand, the arm around her waist. When the kiss ended, Ada stayed close for another moment, her forehead resting lightly against his. His eyes were still glassy, so were hers. “You should get some sleep.”

Leon nodded. “Yeah.” The fact that he agreed so easily told her exactly how exhausted he was. “Probably.” glancing at the morning light.

She helped him settle back against the pillows, one hand supporting his shoulder while he carefully lowered himself onto the mattress. She adjusted the pillows behind him, making sure he wasn't putting too much pressure on his ribs before pulling the blankets over him. “Comfortable?”

“Getting there.”

She adjusted the blanket another inch. “Better?” She reached for the lamp. Leon’s eyes followed her when she stepped away from the bed, walking towards the other side of the room, reaching for the first-aid supplies she'd left on the table.

Behind her, the mattress shifted slightly. From the corner of her eye she saw him glance between the empty space beside him and her. “You're not sleeping?” Something between confusion and disappointment crossed his face before he tried to hide it. “I thought you were staying.”

“I am,” Ada almost smiled. “This is my hotel room, you’re the one who’s staying over for free.”

He laughed, then winced as he realized why that was a bad idea. “Then why aren't you getting in bed?”

“I don't need to sleep.” she said putting away the cotton bandages.

“You haven't slept either.” he said quietly.

She moved across the room, picking up the antiseptic bottle form the nightstand. “I was going to sit here.” she gestured vaguely to the chair, looking at him for a moment. “To make sure you don't decide you're perfectly capable of getting up and doing something stupid while I'm not looking.”

A tired smile tugged at his mouth. “I wouldn't.” Ada raised an eyebrow, before she could turn away again, his hand reached out and caught her wrist gently. “Ada.” She looked down at his hand. His thumb moved slowly over the inside of her wrist, right where her pulse was, almost like he needed the reminder that she was there. “A few hours ago,” he said softly, “I wasn't sure I was going to see you again.”

Ada looked at his hand again. “You want me to sleep with you.”

“I do.” The honesty of it caught her slightly off guard.

She shook her head. “You're getting very demanding.”

“What can I say? I’m injured.” he said innocently, pulling her wrist gently towards him, “come on Ada...”

“You’re gonna run out of that excuse one day,”

He smiled. “Yeah but that day is not today.”

— next morning

There was something quietly satisfying about waking up and finding her still there. At some point during the night, her fingers had slipped between his. Leon looked down at them as a tiny smile tugged at his mouth. Ada was asleep beside him, curled slightly toward him beneath the covers, hair fallen loose around her face, and one arm resting across his chest.

It was like it was like nothing ever happened, except it did, and he could feel it.

He shifted carefully, trying not to wake her. But Ada being the light sleeper she was, she stirred anyway. Her eyes barely opened.“You okay?” Her voice was low and soft with sleep.

Leon looked at her with a stupid smile on his face, forgetting about his ribs entirely. “Yeah.” He liked her like this. There was something about seeing Ada without all her usual composure that made him want to keep her exactly where she was.

Her eyes opened a little further. “Your ribs?” She yawned.

Oh.

Taking in the rare sight of Ada Wong being too sleepy to open her eyes properly, he nodded. “They’re fine.” He said. “I was actually wondering, when are we postponing that date to?” Ada frowned, scrunching her nose as she pressed her cheek into the pillow again. “Leon.. It’s too early for this,”

“But it’s 1pm,” he added. “And I think I’m recovering nicely,” he said looking down at himself. “I was thinking maybe we could reschedule.”

Leon watched her retreat several inches before giving him a sleepy, unimpressed glare. “Go back to sleep.”

Chapter 20: “Trip down memory lane”

Chapter Summary

He’d been here before, coming over after the incident that dragged him from Colorado straight to New York, and apparently her doorstep. The same night he had asked her for a date, and now here he was on the date itself.

Chapter Notes

were officially at the end!!! the title of this fic 'can Leon and Ada be together?' was a working title, and now that the fic is finished, it will be changed to 'Despite the Odds' Thank you so much to everyone that was here during this journey of writing this fic, whether you stayed up to date with every chapter release, or youre binge reading it in one go, Hope you had the most fun!!! <333 theres a bonus scene at the end!

The bell rang when she was still getting ready. Her eyes were finished, at least. The rest of her makeup sat untouched on the vanity, and she was still wearing the silk robe she'd thrown on while deciding what she wanted to wear for the evening. He’s early. That alone made her smile as she walked downstairs. When she opened the door, Ada found him standing there with flowers tucked in one arm all dressed up.

A crisp shirt, dark jacket, clean trousers and hair that had actually been made out of his face to frame his icy blue eyes, although a few strands had already fallen loose over his forehead. There were still faint traces of healing along his jaw and near his temple, but nothing that couldn't be hidden beneath careful lighting. “Good evening,”

“Good evening,” she said, fighting a smile.

"You've got that look." Leon noticed. "The one where you're about to make fun of me."

"I wouldn't dream of it."

He looked down at the flowers. "They're for you."

She took them from him, glancing down at the arrangement. They were simple. Elegant white lilies he knew she loved. "Thank you."

Leon gave her a small smile. "You're welcome."

Ada realized he was still standing outside, awkwardly shuffling his stance. "Are you coming in?"

Leon blinked. "Right—" He shook his head, still smiling as he stepped inside.

She closed the door behind him. “You know you basically live here?”

Leon looked around her penthouse. He'd been here countless times. He'd slept here. Woken up here. Sat at her kitchen counter at two in the morning while she worked. He'd seen her dressed for missions, dressed for nothing, annoyed, tired, amused, furious. Tonight, he was here for a date. Not an accidental evening that somehow turned into staying the night. Though there was not much accident about those evenings. He watched her arrange the flowers in a vase by the door and turned just in time to catch him staring. "You haven't even gotten dressed."

She shrugged at his observation. "I wasn't expecting you this early."

Leon hesitated. He really was nervous, wasn't he? Cute. "I figured I could wait while you got ready."

Ada raised an eyebrow. "Wait?"

"Yeah. Unless you want me to wait in the hallway."

She smirked. "I might."

He laughed quietly when she gave him the same look she'd given him at the door. "You're looking nervous."

"I'm not nervous."

“Mm-hm.”

His smile widened. “That's high praise. I'll take what I can get.”

Leon stood there for another second, smiling to himself. Standing in Ada Wong's penthouse while she got ready for their first date was the happiest he'd felt waiting in a very long time. You're not coming over until we have our date. And Leon, despite being perfectly capable of ignoring orders from almost every government agency on the planet, had somehow found himself respecting that one. Jesus.

— At the restaurant

He didn't need to guide her—she knew perfectly well where she was going—but neither of them seemed particularly interested in changing the arrangement.

Warm evening air met them. The sun had already disappeared below the horizon, but the sky hadn't surrendered its color yet. Deep reds and muted oranges stretched across the horizon, fading gradually into dusky violet above Manhattan glittering beneath it.

Buildings rose around them in every direction, their windows beginning to glow against the fading sky. Traffic moved in distant streams below, tiny and almost silent from this height. Leon came up beside her, his hand still resting against her back. “Worth the wait?” he asked.

She glanced at him. “Yes.”

“I was hoping you'd say that.”

His hand slipped slightly lower along her back, settling comfortably at her waist.

Leon looked at her. “Extremely.” His hand rested lightly against her upper arm, thumb moving absentmindedly against the fabric of her dress.

“Good.” She left her hand exactly where it was. “Then you shouldn't have anything to worry about.”

His eyes narrowed with amusement as he leaned closer to whisper. “You're testing that theory.”

“Perhaps.”

Leon laughed under his breath, covering her hand with his, keeping it against his thigh, smiling, but just not quite as composed as he had been a moment ago. She decided she liked that very much. When the food arrived, they simply ate. The conversation had already settled into an easy rhythm when Leon leaned back slightly, his arm still resting around Ada's shoulders. “It’s nice..” he trailed off, turning his glass slowly between his fingers. “It’s so nice to have all the time in the world with you.” He said. “I don't have to make work with the brief meetings we used to have.”

Ada was quiet for a moment. “Mh-hm. But we have plenty of time to do that, don’t we?”

“It’s a good thing,” she sipped her wine. “You used to ruin all of my schedule.”

“You love to blame it on me don’t you?” Leon shook his head, kissing her temple.

She nodded. “It’s true.”

“Yeah,” He smiled at that, then looked down at his glass. “Though we still haven’t figured out everything yet.” He said. Ada knew what he meant; being together meant being careful. There were plenty of things they couldn't simply decide over dinner and expect to disappear. Leon still worked for the government, which meant his work rarely stayed uncomplicated for long. Ada, meanwhile, existed in the spaces between jurisdictions and identities. Officially, she was a problem that several governments would have preferred solved rather than accommodated.

It meant Leon couldn't simply put her name on a form or introduce her to everyone in his life. Still, there were practical matters to settle. Security. Communication. Missions. What they told people. What they didn't. And perhaps most importantly, what happened when their work put them on opposite sides of something. Neither of them was naive enough to pretend those questions didn't exist. Leon turned the stem of his glass between his fingers. “We'll figure it out.” That was Leon. He wasn't promising that everything would be easy. He simply wasn't frightened by the fact that it wouldn't be. Ada's hand found his again beneath the table. “We'll figure it out,” she echoed. “Speaking of, can I ask you something?”

She turned to him to look at him properly. “You've been asking me things all evening.” She said, smiling faintly. “I'm beginning to suspect this was your plan: get me somewhere with good wine and a view, then interrogate me.”

He smiled. “I prefer to think of it as a trip down memory lane.”

She gave him a look. “I should've known.” she squeezed his hand once. “Ask.”

He hesitated for a moment, deciding how to phrase it. “I've wondered about something for a long time.” he said.“Back in Raccoon City…” His thumb moved slowly over her knuckles. “The kiss…” Leon continued quietly. “Did any of it mean something to you?” She knew immediately what he meant, she felt the memory before she answered—the cable car, the darkness below them, his face close to hers, the strange certainty that everything would go wrong.

For years, she'd wondered whether she'd ever have to answer that question. “Yes.” Apparently, she had. “It did.” There was something almost relieved in his expression, though he didn't interrupt. “But I felt guilty about it.” Leon frowned slightly. “I knew what I was doing. You were afraid. We both knew what was at stake, and I used that moment to push you toward getting the G-virus sample.” She paused the memory coming back to her. “I spent a long time afterward thinking about whether that made the kiss meaningless.” Her voice softened. “I didn’t… if anything, it confirmed something I'd been trying very hard not to acknowledge.”

His expression changed. “You had feelings for me?”

Ada gave him a small look. “Let’s just say Wesker never let me hear the end of it.”

“Wesker?” Leon frowned slightly, the name alone putting him on edge.

“Yeah.. I don’t even know how he knew.” She shook her head slightly. “But he claimed my ‘distractions’ were the exact reason I failed to complete the mission— the only reason why he even gave me a way out was because I managed to secure the tissue fragment from Birkin despite everything.” She sighed. “That sample was my only leverage, my only way out. Wesker wasn’t going to send a rescue chopper for me until I proved I was useful to him.”

“Ada..” Leon’s voice dropped, the harsh reality of what she’d gone through settling in.

“I was in his debt.” she said, her voice dropping to a rare, quiet note of vulnerability. “The one situation I promised to myself to never end up in.” She paused. “So when I kissed you… I meant it.” She looked at him steadily. “But I’ve spent a long time carrying the weight of knowing I used something real to manipulate you.” She looked away, her posture stiffening just a fraction—the closest she would ever get to showing regret. “I shouldn't have done it that way…I’m sorry...”

“Don’t do that.” he shook his head, pulling her closer to him. “… you did what you had to.”

“The sample that fell that night was the only way either of us had a chance of getting out.”

“You were planning to take me?” He asked. “All I remember is you telling me to get out of there.”

Ada's mouth curved faintly. “Yeah. That was until you took that bullet for me.” She continued as though it were the simplest thing in the world. “After that, my plans changed.”

Leon stared at her. “You never told me that.”

“There wasn't exactly an opportunity.” Ada's voice was quiet. “You were bleeding badly. I had very little medical equipment left, so I used what I had to stop you from bleeding out. Which was when I was contacted about the rescue chopper coming to take me. I was told as long as I had the sample they’d give me a ride out of the city.”

His brows pulled together. “And?”

“I was debating to somehow convince them to let you on the chopper too.”

“Seriously?”

She looked at him. “I wasn't going to leave you there, Leon.” She looked away first, staring down at her glass. “Raccoon City wasn't what I'd signed up for,” she admitted quietly. “I thought I understood the work. I didn't. I was inexperienced.” She looked down at her hands, her fingers tracing the edge of the table. “And honestly? Naive.”

He knew exactly what she meant. Annette's gunshot. Leon swallowed. “And here I was hating you for letting me believe you were dead.”

She gave him a small, almost apologetic smile. “I think it’s fair.” She continued. “You should've listened to me when I was telling you to get out of there, you would've avoided a lot of trouble.”

“If I did that, I wouldn't be here would I?” He held her close. “I would have been dead in Raccoon City if it wasn't for you.”

Ada laughed softly. “You would've walked away with far fewer complications.”

“I would do it again.”

Her smile faded softly at the sheer seriousness that cut through his voice. She looked at him, searching his face. “You nearly died, Leon.”

“So did you.” His hand moved from her shoulder, finding hers. “You saved my life.” He looked out over the city. Somewhere below, people were going about ordinary lives, completely unaware of the history that had brought the two of them to this table. “I never imagined Raccoon city would be the reason we'd actually get here.”

A small smile touched Ada’s lips. “Despite the odds?”
