— Ada’s penthouse, same night much after the date.

Ada smiled at him, the expression small but unmistakable. “Okay. Focus.” She shifted closer, placing both hands lightly against his chest. The gesture was innocent enough, but Leon immediately forgot whatever he had been thinking about.

He looked down at her hands, then back at her face. “I'm focused.”

“Are you?” Ada raised an eyebrow. “You're staring at me.”

“I can multitask.” Leon smiled. “I'm listening.”

She kept her hands where they were, looking directly into his eyes. Which, unfortunately, meant Leon was now intensely aware of how close she was. And he wanted to kiss her. That was the problem. Ada was talking. Something about dessert. Baking. Ingredients they had in the kitchen. “...so we could make something tonight,” Ada was saying.

His eyes stayed on hers. “Mhm.”

Ada paused. “Are you actually listening to me?”

“Yes.” Leon considered lying. “You want to bake something.”

Ada gave him a look. “Brownies,” she clarified, a faint smile pulled at her lips.

He glanced briefly at her mouth before catching himself. “Right. Brownies.” Leon sighed. “You're making this very difficult.”

Ada's smile widened. “Focus, Leon.” She shook her head, but she didn't move her hands. “We're going to bake.”

“Okay.” he smiled sheepishly.

“You're going to help me. And you're going to keep your mind on the dessert.”

Leon nodded very seriously. “Absolutely.”

Ada finally took her hands from his chest and stood. he watched her go toward the kitchen. By the time she reached the oven, he was behind her again, arms loosely wrapped around her waist, his chin near her shoulder. “Seriously?”

“I'm helping.”

“I need to preheat the oven.” She gave him an amused look. “You don't need to hold me.” Ada sighed, though she was clearly amused, and reached for the oven controls. She set the oven to preheat and turned back toward him. “Do you have anything in mind?”

A slow, very pleased smile spread across his face. “Well…” He gave her the most innocent look he could manage. “I have a few ideas.”

Ada narrowed her eyes. “About the brownies?”

“Oh.” He nodded solemnly. “Right. Brownies… we could put chocolate chips in them?”

She laughed under her breath. “obviously.”

Leon paused, still wrapped around her waist. “I have complete confidence in you.”

“You mean you're excited to eat them.” Ada gathered the ingredients from the cabinets and refrigerator, setting everything out across the counter. Flour. Sugar. Butter. Cocoa powder. Eggs. Chocolate chips because that was Leon’s input. Ada gave his cheek one last gentle pat. “Come on. Brownies.”

Leon looked at the ingredients, then at her. “So what do I do?”

She handed him the bowl. “Start with the butter.”

He took it carefully. “Okay.”

Ada watched him for a moment. “You've never baked before?”

“I've cooked.”

“That's not what I asked.” Ada smiled and handed him the measuring cup. “Then you're learning.”

Leon nodded with seriousness. “Okay. Teach me.” She showed him how to measure the flour properly, explaining why he shouldn't pack it down. He listened, surprisingly attentive, before carefully leveling the top with the back of a knife. “Like that?” He looked pleased. “I'm good at this.”

“All you’ve done is measured flour.” Ada shook her head, amused, moving on to the cocoa powder. “One cup.”

He measured it out carefully, glancing at her when he mixed the cocoa power into the flour, then the sugar. When it came time to crack the eggs, he tapped the first one against the side of the bowl, hard enough that a piece of shell fell in. “Wanted to be part of the mix, huh?” he said, fishing the shell out. “Second attempt.” This time, he cracked it cleanly, looking at Ada triumphantly right after. “See? No casualties this time.”

She gave him a small nod. “Better.”

She let out a soft, humored huff, a tiny smirk tugging at the corner of her lips as she set the bowl down on the counter. “Don't get ahead of yourself, Leon,” she replied. “We haven’t even gotten to the baking part yet.”

"Please. I'm a professional. They'll be perfect." he mixed in the sugar, the batter slowly coming together. “Is it time to add the chocolate?”

Ada handed him the packet. “A reasonable amount.”

He took it and immediately dumped in a generous, unmeasured handful, folding them into the mix. He scooped a bit onto the edge of the spoon and held it out to her. “Want a taste test?”

Ada raised an eyebrow, looking from the spoon to him. “It's raw batter.”

“I know.”

“And you're still offering?”

Leon let out a soft huff of a laugh, giving her a look. “What can I say? I’m a generous man.”

She hummed thoughtfully, leaning back against the counter as she evaluated it. “Not bad for an amateur,” she said. “But it needs another pinch of salt to balance the cocoa.”

Ada couldn't help the quiet laugh that escaped her. “It enhances the flavor, Leon. Try to keep up.”

"I guess I’ll just have to keep working for your approval." He shook his head, reaching for the salt shaker with a look of pure skepticism. “A pinch, yeah?”

He smiled. “Good company.”

Ada smiled. “Only four minutes gone.”

Leon nodded. “Plenty of time.”

Ada's expression softened.

Leon let out a quiet laugh. “I just got you to say you love me.”

Ada raised an eyebrow. “I believe you said it first.”

He grinned like an idiot. “Still counts.”

“It does.” Being loved by Ada Wong was much more distracting than he'd anticipated. Because when Ada kissed him back, and he immediately forgot about the timer again. Her fingers slipped into his hair, gently combing through the strands while her other hand settled against his back. She smiled. “That's what we're calling it?” She ran her fingers through his hair again. “Twenty minutes left on that timer, Leon. Think your knees can hold out that long, or do I need to let you sit down?”
